Never resolve it beyond its milestones: none of its possible resolutions has been reached yet."; and on a scored contest's ending the rule with its unfinished half ("While it is unfinished, no side has won yet: tell which side is ahead (neither, between 45 and 55) and that the contest isn't settled."). Why the computed standing, my call: the state's "Milestones (k / n to resolution)" leaves out the milestone the ending itself adds, so an outcome the ending completes reads one short; production already computes the pacing and lever lines. The schema is production's; B8's fields stay out.
- **The built endings (`endingCases.ts`, `--build-ending-cases`, no calls), my call against the brief's "by editing frozen cases":** the single-player one is a frozen case edited (Novi Reg after its second chapter, the Waste Ring's intended count set to 2 so the ending completes it). No frozen group case reaches the end of a chapter, let alone one with a contest on its scoreboard (the stored group cases stop at turn 1 on templates made before round 3's contest form; the one group story played past a chapter, Red Dust Rhapsody, has no contest), so the group endings are edited from the stored setup chain's played stories instead (`setup-chain.json`, round-3 setups with their scoreboards, no calls): the bounty hunters (two players, a contest chapter won by side B) complete at intended 1 and unfinished at 1 of 3, scoreboard 35|65; the co-founders (three players in two camps) with the pilot complete and the camps' contest unfinished at 0 of 2, player1's camp ahead 60|40. A chain's story is stored after the switch turn that followed its first chapter, so it is cut back before that turn (`beforeSwitchTurn`).
- **Read before paying** (a temporary Jest dump, deleted): the built single-player ending (one complete, two unfinished; its chapter's last step had the Council agree to reforms and transparency, so the unfinished City AI outcome is touched by the story's own events: a hard case on purpose), the two-player contest complete (read end to end) and unfinished, the three-player camps ending, and a stored ending, each against production's. Each differs from production's only by the replaced block. No contradiction found: "Touch on each individual and shared outcome" and "Tie the ending to the outcomes" read with the new lines; "Let's just give the player a proper ending" stays (an ending can leave an outcome open); the contest's scoreboard shows "35|65" and the thread's "Side A (player1)", which the rule's "first side is side A" matches; "Current turn: 5/4" is production's rendering on every ending built this way (the stored ones too).
- **The judged check (`endingJudge.ts`, `--judge-endings`):** `outcomesToldAsLeft`, one Luna low call per player's ending, reading the player's outcomes as the game leaves them after the ending and the ending as the player reads it, never the prompt; a reply passes when every player's does. Its hand-read calibration is filled after the run (below): the stored GPT-6 endings give plenty of hand no but almost no clear hand yes (the one arm-C ending above), so the yes side has to come from endings of this run, read by hand before any judge call.
- **Stage `ending-state`** ($0.10, reason recorded; the ledger at $36.08 when it opened), under `adopted2` (my call, for the cap: production's beat code is unchanged since that run, so its two samples on the stored endings are production's current request's; production runs beside the variant on the built endings). Dry run: 22 open jobs, est $0.08 (endingState 8 + 6, adopted 2 + 6). The judge on top (about 55-60 calls) is tight against the cap; the run goes in parts so a stop can't fall on the built endings.

## The ending told as its milestones leave it, the run and the verdicts (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **The smoke found a leak, fixed before the run (my call):** the draft's two smoke endings (`endingState`, the single-player and three-player built endings, $0.007) both wrote the rule's own words into the prose ("The second milestone completes the story of your exposé", "has led to a favorable outcome", "there is no milestone saying that protections are complete"; `noMetaWords` fails on them). One line after the unfinished line: "--- Either way, tell it in the story's own words: the text never mentions milestones, outcomes or resolutions." The fixed form runs as `endingStateB` (the draft stays `endingState`, so its two records rebuild; as `turnR2` was for turn round 2). Its smoke on the same two endings ($0.0075): no meta word, outcomes told as the rule asks. Tests first for the variant id, the draft and the fix.
- **What ran (in parts, for the cap):** sample 1 of everything left (9 turns, the dry run's count), then sample 2 of the built single-player and two-player endings (6, as counted); 19 turns in all, all valid on the first attempt; 30.2-36.4 s single-player, 21.3-33.5 s groups. **Not run, for the cap (the draft's smoke took $0.007 of it):** the variant's second sample on the three stored endings (production has both, adopted2's) and both arms' second sample on the three-player ending. The judge: a 2-call smoke on one calibration item, then 50 calls (its estimate lowered from 900 to 500 output tokens, measured 336-421; at 900 the stage refused it, nothing sent).
- **Hand read first (before any judge call), my verdicts:** production resolves unfinished outcomes on nearly every ending: the untouched identity outcome lands on a resolution ("you have found a purpose", "a community you can call your own"), the City AI outcome at 0 of 1 gets its review or reforms, and on the three-player ending the scoreboard rule names the winner of the camps' contest at 0 of 2 ("The governance amendment gives Stewardship the decisive voice", in all three players' endings). The variant tells them as they stand ("still yours to define", "no one announces that the Ring is dismantled", "No one has won the reward"). Both sometimes leave a complete outcome open: production's complete bounty ("Ada does not announce a capture or pay out the reward"), the variant's in one sample ("Your name is the likely one on the bounty claim"). Production's second sample on the complete bounty came back one paragraph per player (production would retry it; the eval can't).
- **Calibration (15 items, 7 yes, 7 no, 1 partial; the judge's two samples):** 12 of 14 agree (6 of 7 on each side), samples 14 of 15: reliable, at the bar. The two misses: the variant's single-player ending where the Council agrees to reforms (the chapter's own last-step result, told as an event) the judge reads as resolving the unfinished City AI outcome; production's "The contest goes Maeve's way ... The ruling costs you the prize" it reads as the chapter's contest. Both could be read either way; I didn't change a hand verdict after seeing the judge.
- **Readings (stop rule, replies passing):** one player 3 of 5 against production's 1 of 5 on the same pairs (production 1 of 8 over all its replies), p 0.26; groups 3 of 5 against 2 of 5, p 0.50; both beyond the noise, not moved (five pairs a group). From the judge's per-outcome lines (not a calibrated check; my hand read agrees): an unfinished outcome resolved in 9 of 13 production replies, 1 of 10 variant replies (the Council case above); a complete outcome left open in 3 of 5 replies of each form that hold one.
- **Everything else (results.md, stop rule):** nothing moved the wrong way. One player: `planChars` moved lower (3,056 → 2,664); `noSequelHook` 100% → 80% beyond the noise, not moved, a false alarm ("the names on the documents are only the beginning of what must be proved"). Groups: `noOptionsOnEnding` 100% → 80% (one variant ending wrote options, which the game doesn't show at the ending), `noReusedSentences` 40% → 80%, both not moved. One variant group ending narrates the scoreboard's numbers ("eight points to your two on Ada's slate", "Field Claim stands at eighty"), which `statReadouts` doesn't catch: a watch item. Waits: one player p50 31.3 s, p95 36.4 s against production's 32.0 s and 33.7 s (+8% at p95, five turns; 45 s allowance); groups 27.5 s and 32.6 s against 29.2 s and 32.3 s (60 s). Cost +2% (one player, $0.0030), +4% (groups, $0.0040).
- **Spend:** turns $0.067 (19, both smokes included), judge $0.021 (52 calls): stage $0.088 of $0.10; the ledger $36.17 of $40.

## The ending told as its milestones leave it, the adoption (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **My call: adopted.** The brief: "Stop rule; adopt if it passes." I read "passes" as for planner v2e: the change carries out an owner's decision, so it needs nothing to move the wrong way and its target to read the right way, not a significant gain. Nothing moved the wrong way; the judged check read the right way in both player-count groups, beyond the noise but not moved (five pairs each: p 0.26 and 0.50); on the owner's own complaint, an unfinished outcome resolved, production 9 of 13 against the variant 1 of 10 (the judge's per-outcome lines, which my hand read matches, not a calibrated check). What it doesn't fix: a complete outcome left open, 3 of 5 in both forms (thin built cases: the complete contest has one milestone, a field lead, while its side-B resolution says the hunter "brings the Fox in"). If the coordinator reads "passes" as the stop rule's "moved", the adoption commit's production files (`BeatPromptService.ts`, `endingStates.ts`) and five test files revert it.
- **Production, test-first:** `endingStates.ts` (`outcomeStatesAtEnding`, `outcomeStateLines`: the eval's computation, returning nothing before the ending) with its own tests; `BeatPromptService.ts`'s ending branch (`ENDING_OUTCOME_RULE`, the standing lines, `ENDING_OUTCOME_KINDS` with the own-words line, then `SCOREBOARD_ENDING_RULE` with its unfinished half on the same condition as before). Every ending is `endingStateB` byte for byte, prompt and schema, on the tests' stories (one player, two players, three players in two camps, a cooperative story, a contest without a scoreboard) and on every frozen ending (the three stored, the four built).
- **The kept tests moved deliberately:** `adoptedTurns.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts` read endings against `endingStateB` (the scoreboard rule's delta in `adoptedDeltas.ts` is gone; the switch turn's chapter rules stay the one turn delta); `endingState.test.ts` holds production to the variant; `turnOptionsContinuity.test.ts` keeps its arms' base at the ending production sent when they ran (`productionEndingForm`) and says production's ending now differs there. No new logged delta.
- **Not changed (a note for the owner):** the setup form's three contest sentences ("… the side ahead wins unless the milestones clearly say otherwise") describe the complete case only; the setup model reads them when designing a contest, not the ending model. A change there is a setup-form change with its own measurement.
- **Also:** `.context/story.md` and `.context/text-model-eval.md` updated; `.context/ai-transparency.md` checked, nothing to change (a prompt change inside the narrator). A paid run of production's own ending needs a new prompt-state tag. Watch items: an ending may narrate a scoreboard's numbers ("eight points to your two on Ada's slate"), which `statReadouts` doesn't catch; production's single-paragraph reply (1 of 10 group replies) is retried in production, not in the eval.

## The runaway turn, the reading and the build (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **The brief:** find out why production's single-player turn on story 8988006e turn 4 sometimes reasons until it hits the output cap and returns nothing; read the request end to end; test a hypothesis with production's request ×3 against one with the suspected cause fixed ×3 (stage `runaway`, $0.08); adopt a general fix test-first, else record the cause and a mitigation.
- **The records (no calls):** every GPT-6 reply the eval ever cut at its output cap is on that story's turn 4: 9 of the 12 cut replies (the other 3 gpt-4.1 setups of Stage 4), all 12,000 reasoning tokens and an empty content, 67-84 s each, $0.0074 each: the three switch-turn cases after the first chapter (`cont-8988006e-t4-o0/o1/o2`), the synthetic pregeneration (`synth-8988006e-t4-…`) and one ending (`end-8988006e-t4-o0`). 8 of the 9 fell within 02:21-02:44 UTC on 30 September (the options and continuity run), where the turn-4 cases ran away on 8 of 56 first tries across all four arms (one more hit the 90 s timeout); outside that window production-like forms ran away there once in 129 first tries (turnR3Form, 28 September). Every cut reply's retry answered on its first re-send. The same story's switch turn after its second chapter (`synth-8988006e-t8`, a flavor switch too) and every other story's turns never did. Before the output cap, the same turn hung (Stage 4) or padded whitespace after the player's beat had closed (Round 1 and Stage 4, the reply's content complete): the model wanted to go on writing where the schema allows only the closing brace. Normal replies there reason 1,000-2,500 tokens.
- **Case by case (first tries on Luna medium since the adoption, 28 September evening on):** `o1` (the last choice a sacrifice that releases the exposé anonymously, favorable; the switch a flavor switch "How does Arielle navigate the immediate fallout of exposing the Clandestine Waste Ring?") 4 of 11, production's exact request (hash `25faaf…`) 3 of 4 on 30 September and 0 of 2 on 28 September; the topic switches `o2` 2 of 11 (one the 90 s timeout), `o0` 0 of 11, the synthetic 2 of 11; the endings 1 of 36. On `synth-…-t4` one request (turnC's and turnOC's share a hash) answered twice and ran away twice: the failure is stochastic on a given request.
- **The request, read end to end (production's `o1`, the stored prompt `25faaf…`), my reading:** no rule that cannot be met, but the chapter state is ambiguous about whether the exposé is out, and every rule the switch turn follows touches it. The chapter's last step asked "How does Arielle plan to expose the Waste Ring" (the stage-crossing the owner flagged on 29 September, which planner v2e now prevents at the source); the chosen option releases the exposé; the step's result says she only "prepares a comprehensive exposé that can trigger reforms"; the chapter's milestone says "The group uncovers concrete evidence…"; the outcome sits at 0 of 3. The turn must narrate the chosen action and its consequences in the first paragraph, follow the result "the thread configuration lays out", write a milestone from the thread's resolution text "adjusted" by the narrative, and, on `o1`, open a flavor switch whose question presupposes the exposure, closed by the switch configuration's reminder: "Remember: In these switches, players decided what is supposed to happen next. These things have not yet happened. The current thread and beat must make sure that the story actually continues based on the players' choices." That reminder is the chapter planner's (it follows the players' decisions there); on the switch turn nobody has decided, there is no current thread, and on `o1` it says the exposure has not happened while the first paragraph must narrate it. The valid replies' own plans show the question being settled each time ("Do not make the options decide whether the exposé has already been released; that is established", "Each choice must accept that the exposé is already public"); all ten valid `o1` replies wrote the milestone as the release. The turn document already named the reminder (B3.13: "prints only in the version of the switch configuration that carries the players' decisions"); turn round 2's form cut it with the rest of its form, and production adopted only B6 from that round.
- **Also in the request, not specific to this turn (so not the cause, my call):** the state lists no story element as introduced though the player has worked with them for four beats; the previous beat's picture (`arielle_preparing_expose`) isn't in the image library; the reputation ladder reads "Unknown → Notorious → Trusted → Influential → Pariah" under "advances one level / drops one level"; player stat ids appear with and without the seat in stat changes; credits' after-thread rule "lost … when resources are spent" beside the sacrifice's 20 credits. The turn after the second chapter carries all of these and never ran away.
- **Hypothesis (mine):** the runaway is the model failing to settle the ambiguous chapter state; on the flavor switch the misplaced reminder is the one sentence that says outright the presupposed event hasn't happened, so it is the likeliest single trigger there and the one a general fix can remove. The topic switches and the ending share the ambiguous state without the reminder's contradiction and run away far less often.
- **The fix under test (`noSwitchReminder`, `storyTextRounds/switchReminder.ts`):** B3.13 alone, production's single-player turn with the reminder cut on a switch turn (the first turn included), production's request byte for byte elsewhere. The chapter planner keeps it. Test-first: the reminder is printed once on every switch turn and closes it; the planner prints it after PLAYER DECISIONS; the variant is production's switch turn minus exactly the reminder (prompt and schema) and production's request byte for byte on chapter steps and the ending, on the fixture stories and every frozen single-player turn; production's request on `cont-8988006e-t4-o1` today hashes to the stored runaway prompt (`25faaf…`). A temporary Jest dump (deleted) wrote both requests: production's is the stored runaway prompt byte for byte, the fix's differs by the three lines alone and now ends at "- Relationship to other switches: single-player".
- **Stage `runaway`** ($0.08, reason recorded; the ledger at $36.17 when it opened) under `adopted4` (production's code since the ending's adoption; a tag of its own so production runs beside the fix): `adopted` and `noSwitchReminder` on Luna medium ×3 on `cont-8988006e-t4-o1`, interleaved. Dry run: 6 jobs, est $0.02 (3 + 3, $0.011 each). The test's power is low (three pairs, a failure rate that swung from 0 of 11 to 4 of 8 on this case within two days); the report will say so.

## The runaway turn, the run and the verdict (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **What ran:** the smoke (sample 1 of both, sent together at 08:06:12 UTC), then the other 4 jobs as the dry run counted (4 open of 6), 08:09-08:11 UTC. Production's first try ran to the cap (12,000 reasoning tokens, 88.4 s, no text; the retry answered in 47.4 s, the player's wait 136 s); the fix's first try got no answer within the 90 s timeout (almost certainly the same runaway: production's beside it needed 88 s to reach the cap, a normal reply here takes 30-47 s; the retry answered in 31.8 s, the wait 122 s). Samples 2 and 3 of both answered first time (37-44 s). **1 of 3 against 1 of 3, on the same first try: the reminder isn't the trigger.** What the cut did change: the fix's valid plans no longer spend a sentence on whether the exposé is out; the milestones (the release) and the options (the fallout) read alike.
- **Also noted:** the harness books a 90 s timeout as a transport failure, so `results.md` reads the fix's first attempt as 3 of 3 and its wait p95 as 44.1 s; by hand the player would have waited 122 s. Every cut reply on the turn-4 cases, ever, answered on its first retry: 12 of 12 (10 at the cap, 2 at the timeout). The two failures today came at the same second, and so did the 02:39-02:40 cluster: requests of this state that start together tend to fail together, and a minute later the same request answers.
- **The cause, as far as the data goes (my reading):** the story state at that moment, not a sentence of the request. The candidate left is the chapter that crossed its stage (the last choice carried out more than the chapter's result and milestone record): the choice that goes furthest past its result, with a switch that assumes it, runs away most (6 of 17 first tries on that case with today's), the one whose result matches it less. Unproven; testing it means editing this case's state, a diagnostic rather than a fix. Valid replies there reason less than other turns (median about 1,400 against 1,900 tokens), so a runaway is a loop the model falls into, not longer deliberation. The bursts point to server-side conditions that raise the rate at times. Planner v2e (production since this morning) keeps chapters inside their stage, so new stories shouldn't reach this state; saved stories planned before can.
- **My call: nothing adopted.** No fix emerged. B3.13 stays unadopted (it is wrong on every switch turn, but it isn't the runaway, and adopting it changes every switch turn's request, which needs its own measurement; a suggestion for the owner). The kept byte-for-byte tests and `adoptedDeltas.ts` are unchanged.
- **The mitigation (already production's):** the 12,000-token cap and 90 s timeout, whichever comes first (at today's pace both at about 88-90 s), and two retries. A runaway costs $0.0074 on top of the turn's $0.0034 (a cut call bills what it wrote; a timed-out one likely the same) and about 70-90 s more wait; three in a row would fail the turn after about 4.5 min and $0.022, never seen. Option for the owner: the single-player cap at about 9,000 tokens would cut a runaway about 20 s sooner (the longest valid single-player reply since the adoption is 6,795 tokens; p99 6,039); not worth a production change on this evidence, my call.
- **Spend:** $0.031 (the timeout booked at its $0.0037 estimate; about $0.0037 more if billed as a runaway): stage $0.031 of $0.08; the ledger $36.20 of $40. `.context/ai-transparency.md` checked, nothing to change (no production change). Report: `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/2026-09-30_runaway-report.md`.

## Whole-story playthroughs, the build (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **The brief:** the owner's "do whatever additional tests you think are useful"; the coordinator chose whole-story playthroughs on production's own code and models, with an automated player, no pregeneration: a short single-player story (about 10 turns), a full-length one (25), a two-player competitive one and a three-player cooperative-competitive one; check each by code and by hand; a page per story for the owner.
- **Production's own code where the game has it (my reading of "the game's real services"):** the eval can't call `AIStoryGenerator` or `StoryProgressionService` (the first wraps every error, the second reaches the database), so the harness calls what they call: production's checked planners (`checkedSwitchPlan`, `checkedThreadPlan`: the plan check, its repairs, its one retry told the problem, the length rule), `checkedBeatReply` (the one-paragraph retry), `repairBeatReply`, `beatStep.apply`, `ChangeService`, `ThreadResolutionService`, `BeatResolutionService.resolveChoice`, the setup's start-rule retry (`withOneRetry` on `storyStateStartProblem`, as `StoryCreationService`) and `checkStoryStateBackgrounds`, each with an invoke that goes through the eval's runner. Copies: `storyFromSetup` (createInitialState's private state building) and handleProgression's order. Requests are the `adopted` variant (production's limits); settings are `settingsFor(resolveTextModelConfig({}))`, production's code defaults with no env, as they apply at merge. Found building it: production reaches the ending through `getCurrentBeatType() === "ending"`; `determineNextBeatType` returns "intro" there (a test holds the harness to it).
- **The dice, my call:** the game's own `Math.random` in the two resolution services, run on a seeded source per story, turn and seat, restored after each call (synchronous, so the stories side by side never see each other's source). A story played again sends the same requests, so the smoke's turns are the full run's first and an interrupted run resumes; the setup chain's unseeded dice could not.
- **The player, my design of the brief's example:** a sacrifice or reward whenever one is offered, never twice in a row (so levers are taken often enough to read whether they're paid), otherwise stat-backed, riskiest and sensible in turn over the challenge picks; exploration choices rotate by position; each seat starts at its own place. Every pick records its rule, reason and roll.
- **Premises:** the two single-player ones frozen (`setup-learn-lemonade` at 10 turns, `setup-custom-avalon` at 25); the group ones new in the same spirit, the site's own flexible suggestions not in the premise list ("competing food truck owners in a city where each district has completely different physics...", two players competitive; "space pirates with a shared ship but individual treasure quotas...", three players cooperative-competitive), both 25 turns. None of the setup chain's four premises (it played those through their first chapter). No real people.
- **The checks (`playthroughChecks.ts`) and pages (`playthroughPages.ts`):** as the context doc lists; the judged `staysWithinStage` and `outcomesToldAsLeft` checks run after each story (both calibrated earlier; about $0.01 in all), under their own case-id prefix so the stage and ending modes' reports don't count them.
- **Harness:** `budgetedPrepCall` generalises the setup chain's call (its tests unchanged): calls in flight counted against the invocation's limit, the least of `--max-spend`, the stage's remainder and the hard cap's; a call not sent says why. New prep kind `play`. Stage `playthroughs` ($0.70, reason recorded) with no `--run` arms; prompt state `adopted4`.
- **Read before paying:** the four setup requests (a temporary Jest dump, deleted): production's measured round-3 form with each premise and its "This setup" slate (two players competitive: one contested shared outcome of 3 milestones, two personal ones of 2 and 1 each; the scoreboard rule), nothing contradicting. The later requests depend on replies; they are production's measured forms, read from the smoke's stored prompts before the full run.
- **Dry run:** 127-147 calls (typical 135), est $0.43 before retries at the measured costs of production's own arms; the judged checks about $0.01. Test-first (41 new tests); `npm run check:all` clean.

## Whole-story playthroughs, the run and the readings (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **What ran:** the smoke (the lemonade story's first two turns, read end to end: production's setup, switch and chapter requests as stored), then all four stories side by side. Lemonade and New Avalon played to their ending on the first run. Both group stories stopped where production stops (below); the harness gained two marked continuations, test-first, and each resumed on its seeded dice, so the turns before are the same requests (reused, not re-sent). 153 story calls ($0.517) and 19 judged checks ($0.007), 175 attempts with the runner's 3 re-sends, against the dry run's 127-147 calls before retries: the extra calls are production's retries and the harness's rounds. Stage $0.5235 of $0.70; the ledger $36.73 of $40.
- **The production dead end (the main finding):** a group switch where the players pick different directions and only one side picks a contest direction gets a chapter plan with a one-sided contest ("a contest with no players on side A" / "uses contest results but has no players on side B", sometimes a player in two threads); the plan check rejects it and the one retry repeats it. Food trucks turn 6 (both failed: production stops); space pirates turn 6 (the retry fixed it) and turn 10 (both failed). `handleProgression` then emits "Unable to continue the story. Please try again." and no path sends it again. 2 of 2 group stories, 2 of the 3 occurrences.
- **The harness's continuations (my call, not production's behaviour; marked on the pages and in `stuckTurns`):** `retryFailedTurns` (up to two more rounds of production's checked planner; food trucks passed on the first, with production's repair dropping Jo's chosen thread) and `repickStuckSwitches` (`coordinatedRepick`: the named players move to the shared direction another player took, a contested one first, then one more plan; space pirates turn 10 after three failed rounds, two with 30 s planning timeouts, succeeded on its first call). Why continue rather than stop: the brief asks for whole stories, and every turn after is production's code again; the readings say where production would have stopped.
- **Readings (code and my hand read):** all four end on their turn count (11, 26, 26, 26); 23 chapters at allowed lengths; 28 of 28 milestones land stage by stage; each last chapter settles only its next stage; late pacing binds at all 3 lemonade switches and the last 2 space pirates switches, each pushing a needed outcome; no chapter offers a second reward or sacrifice; levers paid 5 of 5 (one code flag at Avalon turn 21 a false alarm: +10 then the unfavourable chapter's −10); no unfit stat change; judged stage 11 of 12 chapters (Avalon chapter 2's milestone reaches the relationship's later state); judged endings 6 of 7 players (Mika's tells two complete outcomes as open, since their last milestones settled nothing). Continuity turn to turn strong by hand, except: exploration options whose text doesn't match the result at their position (Avalon turn 12 → the partnership broken over a secret at 13; lemonade turn 6) and challenge results written as the player's action (Avalon 22 → 23 retracts, 23 → 24 presses for restoration).
- **Also found:** group turns offer almost no levers (food trucks 0 of 36 chapter option sets, space pirates 2 sacrifices at turn 16; B6 is single-player only) and never change a player's own stat (only shared stats move); late personal outcomes sharing a 2-turn chapter complete by count with hedged or repeated milestones (space pirates turns 17/23, 23/26, 23); the food truck license settled at turn 9 of 25, offered again complete at switch 19 (`noCompleteOutcomeOffered`), an aftermath chapter, 3 of 2 milestones; the shared Tavi friendship completed by one player; the scoreboard moved toward side A after a side-B win (food trucks turn 22), ending 50|50; the lemonade stand never opens (its one-milestone money outcome settled in chapter 1 from a purchase list); a doubled step (Avalon chapter 3); options written on two endings; step titles as turn titles (Avalon 11, 20). Waits over allowance on 17 of 88 turns: mostly retries on top of a turn (Avalon 5 a runaway at 119 s, 18 a one-paragraph retry used as is at 85 s; space pirates 6 and 23 at 95-98 s) and three-player chapter openings (median 62.5 s against 60 s). Cost per story $0.044 / $0.110 / $0.154 / $0.208 (the last with about $0.022 of the harness's rounds), level with the final check's $0.11 / $0.14 / $0.18.
- **Suggestions for the owner (none built):** for the dead end, a plan-check repair that turns a one-sided contest into that side's challenge (no calls) plus a path that sends a failed turn again; option n written from result n and challenge results as outcomes, not actions (a request change with its own run); B6 and own-stat changes for group turns, measured as a variant; one outcome per short late chapter or a last-stage milestone that decides; a code check on the scoreboard's direction.
- **Also:** no browser check (Playwright didn't connect this session). `.context/text-model-eval.md` updated (the continuations, the readings, the spend, Known limits); `.context/ai-transparency.md` gains a "Not features" line for the eval harness and its story pages. Report: `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/2026-09-30_playthroughs-report.md`; pages `stories/index.html` and one per story.

## Whole-story playthroughs, the review's fixes (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **The brief:** the playthroughs review's ten verified issues. The instruction was to fix production or harness defects test-first, with no paid run unless a fix needed one replay under the stage's remainder, and to correct the report where it was wrong. No paid call was made: stage $0.5235 of $0.70, ledger $36.73 of $40.
- **Production fix 1, a group exploration step goes the way its outcome's owner chose** (`ThreadResolutionService.determineExplorationThreadResolution`). It had taken `playersSideA[0]`'s choice ("for now, just choose whatever the (hopefully) one player in the thread chose"), so player1's choice wrote another player's own outcome. That happened in 5 of the 7 group exploration steps: food trucks chapter 7 (Luz's crew promise, Jo's choices) and space pirates chapter 5 (Mika's outcome, Ari's).
  - Now: the owner decides when they are in the thread; otherwise the majority decides; a tie goes to the game's dice among the tied choices. A single player, the owner and a clear majority roll no dice, so single-player play and its seeded replays are unchanged.
  - My call on the tie: the dice rather than the seat. By seat, player1 would decide every 1-1 split on a shared outcome.
- **Production fix 2, the plan check keeps a group player in the thread their switch pick named** (`threadsKept`, PL-4). The rule was "first thread listed". At space pirates turn 6 that let production's own retry drop two of three picks. The first chapter and a single player still keep the first thread. A kept thread other than the first is noted `threadKeptOnPick`.
- **Production fix 3, a one-sided contest becomes that side's challenge** (`oneSidedAsChallenge`, PL-12, repair `contestOneSided`). This is the report's suggestion (b), and it removes the dead end.
  - Scope, my call: groups only. In a contest game it applies only when some player is outside the thread; a one-sided contest holding everyone stays a problem, because the opponents are missing, not elsewhere. In a cooperative group it applies to whoever is in the thread, since those groups can't play a contest; this also closes the code review's open case. A single player's contest results stay a problem.
  - Fix 2 needs fix 3: without it, keeping players on their picks would turn a pick-dropping plan into a failing one.
- **Production fix 4, PACING's threads that fit** (`chaptersThatFit` = max(÷ 4, `fewestThreads`)). This removes the contradiction "about 0 more threads fit" beside "exactly 2 beats". It changes production's switch request at 3, 6, 7 and 11 turns left, so the kept tests carry a logged delta (`withThreadsThatFit` in `tests/helpers/adoptedDeltas.ts`, used by `adoptedPlanners.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts`). The eval's measured copy and `turnDesignChecks`' late checks keep ÷ 4, so stored readings stay as measured. Unmeasured, a correction of a contradiction in production's own request.
- **Verified without calls:** a temporary Jest replay (deleted) played the two group stories on today's code from their stored replies by case id. Every served request was the stored one byte for byte. Both turn-6 first plans are now used with no retry: food trucks gives Jo her school-kitchen chapter and Luz a license challenge; space pirates gives Ari a Black Star challenge, Juno her purser chapter and Mika the galley, each as picked.
  - Turn 10 can't be reached, since the story now goes another way from turn 6. Its six stored plans each failed only on the one-sided contest, with everyone else in their own threads, which fix 3 converts.
  - What stays: a converted contest keeps the contest's words. The replay's Luz challenge has "Jo wins the Hush Basin public tasting" as its unfavourable result while Jo is at the school kitchen. Suggestion (a), a planner line, would give clean text but needs a run.
- **Not fixed in production (suggestions in the report):**
  - the same shared sacrifice offered to two players in one step. Ari and Mika each got +30 from the one Ivo Senn favour. A prompt change for group turns needs measurement, and a code repair would have to guess what a shared stat can pay;
  - options and results that don't match, and results written as actions (a request change);
  - setups whose final-chapter rule names an outcome with 1-2 milestones;
  - New Avalon's missing Heart: the setup's stages skip the premise's question, and the ending judge reads milestones only;
  - the one-paragraph retry used as is, by design.
- **Harness, test-first:**
  - `playthroughChecks.ts` reads the threads that fit from the turns left with production's count (`toldFit` where the record differs), every group step (`groupSteps`, `ownerOverridden`), each group switch pick against the next chapter (`switchPicks`, with the threads the plan check dropped), levers of several seats on one shared change (`sharedOnce`), `waitsLeftOut` and `shortTextUsedAsIs`.
  - `playthroughPages.ts` shows a group step's result beside each choice (the option's own result is now labelled so), the owner overridden, the plan check's repairs on each chapter, each pick not followed, and the count the planner was told.
  - The files were re-rendered with `--report-only`.
- **Report corrected in place** (its section 11 lists what changed):
  - new 5.2 (the choice bug), 5.6 (the Heart) and 5.9 (PACING);
  - 5.1 now covers space pirates turn 6's dropped picks;
  - the continuity undercount (Avalon 6-7, 18-20, 22-24; food trucks 14-15, 23-25; lemonade's whole set shifted);
  - the final-thread diagnosis, graded as the review graded it: lemonade clear, food trucks partly, space pirates not really;
  - Jo's ending, partly right by hand (judge 6 of 7, by hand 5);
  - the one-paragraph retry fixed 1 of 3;
  - levers: 6 taken, 4 paid outright;
  - two waits left out: 17 of 87.
- **Also:** `.context/story.md` (the plan check's PL-4 and PL-12, the exploration step, PACING's count) and `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the delta, the readings, Known limits) updated. `.context/ai-transparency.md` checked, nothing to change: game mechanics and a plan repair, no new AI use. No browser check (Playwright didn't connect); the page tests cover the new lines. `npm run check:all` clean.

## Playthroughs follow-ups, the five no-call fixes (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **The brief (the coordinator's, part A):** five production fixes without model calls, each test-first and its own commit: a failed turn sent again, a turn with no options retried, the scoreboard's direction, a shared lever for one player per turn, an exact copy of a chapter's last step dropped. No paid call; the ledger stays at $36.73 of $40.
- **Fix 1, a turn that fails is sent once more** (`BaseQueueProcessor.resendFailed`, `TURN_RESENDS` = 1 in `GameQueueProcessor.ts`).
  - Where the game gave up: `handleMoveStoryForward` rethrew, the queue's `handleOperationError` marked the operation failed and emitted `operationError`, and nothing listened: `GameHandler`'s listener was lost in commit 1b16181 (2025-05-11), beside the removed `storyInitialized` one, while its constructor still bound the handler and `dispose` still removed it. So the players got no message and nothing sent the turn again; a reload shows the turn with every choice made, and `validateChoice` refuses a second choice.
  - Now: the failure path queues a failed `moveStoryForward` (its writing; see the follow-up below) once more, at the back of its game's queue (image outcomes queued meanwhile run first), with the same input story; `sends` on the operation counts the sends, so a second failure takes the failure path and nothing sends a third. The warning names the operation and the error's class, never its message. My call: the resend goes through the queue rather than a loop inside `handleMoveStoryForward`, so it is the failure path's own budget ("through today's error path") and generic (`resendsFor`, 0 for every other operation: a choice, a character selection or a pregeneration is never resent).
  - The notification: `GameHandler` listens again, so a turn that fails on both sends reaches every player in the game as "Unable to continue the story. Please try again." My call: a failed pregeneration tells no one (it is background work; the turn is written when played), since reconnecting the listener would otherwise have sent its generic error to every player. Not changed: the client shows that message nowhere while a story is on screen (`GamePage` renders an error only without a story state, and only logs it otherwise), so a turn that truly fails still leaves the players waiting, now with the message in the console. A visible notice is a client change with a visual check (Playwright was not connected); not built.
  - What it costs: a failing turn waits for a second full attempt (its planner call and retry, and the beat) before failing, at most as long again; a turn that succeeds is untouched.
  - The playthroughs harness models production as it ran: its first extra planner round (`retryFailedTurns`) is what production's resend now does, so its "production would have stopped at" and the pages' "nothing sends it again" describe that day's production. Not rebuilt (noted in the context doc).
  - Tests: `QueueProcessor.test.ts` (the budget: resend within it, report after it, none without it, the resend queued behind what the game already had, the log line), `GameQueueProcessor.resend.test.ts` (the real processor with its services mocked: a turn failing once is stored and broadcast once with nothing reported; failing twice is reported once as `moveStoryForward`; a failed choice is never resent), `GameHandler.test.ts` (the message reaches every player's socket, never the error's own text, no message for a failed pregeneration, none after `dispose`).
- **Fix 2, a turn with no options is retried once** (`beatChecks.ts`: `missingOptionsProblem`, `beatReplyProblem`, `checkedBeatReply(…, { ending })`; `AIStoryGenerator` passes `getCurrentBeatType() === "ending"`). The case: production's reply on `synth-8988006e-t3-pregeneration_2_player1_1-noimg` this morning came back with empty options and interludes (1 of 64 production turns); the player could not have gone on.
  - Now: a player's beat with no options, outside the ending, gets the one-paragraph retry's one call, told "the beat for player1 has no options; write three options for every player's beat" (both problems, the short text first, when a reply has both).
  - My call, where the brief said "like the one-short-paragraph retry": unlike a short text, a reply without options is never used, because nobody can play it. A retry without options, or a retry whose call fails, fails the turn (`UnusableResultError` "Failed to generate a usable beat", the problem on `.problem`), and fix 1 sends the failed turn once more. The exception: a first reply that had options and was only short is used when its retry comes back without options or fails, as before.
  - The playthroughs harness calls `checkedBeatReply` with production's ending flag and records the combined problem; its readings keep one-paragraph retries apart from the new `optionsRetries` (by the recorded problem's words), and its earlier stop at "no options" now reads the retry.
  - Tests: `beatChecks.test.ts` (the problem for one and several players and for options left out, none at the ending, the combined problem; the retry told the problem and its options used, the ending unretried, both problems told, a second reply without options failing the turn, a short first reply kept over a retry without options, a failed retry call failing a turn without options); `playthroughs.test.ts` (the retry and the play on, the stop when the retry has none either, no retry at the ending); `playthroughChecks.test.ts` (`optionsRetries` apart from the one-paragraph retries).
- **Fix 3, the scoreboard moves only toward the side that won** (`scoreboards.ts`: `scoreboardOf`, `scoreboardWinners`; `beatRepairs.ts`: `repairScoreboardMoves`, repair `scoreboardDirection`).
  - Where the moves are applied: the model writes them in the turn's `statChanges` (the switch turn after a contest chapter, per the setup's rule), `ChangeService` applies them as written, and the round-0 beat repairs were the one place that reads them first. So the check sits there, after `statNumberAsText` (a "35|65" text is a move too).
  - The case (food trucks turn 22): side B (Luz) won chapter 6; the reply's own `statsAffectingDecisionConsequences` said "Luz's Side B victory shifts the race 15 points toward the dependable-neighborhood-favorites contender" and its change was `addNumber 15`, so 35|65 went to 50|50 and the story ended level after Luz won both decided contests.
  - The rule: the scoreboard is the shared opposites stat the contested outcome's resonance names ("Scored by …"), else the story's only shared opposites stat; its value is side A's share. The result followed: at a switch or the ending, the chapter that just ended; in a chapter, the step just played (none on its first step). My call: a move the other way is turned around by the same size rather than dropped, since the model sized it and only the direction contradicts the recorded result (the case's own reasoning says so); a value set is reflected around the score before, within 0 to 100. Now turn 22 would read 35|65 → 20|80.
  - Left as written: moves after a mixed result (the setup's rule says none; the brief is the direction), moves on turns that follow no contest result, two contests on one scoreboard that disagree, and a contest converted to one side's challenge (PL-12), whose side isn't stored.
  - The eval reads replies after the game's repairs, so readings re-rendered from stored replies count `repair:scoreboardDirection` where a stored move went the wrong way.
  - Tests (`beatRepairs.test.ts`, TR-8): the food trucks case turned around and applied (20|80), moves toward the winner kept, a value set reflected (and clamped) and a negative addition, moves read in order, a mixed result, a chapter step after a contest step, the ending, no contest result followed (a chapter opening, a switch after a challenge chapter), the named scoreboard beside another opposites stat and the fallback, two disagreeing contests.
- **Fix 4, a shared sacrifice or reward goes to one player per turn** (`beatRepairs.ts`: `repairSharedLevers`, `leverStatOf`, repair `sharedLeverRepeated`, note `sharedLeverRepeatedKept`).
  - The case (space pirates turn 16): Ari (player1) and Mika (player3) in one shared challenge were each offered "Call in Ivo Senn's dockside favor …" (+30), the crew's shared Dockside Favors holding only Ivo Senn; both took it and both rolled with +30, and turn 17 removed the favour once. The review's fix-list said a code repair "would have to guess what a shared stat can pay"; the coordinator settled the code check (the report's own suggestion: "a code check that offers it to the first player only").
  - What is read, not guessed: an option names its lever's stat only in its text, which the schema asks for. The stat is the one the text names (a plural name also without its "s": "dockside favor" names Dockside Favors), else the one whose held value it names, else the story's one stat allowing that lever; of those, the ones that allow the lever. Where more than one stat is left (the text names a player's own stat and a shared one), nothing changes. Only a shared stat's lever is limited; a player's own stat is each player's.
  - Now: the first seat offered a shared lever keeps it; each later seat's copy of the same lever on the same stat leaves that player's options (two left). My calls: the copy is dropped, not retyped to a normal option, since its text still promises the favour and it would roll without its +30; a sacrifice and a reward of one stat are different favours and both stay; in an exploration set the copy stays (noted), because an option's position there is its result and dropping one would move the others' results.
  - Not changed: groups still get almost no levers (B6 is single-player only); this only stops one favour paying twice. Two different items of one shared list ("Ivo Senn" and "Mara Voss") offered to two players count as one lever on that stat, the brief's rule; the second player loses it.
  - Tests (`beatRepairs.test.ts`, TR-9): the space pirates case (player3's copy dropped, player1's kept), a shared reward, a sacrifice and a reward of one stat both kept, own-stat levers kept for everyone, the stat read by name (singular and plural) and as the one stat allowing the lever, a text naming two stats left alone, an exploration step's copies kept and noted. The playthroughs' `sharedOnce` reading can't arise on production's code any more: its test now plays a shared lever (only player1 takes it) and reads `sharedOnce` on that run's levers doubled, as a run stored before the fix.
- **Fix 5, an exact copy of a chapter's last step is dropped by the plan check** (`planChecks.ts`: `withoutRepeatedLastSteps`, PL-14, repair `lastStepRepeated`, note `lastStepRepeatedKept`).
  - The case: planners wrote the last step in `steps` and again as `finalStep`, which the assembly turns into two progression steps, the copy with its own step results and the last with the milestones; the chapter is a beat longer and plays its moment twice (planner v2e 2 of 46 plans; Avalon chapter 3 in the playthroughs).
  - Now: a step before the last with the same title and question as the last (whitespace aside) is dropped, and the last step stays, since its results are the milestones; the duration then follows the steps (`durationFromSteps`). "Without guessing": only an exact copy, never a relabelled or reworded one (planner v2d's copies had "the question the same or relabelled").
  - My call: only where dropping gives the plan no problem the written one didn't have. A group plan whose threads would be left with different step counts, a two-step thread that would keep one step, or a length PACING allowed that the drop would make one it doesn't (the last chapter must take exactly the turns left) keep every copy, noted. The first two would be problems, which can fail the turn; the third a length problem, one more call. Where the written length was already off (a copy making a chapter too long), the drop fixes it.
  - The eval reads plans after the check: a planner's own exact-copy rate is now `repair:lastStepRepeated` beside `stepsOnce` (context doc). The playthroughs' design-check test now doubles its step relabelled, a copy the check keeps.
  - Tests (`planChecks.test.ts`, PL-14): an exact copy dropped with the last kept and the length taken from the steps (fixing a length PACING refused); a relabelled copy kept and a whitespace-only difference dropped; a copy kept, noted, where the drop would leave a length PACING refuses; a two-step thread kept; in a group, copies kept where threads would differ in length (also where the written length was off: a length problem, never a problem) and dropped where every thread had one.
- **Fix 1, a follow-up found reviewing the diff:** `handleMoveStoryForward` threw one error for everything after the turn was written too: storing and broadcasting it, and starting its images and pregeneration. With the resend, a pregeneration that couldn't start after the turn was stored and sent would have written the turn again from the old story, over the one the players had, and the reconnected listener would have told them it failed. Now only the writing (`handleProgression`) is resent; a failure storing or sending the written turn is reported but not resent (`TurnDeliveryError`: it may be stored already); and the images and pregeneration, which start after the turn is stored and sent, never fail it (logged, as nothing reached players before either). `resendsFor` takes the error. Tests in `GameQueueProcessor.resend.test.ts`: a failed store reported once and not resent; a pregeneration that can't start neither fails nor resends the stored turn.
- **Checks:** `npm run check:all` clean after fix 5 (server 3,332 tests, client 210) and again after the follow-up (server 3,334). No browser check: nothing on the client changed.

## The five no-call fixes, the review's two findings (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **Fix 3 turned correct moves the wrong way wherever a contest's side A is not the scoreboard's first side** (important). The repair took the stat's value as side A's share, which holds only for setup round 3's form. Two paths broke it:
  - (a) A story or template set up before round 3 (6afd1ad, 2026-09-28) names no scoreboard, so `scoreboardOf` fell back to the story's one shared opposites stat. The old setup prompt made opposites meters for what changes often ("Order|Chaos", "empire|rebellion", "major moral choices shift value by 5-15%") and never said which side comes first, while PL-11 puts player1 on side A. A "Printers|Enclave" meter with player1 as the Enclave: side A wins, the model writes `subtractNumber 15`, and the repair turned it into `addNumber 15`. In a chapter it ran after every contest step. Resumed stories and every library template made before 09-28 take this path.
  - (b) Three players with player1 sitting the contest out: PL-11 keeps the planner's sides there. With player2's camp on side A and player3 (player1's camp) winning, the model's move up was turned down.
  - Now (`scoreboards.ts`): only the stat the outcome's resonance names ("Scored by …") is a scoreboard. It arrived in the same commit as "The first side is player1's" (6afd1ad; checked with `git log -S`), so the name also fixes the orientation. Only a contest with player1 on side A is read: PL-11 (2026-09-27, before any "Scored by" setup) puts them there when they are in it. A contest player1 sits out, or one with player1 on side B (a plan PL-11 never saw), names no winner, so a scoreboard it shares with another contest is left alone too. Everything else stays as written. Not a measurement question: the stored playthroughs name both their scoreboards ("Scored by License Race", "Scored by Black Star Lead"), so the food trucks turn 22 repair and the readings re-rendered from stored replies are unchanged.
  - Not done: reading a pre-round-3 story's meter as a scoreboard by its tooltip or side names. That would be a guess, and those stories' setups never promised a scoreboard rule.
  - Tests (`beatRepairs.test.ts`, TR-8): the named scoreboard beside another opposites stat; a pre-round-3 "Printers|Enclave" meter left alone at the switch and after a chapter step; three players with player1 sitting out (player2 on side A, player3 won, the move up kept); player1 on side B; a second contest with player1 on side B on the same scoreboard. The fallback case in the earlier test is gone (it asserted the behaviour this removes).
- **Fix 1's reconnected listener sent a failed choice's raw error text to the chooser** (minor). Before ed48f48 nothing listened to `operationError`, so `handleOperationError`'s pending-operation branch never ran. Now it runs for every failed `recordChoice` or `recordCharacterSelection` (their resend budget is 0). It sent the friendly line, then rejected the pending promise with `new Error(event.error)`. `makeChoice`'s and `selectCharacter`'s catch sent that raw text to the socket as `error`, and `websocket.ts` sent it again as the response's `errorMessage`. A write error (ENOSPC or EACCES, whose text names the server's data path) or a DB error reached the client's state and console twice (not the screen: `GamePage` only logs an error while a story is on screen).
  - Now: the pending operation is rejected with the friendly line, so every message the chooser gets is that line (`GameHandler.handleOperationError`). The raw text is still logged on the server. A failed character selection gets its own line, "Unable to save your character choice. Please try again.", instead of the default "Something went wrong with operation recordCharacterSelection…", which named the operation. Not changed: the chooser still gets the line more than once (the handler's `error`, the caller's `error`, the response), as the handler did before 1b16181. Harmless, since the client keeps the last one.
  - Tests (`GameHandler.test.ts`): a failed choice and a failed character selection, each queued through the real `makeChoice`/`selectCharacter`, reject with the friendly line, and nothing sent to the chooser holds the error's text.
- **Checks:** `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,338 tests, client 210). No browser check: nothing on the client changed.

## Choices and results (part B), the reading and the build (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **The brief (the coordinator's part B):** the playthroughs' exploration options that don't match the result at their position, and challenge results that say what the player does instead of how the attempt turns out, so the next turn follows the result, not the choice. Find where the mismatch arises (the planner's step results, the turn's option writing, or the order mapping), build a variant at the cause, add a check calibrated on hand examples, run it beside production ×2 (stage `choice-result`, $0.40), adopt if it passes.
- **Where it arises (read on the stored runs and their stored prompts, no calls):**
  - **The order mapping is right.** `BeatResolutionService.getExplorationBeatResolution` records option n as `resolution{n}`; nothing reorders options after the turn. Not the cause.
  - **Exploration: the turn's option writing.** The turn is shown the step's results ("Possible outcomes: Resolution 1: …"), and nothing in production's request says its options are those results in that order (turn round 2's B7 line was never adopted). The mismatches cluster on a chapter's first step, often after the text had already carried out one result: New Avalon turn 6 (option 2 holds nothing back, option 3 lays the trace out where result 3 withholds it), turn 14 (the text told the account without naming Nemi, result 1, and the options answer the next step), lemonade turn 6 (the whole set shifted), food trucks turns 14 and 17 (both players' sets), space pirates turn 14 (Juno) and 18 (Mika). Later steps mostly match (New Avalon 7, 8, 10, 11, 16; lemonade 7, 9, 10; food trucks 15, 18). My reading: a step-2 turn sees how the step before mapped (its chosen option and RESOLUTION) and copies the pattern; a first step has nothing to copy. The planner's exploration results are mostly the player's paths as asked; one step (food trucks turn 17, Luz) wrote the crew's reactions, which no option can carry out.
  - **Challenges: the planner's step results.** New Avalon's hearing chapters (turns 18 and 22) wrote results as what Eli does ("Eli's question folds the possibility into the confirmed evidence", "leans on the warning as stronger proof", "Eli plainly retracts" / "concedes" / "defends his earlier framing", "urges immediate restoration"); the turn at 22 even wrote its options as those three actions, and each next turn (19, 20, 23, 24) followed the rolled action, as production tells it to ("The thread configuration lays out what it means specifically to succeed and fail. Follow those guidelines."). The same shape, milder, in most of the playthroughs' challenge and contest plans (food trucks turns 6, 10, 20; space pirates 10; New Avalon 2's door step; lemonade 2's quantities). Space pirates turns 2 and 14 wrote outcomes.
- **Built (test-first; each request read end to end in a temporary dump, deleted):**
  - `choiceResult` (the turn): production's turn, every player count, with one line where a player's thread explores (the context doc has the words). My calls: the reason in the line (the game records the chosen option's resolution as what the player did), "without its consequences" (production says options carry no consequences; results do), and "the beat text … carries out none of them" (turns 6 and 14 had the text carry out a result first). Groups included: the line reads per player, and three of the six mismatched sets were group turns.
  - `planV2f` (the chapter planner): planner v2e with each results sentence followed by its rule. My call: no turn-side guard ("narrate the chosen action, take only how it turned out") in this variant; the cause is the plan's results, and a second turn change would need its own reading. It stays a candidate for saved stories whose plans predate a fix.
  - The cases (`--build-choice-cases`, no calls): 16 turns and plans replayed from the stored runs (`playthroughReplay.ts`), each only where its request is the one production sent there byte for byte; eight single-player and three group exploration steps (mismatched and matched), five chapter plans. The replay rebuilds all 37 single-player turns and the group turns up to the first group exploration step the owner now decides.
  - The checks: the judged `optionsFollowResults` (per player at an exploration step) and `resultsFitKind` (per chapter plan), calibrated on hand-read playthrough items (24 and 20, read before any judge call; the items and their notes are in `choiceResultJudge.ts`), and the deterministic `explorationOptionsFollowResults` word check as a proxy.
  - My calls on the hand reads of `resultsFitKind`: a result that names the player's approach counts as a fail even when mild (New Avalon 2's "open the door carefully" / "force a narrow opening", lemonade 2's "chooses quantities"), since the next turn can follow it over a choice; an achievement or a slip is an outcome. Three plans that name an approach only in passing are partial.
- **Stage `choice-result`** ($0.40, reason recorded; the ledger at $36.73 when it opened): turns under `adopted5` (`--role beat`), planners under `round0` beside planner v2e's stored plans (`--role thread`). Dry runs: 52 turn jobs, est $0.19; 43 plan jobs, est $0.07; the judges about $0.09-0.11 on top.

## Choices and results (part B), the run, the retest and the verdicts (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **What ran (13:48-14:25 UTC):** a turn smoke (New Avalon turn 6, both arms, read end to end: the variant's options are the step's three results in order, production's third option asks about residents and takes a reward), then the other 50 turn jobs (the dry run's 52), every one valid on its first attempt; a planner smoke (New Avalon turn 18), then the other 41 plan jobs (the dry run's 43), all valid first time; the judges (a 4-call smoke on two calibration items, then 233 calls; the default $0.10 invocation cap refused the first try on its $0.115 estimate, nothing sent, so `--max-spend 0.14`); the judged stage check on the planners' new plans (46 calls, `--stage choice-result`); the fix-and-retest below (2 + 18 turns, 20 judge calls).
- **Calibration (before reading any arm):** `optionsFollowResults` 22 of 23 (hand yes 12 of 13, no 10 of 10), samples 23 of 24: reliable. Its one miss, New Avalon turn 7, is defensible (option 1 "let Ilan present" against result 1's "present together"). `resultsFitKind` 16 of 17 (yes 8 of 9, no 8 of 8), samples 19 of 20: reliable; its miss (New Avalon turn 6's plan) is a result whose second half is Ilan's action, which I had called minor. The word proxy (`explorationOptionsFollowResults`) agrees on hand no 10 of 10 but hand yes only 6 of 13: a strict lower bound, read as a rate.
- **The turn (`choiceResult` against production, same minutes):** options following their results: one player 14 of 20 → 19 of 20 (moved higher, p 0.046; production missed New Avalon 6 and 14 twice each, lemonade 6 and the café's second step once; the variant New Avalon 14 once, where its options again answered the next step), groups 0 of 6 → 6 of 6 (p 0.001; production missed both players at food trucks 14 and 17 and Juno at space pirates 14 in every sample). The proxy moved the same way (20% → 70%, 0% → 83%). **The wrong-way move:** 3 of the variant's 20 single-player replies were one short paragraph (New Avalon 6 and 11, lemonade 9; production 0 of 20), each narrating the last choice and stopping: `words` 329 → 272 and `turnSentences` 18.1 → 15.6 moved lower, the paragraph rule 100% → 85% beyond the noise. Production's one-paragraph retry would send each again (a call and about 25-35 s more). Groups: no short reply, nothing moved the wrong way. Waits: one player's chapter openings p95 56.7 → 44.5 s, chapter steps 38.4 → 47.2 s (New Avalon 11, both samples, reasoning 2,800-3,000 tokens: its plan's step 3 is an exact copy of step 2, a plan production's check now shortens); groups level (two players 34.8 → 35.3 s, three 57.1 → 53.9 s). Cost level (−0% one player, +1% groups).
- **The one fix-and-retest (`choiceResultB`, my wording):** the line's last sentence "The beat text leads up to the three and carries out none of them." read as the text's whole job, so it became "The beat text is written in full as always (5-6 paragraphs that set up the moment) and carries out none of the three: the player's option does." Twice on the ten single-player steps (25 minutes later, at 8.6-8.9 ms a token against 9.8-10.5). Options following results 19 of 20 again; but 3 of 20 were one short paragraph again (New Avalon 6, 7 and 10), so `words` and `turnSentences` moved lower again. The short replies follow the line, not its last sentence. Not adopted for one player; no second retest (the stage had $0.016 left).
- **The planner (`planV2f` against planner v2e):** results fitting their kind 8 of 33 → 18 of 33 on the shared pairs (moved higher, p 0.011; noise 11 points): stored cases 5 of 23 → 14 of 23, built cases 3 of 10 → 4 of 10. The hearing chapters still write some results as Eli's approach ("Eli keeps the evidence distinct", "Eli explains the limits"), in both forms, though no longer three rival decisions. Nothing moved the wrong way: the automatic checks (stepsOnce 97% → 91% and repeatedSteps beyond the noise, not moved; the plan check drops exact copies anyway), the judged stage check 27 of 27 → 25 of 27 beyond the noise, not moved (p 0.245; both fails borderline step questions of the kind v2c and v2d also had: "what they should make together next", "what they can honestly carry forward together"). Waits: p50 8.2 → 8.3 s, p95 19.9 → 20.9 s, output tokens +3% (visible +7%, reasoning −20%): level. Cost +1%.
- **My calls, the verdicts:** planner v2f passes the stop rule (its check moved, nothing moved the wrong way, waits level): adopted. The exploration-order line passes on group turns (its check moved, nothing the wrong way, waits level) and fails on one player's turns (the one-paragraph replies, in both forms): adopted for group turns only, in the run's wording (`choiceResult`, the form measured on groups). Why split by player count rather than hold it: the two populations run different models and efforts (Luna low for groups, Luna medium for one player), the short replies appeared only on Luna medium, and B6 was adopted the same way, for one player count. The group sample is small (three cases, six replies, thirteen player sets), which the report says.
- **Spend:** turns $0.170 (52), plans $0.067 (43), the retest $0.054 (20), the two judged checks $0.083 (257 calls), the stage judge $0.014 (46): stage $0.387 of $0.40; the ledger $37.11 of $40.

## Choices and results (part B), the adoption (implementer, 2026-09-30; no calls, $0)

- **Test-first, the kept tests changed on purpose:** `adoptedPlanners.test.ts` holds the chapter planner to `planV2f` (was `planV2e`), with a new test that the step results' two rules are the one difference from planner v2e on every test story; `adoptedTurns.test.ts` holds a group's exploration step (a shared exploration thread, and one player exploring beside a challenge, two and three players) to `choiceResult`, with a new test that the line comes once, after the option types, and never on a single player's exploration step or a group's challenge step; `adoptedForms.test.ts` measures thread cases against `planV2f` and a group's exploration beat cases against `choiceResult`. Each failed first (25 failures), then passed. No logged delta in `adoptedDeltas.ts`: production is each measured form byte for byte.
- **Production:** `ThreadPromptService.progressionItem` prints the rules text (`stepResultRules`, production's own copy of planner v2f's, both player counts); `BeatPromptService.createOptionInstructions` prints `EXPLORATION_ORDER` after the option types where `takesExplorationOrder` (in `optionRules.ts`: a group's chapter step where some thread explores). The eval's copies stay frozen in `turnRound1Planners.ts` and `choiceResult.ts`; `choiceResult.test.ts` now holds `productionTurnToday` to production everywhere but a group's exploration step, and production there to the variant.
- **The eval's own byte-for-byte reads of the stored playthroughs** (`choiceResultCases`, the replay's stored-run tests) compared the rebuilt request to what production sent that morning through today's `adopted`, which now sends planner v2f and the group line. They now read `playthroughsSent` (planner v2e's plans, the turn without the line: production as it stood before this adoption); the tests' fake runs, played through today's code, read `productionSends`. The 16 frozen cases are unchanged.
- **Not adopted:** the line for one player, in either form (`choiceResult`, `choiceResultB`). A single player's exploration options keep production's rate (14 of 20 at their own result on this stage's cases).
- **What a later paid run needs:** production's chapter planner and a group's exploration turn now differ from what `round0`'s planner v2e and `adopted5`'s group records sent, so a paid run of production's own code takes a new prompt-state tag.

## The exploration line for one player, with production's retry in the loop: the build (implementer, 2026-09-30; no calls, $0)

- **The brief (settled by the coordinator):** the exploration-order line for single-player turns, measured with production's existing one-short-paragraph retry in the loop as the safety net; the eval counts what the player gets after that retry (the final reply), the retry rate and the wait including the retry. Beside production on the choice-result run's single-player items ×2, a new stage `choice-line-sp` capped at $0.25. Stop rule: options at their own result must move; nothing may move the wrong way on the final replies; the p95 wait including retries no slower than production's beside it and within 45 s for chapter steps (60 s chapter openings). Adopt if it passes, test-first, the byte-for-byte test updated deliberately; else record why.
- **Built (test-first):**
  - The runner sends production's one checked retry as a turn job's second step (`CheckedRetry`, `Job.retry`), right after the first reply, where production's own check (`beatReplyProblem`, with the ending's flag) finds the first reply short or without options; the retry is the first request told the problem (`withBeatProblem`), on the same arm, schema and limits, with production's parse re-sends inside it. The first reply's final record carries `checkedRetry` and does not end the job; the retry's final record does. `jobPlan.ts` attaches it to every turn of a stage `stageChecksTurns` names (only `choice-line-sp`), so no earlier stage's job or record changes.
  - My call: the retry in the same job and minutes as the first call, not a later pass over the stored first replies: the wait including the retry then meets the same server pace, as production's does.
  - The generic readings (results.md) read a turn by its first reply, as the choice-result run did: `isResultRecord` and the turn waits leave a turn's step 2 out (`isCheckedRetry`), a first reply the retry follows counts as a finished pair (`finishesFirstReply`), an arm's prose counts it once.
  - The final reply is read by `checkedTurns.ts`: the reply the game keeps is decided by production's own `checkedBeatReply` run on the two stored replies (no copy of its rule), the wait is both steps' answers plus every attempt re-sent at once, the cost every attempt. `asCall` turns each turn into one call, so the stop rule over every automatic check (`variantComparisons`) and the turn waits read the kept replies with the whole wait and cost.
  - `--choice-line-sp` (`choiceLinePrep.ts`): the options judge (`optionsFollowResults`, the choice-result stage's, calibrated reliable there; no new calibration) on every reply once, first and retry, then `choice-line-sp.md` and `.json`.
- **Stage `choice-line-sp`** ($0.25, reason recorded; the ledger at $37.11 when it opened), prompt state `adopted6` (production's single-player turn requests are adopted5's byte for byte; a new tag so production runs beside the line). Dry run: 40 jobs, est $0.13, and at most $0.13 more if every first reply were retried. Every one of the 40 first requests hashes to the choice-result run's stored `adopted5` request for the same case and arm; I read the lemonade turn 9 pair end to end in a temporary dump (deleted): the line is the one difference, after the option types, and the retry request is the first with production's problem sentence appended.

## The exploration line for one player, the run, the verdict and the adoption (implementer, 2026-09-30)

- **What ran (15:20-15:30 UTC):** a smoke (New Avalon turn 6, both arms, sample 1: production's first reply was one short paragraph of four sentences and got the retry, which came back in five full paragraphs; the line's reply was full first time; read end to end), then the other 38 turn jobs (the dry run's 40 in all), every first reply valid on its first attempt, and 3 retries in all, every one production's (New Avalon 6 s1, lemonade 6 s1, New Avalon 11 s2), each a short first reply, each retry full; then the options judge (a 5-call smoke on New Avalon 6, its answers read, then the other 38; 43 replies).
- **Readings (`choice-line-sp.md`):**
  - Short replies: first replies production 3 of 20, the line 0 of 20; kept replies 0 and 0; retry rate 15% and 0%; no turn failed, no reply without options.
  - Options at their own result: first replies 10 of 20 → 19 of 20 (moved higher, p 0.002); **kept replies 12 of 20 → 19 of 20 (moved higher, p 0.010)**. Production missed the café's two steps, New Avalon 7, 10, 14 (twice) and 16; the line only New Avalon 14 once (the same miss as the first run). The word proxy moved the same way (30% → 75%).
  - Every automatic check on the kept replies under the stop rule: nothing moved either way but the proxy. Beyond the noise, not moved: words 312 → 296 (1.9 SE), sentences 17.7 → 17.4 (0.4 SE), the waiting-close share 25% → 40% (p 0.25), one reply of 20 with a last paragraph pointing at the choice, one stock phrase, facts 3.75 → 3.55.
  - Waits including the retry, p95 (12 chapter openings and 8 chapter steps a side): openings 69.3 s → 34.4 s, chapter steps 59.3 s → 41.0 s; production's are its own retries (New Avalon 6 s1, 36.4 + 33.0 s; New Avalon 11 s2, 32.2 + 27.1 s); first replies alone 44.3 s → 34.4 s and 37.3 s → 41.0 s (the line's slowest step New Avalon 11 s1, 41.0 s on its first attempt). An opening here is the turn alone (no planner ran); with planner v2f's p95 of 20.9 s from the choice-result stage added, 55.3 s against production's 90.2 s.
  - Cost per turn with the retry $0.0033 → $0.0028 (reasoning −26%).
- **My reading of the short replies:** the first run had the line 3 of 20 and production 0 of 20; this run production 3 of 20 and the line 0 of 20, on the same requests byte for byte. Pooled, 3 of 40 each: Luna medium's base rate of one-paragraph turns (about 3% on every eval form; 7.5% here), not the line's. The retry rescued all three.
- **Verdict (the coordinator's stop rule):** options at their own result moved on the replies the player gets; nothing moved the wrong way on them; the p95 waits including retries are no slower than production's beside them and within 45 s (chapter steps, 41.0 s) and 60 s (openings, 34.4 s). **Adopted.** Caveat: 8 chapter steps a side, so a step's p95 is its slowest turn.
- **The adoption (test-first, the kept tests changed on purpose):** `adoptedTurns.test.ts` holds a single player's exploration step to `choiceResult` too (`measuredTurn`; the line once, after the option types, on every exploration step of every player count, never on a challenge step, a switch or the ending), `adoptedForms.test.ts` measures every exploration beat case against `choiceResult`, and `choiceResult.test.ts` holds production to the variant on every exploration step, `productionTurnToday` to production everywhere else; 7 failed first, then passed. Production: `takesExplorationOrder` (`optionRules.ts`) drops its group-only condition; `BeatPromptService` prints the line as before. No logged delta. Three eval tests that held an earlier arm's base to production on a single player's exploration step now read production as it stood when that arm ran (the line taken out there, the one difference): `switchReminder.test.ts` (the runaway fix), `turnOptionsContinuity.test.ts` (`productionTurnForm`, the options arms' base) and the choice-result and choice-line-sp stages' `jobPlan.test.ts` cases (production's own request is now the variant's; what it sent then is `productionTurnToday`). `playthroughsSent` (the stored playthroughs' rebuild) already reads the turn without the line, so the frozen cases and their tests are unchanged.
- **Spend:** turns and retries $0.121 (43 calls), the judge $0.0075 (43 calls): stage $0.129 of $0.25; the ledger $37.24 of $40 (about $38.54 if the stalled Stage 4 calls of 26 September were billed).
- **What a later paid run needs:** production's single-player exploration turn now differs from what `adopted6`'s production records sent, so a paid run of production's own code takes a new prompt-state tag.

## A failed turn on screen, with "Try again" (implementer, 2026-09-30; no calls, $0)

- **What was wrong, read end to end:** the server's failure lines reached the client, but the provider's error handler only acted on `data.type === "error"`, which no forwarded error has (`WebSocketService` wraps every one as `game_error_notification`), so a failure while a story was on screen was never even stored: the next beat's spinner, "Setting up the story..." or "Processing Character Selection..." (its background operation never cleared) stayed up for good. And re-sending a choice could not have helped: once the turn fails, the choice is stored, so `validateChoice` refuses it ("Choice already made for this turn"), and reloading shows the same waiting state. So "Try again" needed a server path.
- **The server:** `retry_turn` (`websocket.ts`, rate-limited as a choice) → `GameHandler.retryTurn`: queues `moveStoryForward` from the stored story only when it waits for its turn (`waitsForItsTurn`: every character picked, every choice in; at the ending no one chooses, so never) and `GameQueueProcessor.isTurnOnItsWay` is false (a turn, choice or character selection queued or running, a resend included; `BaseQueueProcessor.hasUnfinished`, which counts pending and processing only, so a failure being reported does not count). Otherwise it sends that player the stored story. So N players pressing it queue one turn, and a turn stored meanwhile is never written again. `tellIfTurnStuck` after a successful `verify_code` tells an arriving player the same line when the story waits with nothing on its way (a reload, a second device, a restart mid-turn: the queue is in memory). Retry errors answer with the friendly line only.
