- **The client:** `turnFailure.ts` (`turnFailureFrom`: turn, retry, and the player's own choice or selection from the queue or the response, never untyped or join-code errors; `stillFailed`: until a state shows a new beat or the player's own choice or character stored, images ignored; `tryAgainRequest`: the selection again while picking, the choice again when it never got stored on that beat, else `retry_turn`), `gameService` keeps the last choice (with its beat count) and selection and forgets them on exit, the provider holds `turnFailure` and `tryAgain` (clears the notice and the stale `select_character` operation, sends the request; a rate-limited retry brings the notice back). `TurnFailedNotice`: the ui `Notification` in its error style (new `dismissible={false}`: no close button, since closed it would leave the player waiting on nothing), `role="alert"`, title "Something went wrong", the server's line, a "Try again" `PrimaryButton` (44 px). It replaces the spinner in the next-beat placeholder (`isWriting`), both "Setting up the story..." spinners, sits below the latest beat when a choice failed (never below an earlier beat), and above the character selection's confirm button, which stops showing "Processing…".
- **Multiplayer:** every player in the game gets the line (as before) and sees the notice; any one's "Try again" queues the turn once; when its beat arrives every notice goes. Left open: the other players' notices stay up while that retry is being written.
- **Not changed:** the provider's dead `data.type === "error"` branch (making it live would put join-code errors on the error page, a separate change); the handler's duplicate untyped error emits.
- **Tests (test-first; failed first, then passed: 13 server tests; on the client 8 tests, and 4 new suites that could not load before their modules existed):** `QueueProcessor` (hasUnfinished), `GameQueueProcessor.resend` (isTurnOnItsWay through both sends, choice and selection count, images never), `GameHandler` (retryTurn on real two-player stories, tellIfTurnStuck), client `turnFailure`, `GameService`, `TurnFailedNotice` (static markup, and the button's handler through the element tree), `StoryDisplay`, `CharacterSelection`, `GameLayout`. `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,451, client 243). No visual check: the Playwright MCP server failed to connect and ToolSearch found no Playwright tools, so the notice's look at desktop and mobile widths is unseen.

## The review's three findings on the failed-turn notice and the single-player line (implementer, 2026-09-30; no calls, $0)

- **1. A copy of a failed choice or selection could queue a second turn (fixed, client and server).** After a failed choice or pick, sending it again through the game's own controls left the notice up (it cleared only on "Try again", a state that moved on, or exit), so its "Try again" re-sent the same request beside the one on its way; the Confirm button stayed free while that request was pending (`isSelectionPending` was forced false under any failure); and "Try again"'s own `make_choice` left the options open with no local choice. On the server, `validateChoice` and `validateCharacterSelection` read the stored story, which lags the queue, so a copy passed while the first was queued or running, and each `recordChoice` / `recordCharacterSelection` re-ran the all-in check on its own copy of the story and queued `moveStoryForward`: two paid turns, the second over the first, and a copied selection stored "selection not complete" for a moment.
  - Client: the failure on screen is a reducer now (`failureOnScreen` in `turnFailure.ts`: failed, sent, rate limited, state arrived, exited; it replaces the provider's `turnFailure` state and its `triedAgainRef`). Any send that answers the failure hides it: "Try again", and the player's own choice or character through the game's controls (`clearTurnFailure`, called in `GamePage`'s two handlers before they send); a rate-limited send brings it back. Confirm is locked while a `select_character` request is pending whatever failure is up (the stale queued operation still counts only while none is). "Try again" returns what it sent (`gameService.tryAgain`, the provider's `tryAgain`), and when that is the choice again the display shows it as chosen, as a click does (`showChoiceSent` in `useStoryBeatState`).
  - Server: the queue records each player's choice on a beat, and each player's character, once. `handleRecordChoice` reads the stored story first and skips a choice already recorded on that beat, or whose beat the story has moved past (`choiceRecorded`); `handleRecordCharacterSelection` skips a player whose character is stored, or a selection already complete (`characterRecorded`). A copy of a choice whose first send failed before storing is still recorded. The per-game queue runs them in order, so a copy always finds the first's result.
  - Left as it was: the options' reset effect in `useStoryBeatState` still reopens them if any render lands between `make_choice_response` and the stored choice's broadcast (as after any click, milliseconds without a model call); a click there is now a copy the queue skips.
- **2. The single-player line's wait pass and safety net were untested on the line (corrected in the record, no code).** The readings count what they should (the kept reply by production's own `checkedBeatReply`, the retry rate, the wait including the retry), but no request carrying the line was retried (0 of 20), so its "waits including the retry" are first replies alone, and like for like the line was slower on chapter steps (41.0 s against production's 37.3 s). Short replies look like a base rate on both forms (3 of 40 each over the two single-player runs; the line 6 of 60 counting `choiceResultB`), so one retry among the line's 8 chapter steps was about as likely as not (1 − 0.925^8 ≈ 46%), and a retried turn waited 59-69 s, which would have put the line's step p95 over 45 s as it put production's. "No slower and within 45 s" was where the short replies fell, not a property of the line: expect it level with production in expectation, and at or over the limit whenever a retry lands. And "the retry rescued all three" is evidence for production's form only; whether a short reply carrying the line comes back full, options at their own result, is unmeasured (the risk the stage was for; `choiceResultB`, which asked for the full text inside the line, still drew 3 of 20 short). The adoption stands on the options (kept replies 12 → 19 of 20, p 0.010) with short rates not measurably different; both untested points are in `.context/text-model-eval.md` and the status note's section 9. Measuring them needs a paid run: production's retry sent on the line's own short first replies (the choice-result run stored six, three from `choiceResult` and three from `choiceResultB`), read by the options judge, and a larger single-player sample for the waits.
- **3. A player who stayed on the page through a reconnect never got the notice (fixed, server).** On every reconnect (a network drop, a server restart) the client sends `rejoin_session` with the player's code, and no server handler existed: the new socket was never added back to the game or its room, so the player got no more states, kept the spinner of a turn the restart had lost, and every choice or "Try again" failed ("Player not found") until a manual reload. `.context/story.md`'s "a server restart mid-turn … gets the same notice" held only for a reload. Now `websocket.ts` answers `rejoin_session` (rate-limited as `verify_code`, since it looks up a code): `GameHandler.rejoinGame` adds the connection to the game as `verify_code` does, joins the room, sends the stored story (a turn may have arrived meanwhile), runs `tellIfTurnStuck`, and the handler broadcasts the active players and answers `rejoin_session_response` (`{ rejoined }`; core types `RejoinSessionMessage`, `RejoinSessionResponse`), which also clears the client's pending `rejoin_session` that used to stay set for good. Not done: the rejoin carries no user id (the slot's user was assigned at `verify_code`); a rate-limited rejoin still leaves the client's pending flag set.
- **Found while reading, not fixed (pre-existing, not seen in play):** `recordChoice` and `recordCharacterSelection` work from the story as it was when each request was checked, so two players choosing or picking within the same moment (before the first is stored) can leave only the second stored; the first player's options or selection come back with no notice. A fix is to build each record from the stored story when it runs (the character selection is one line; the choice goes through `processChoice`'s pregeneration paths and wants its own look). `handleRecordChoice` also fails the choice when pregeneration can't start after the choice is stored and the turn queued (the same shape `40f2b34` fixed for turns): the player sees "Unable to process your choice" while the turn is on its way; "Try again" then sends `retry_turn`, which finds the turn on its way, so nothing is written twice.
- **Tests (test-first; failed first, then passed: on the client 5 tests and the `turnFailure` suite, which could not load without `failureOnScreen`; on the server 6):** client `turnFailure` (`failureOnScreen`), `GameService` (tryAgain returns what it sent), `CharacterSelection` (Confirm locked on a pending request beside a failure), `StoryDisplay` ("Try again" shows a choice it sends again as chosen, never for `retry_turn`), new `GamePage.test.tsx` (both handlers clear the failure before sending, through a `GameLayout` mock that keeps its props); server new `GameQueueProcessor.duplicates.test.ts` (a copied choice and a copied selection recorded once, one turn; a failed first send and another player's stored choice still recorded) and `GameHandler` (`rejoinGame`: adds, joins, sends the story, then the stuck-turn line; nothing without a code or a story). `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,459, client 256). No visual check: the Playwright MCP server failed to connect again.

## GPT-6.1 Sol accepted, its price, its effort guard, and the $42 hard cap (implementer, 2026-09-30; no calls, $0)

- **The brief (settled by the coordinator, the prep for a second round of playthroughs):** accept GPT-6.1 model names in the text-model allow-list and settings, add `gpt-6.1-sol`'s price to the eval, stop the server at startup on an effort a model doesn't take, change no production default; raise the eval's hard cap from $40 to $42 (the owner OK'd "a few more dollars to do useful playthroughs") and record why. Source for the model's facts: `DOCS/2026-09-30_sol-6-1-assessment.md` (OpenAI's model and pricing pages, read that day).
- **The allow-list:** `modelFamily` (`chatModel.ts`) takes `gpt-6-*` and `gpt-6.1-*` as the gpt-6 family (same request: no temperature, effort through `modelKwargs`, explicit caching, `max_completion_tokens`), a served snapshot name included. My call: `gpt-6.2-*` and later stay refused. A point release can change the efforts or the request form, as 6.1 did with `none`, so the next one should be let in only after someone has read its page. That's a one-character change when it comes. The settings' startup message now says "production text calls take gpt-6-* and gpt-6.1-* models". `gpt-6.1-luna` would pass the family check, but no such model exists (OpenAI's page is a 404). The check reads families, as it did for `gpt-6-*`.
- **The effort guard:** `reasoningEffortsFor(model)` (`textModelSettings.ts`, beside `REASONING_EFFORTS`) gives each model the efforts it takes: `gpt-6.1-sol` low, medium and high (OpenAI lists low to max; the settings have never offered xhigh or max, and still don't); everything else keeps none to high. `assertSupportedSettings` checks against it, so the factory refuses `gpt-6.1-sol` at `none` before any call is sent: "gpt-6.1-sol needs a reasoning effort of low, medium, high (got none)". `resolveTextModelConfig` stops the server at startup, naming the variable, what the model takes, why, and what to set, e.g. "GENERATION_MODEL_REASONING_EFFORT=none is not supported by gpt-6.1-sol, which takes low, medium or high: OpenAI refuses it and a refused call is never retried, so every call would fail. Set GENERATION_MODEL_REASONING_EFFORT=low." The suggestion is the group's own default effort. The missing-effort and not-an-effort messages now list the model's own efforts. `minimal` was already refused for every model and keeps its hint. The guard reads the model, not the group: `gpt-6-sol` and `gpt-6-luna` still take `none`. The probe is untouched, because it sends refused shapes as raw bodies, never through the factory.
- **No default moved:** every group's default is as it was, and the kept byte-for-byte tests are untouched.
- **The eval's price table (`pricing.ts`):** `gpt-6.1-sol` at $2.00 input, $0.10 cached input, $2.50 cache write and $10.00 output per 1M tokens. Uncached calls cost the same as on today's Sol (a setup at the assessment's medians: $0.105). The reasoning-token and speed estimates used to key on "gpt-6-sol", so a 6.1 arm would have fallen back to Luna's reasoning and 140 tokens a second. They now read 6.1 as Sol (`isSol`: 700 reasoning tokens at low, 2,400 at medium, 90 tokens a second) until measurements exist. Artificial Analysis measured the same pace at low (66.1 against 65.9 tokens a second). No arm, stage or probe check runs 6.1 Sol yet (the assessment's step 4 and the probe are the owner's separate call).
- **The hard cap: $42** (`HARD_CEILING`, which is also the default global cap). The ledger read $37.24 of $40, so $2.76 was left, or about $1.46 if the stalled Stage 4 calls of 26 September were billed. That's too little for a second round of playthroughs beside anything else. The reason is recorded in `budget.ts`'s header and the constant's comment, in the dry run's total line ("raised from $40 by the owner on 2026-09-30 for more whole-story playthroughs, …") and in `.context/text-model-eval.md`. `--global-cap` still can only lower it. With the stalled calls on top, $42 leaves $3.46 above the ledger, enough for the planned `playthroughs-2` stage ($1.20, not built here).
- **Tests (test-first; 11 failed first and the factory's suite couldn't load without `reasoningEffortsFor`, then all passed):** `chatModel.test.ts` (the gpt-6 shape on `gpt-6.1-sol`; the family, a snapshot name and `gpt-6.2-sol` refused; the efforts per model; the factory refusing `none` and `minimal` on 6.1 Sol, taking `none` on today's Sol); `textModelSettings.test.ts` (6.1 Sol accepted in a group, no default moved; the startup refusal of `none` with its variable, efforts, reason and the group's effort as the suggestion; the model's own efforts in the minimal and missing-effort messages; `none` kept for today's Sol; a later point release refused); `armsBudget.test.ts` (6.1 Sol's price and a snapshot name; uncached equal to today's Sol; its reasoning and speed estimates as Sol's; the $42 cap and lowering it; the global-cap check moved to $42); `dryRun.test.ts` (the total line names $42 and why); `resultsReport.test.ts` (the total row's cap). `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,470, client 256).

## Whole-story playthroughs, round 2: the build, the run and the readings (implementer, 2026-09-30; stage `playthroughs-2`, $0.746 of $1.20; the ledger $37.99 of $42)

- **The brief (the coordinator's, after the owner OK'd "a few more dollars to do useful playthroughs"):** replay the first round's four stories on production's current code, add a two-player competitive story whose contest runs through several chapters and a short read-with-kids story, in a new stage `playthroughs-2` capped at $1.20. Estimate first, smoke two turns of one story, compare the dry run's counts with the run's, then read every story by code and by hand against the first round's issues.
- **Harness, test-first** (6 tests in the checks and files suites failed first, and four suites couldn't load until `PLAYTHROUGHS_2`, `turnSends`, `PLAYTHROUGH_ROUNDS` and `PLAYTHROUGHS_2_PROMPT_STATE` existed; the scoreboard refinement below failed first too):
  - **Rounds** (`PLAYTHROUGH_ROUNDS`, `--round 2`). Round 2 has stage `playthroughs-2`, prompt state `adopted7` (production since the single-player exploration line and the failed turn's resend), `PLAYTHROUGHS_2`, `playthroughs-2.json|md` and pages in `stories/round2/`. The job key's prompt state keeps its case ids apart from round 1's. `evalFiles` takes a file base and a pages folder. Round 1's files and pages stay as they were.
  - **The two premises, my picks.** "Rival estate agents trying to sell the same haunted mansion" (flexible competitive #4): one prize and no shared bond named, so the setup's competitive slate gives the contest 3 milestones rather than the bond slate's 2 (the first round's food trucks got 2). "A field mouse saving the burrow village" (read-with-kids single-player #1, age 5, the youngest suggestion), merged as the client merges that category; `kids: true` follows production's `category === "read-with-kids"`. `Category` is now exported from `setupPremises.ts`.
  - **A failed turn is sent as production sends it.** `writeTurn` runs one send: the planner, the turn, the repairs and the changes, from the resolved story. `turnSends(tryAgain)` gives the order: first, the queue's resend (`TURN_RESENDS`), then per Try again press a send with its own resend. Each failed send is kept (`failedSends`), with `sentBy` for the send that worked. A spend-limit refusal is never resent.
    - My calls: round 2's player presses Try again once, and round 1's rules press none, since the notice didn't exist then. The chapter's dice are resolved once per turn, so a resend doesn't roll them again. Production would re-roll them, which only matters for a group tie-break.
    - The harness's own planner rounds (`retryFailedTurns`) are gone. `coordinatedRepick` stays as the last resort after every send, marked `after repick`.
    - `TURN_RESENDS` moved to `retryOnce.ts` so the harness reads production's constant. `GameQueueProcessor` re-exports it; no behaviour change.
  - **More judged checks after each story:** the choice-result stage's calibrated `optionsFollowResults` per player at every exploration step, and `resultsFitKind` per chapter plan (`judge-play-options-v1-…`, `judge-play-results-v1-…`).
  - **New readings:**
    - `resentTurns`. Waits leave out a turn whose resend failed too, because the players saw the notice; a turn the resend saved counts with every send.
    - `ownStats`.
    - `scoreboard`: moves against the recorded `contestResults`, from production's `resultsFollowed`, now exported. A step's win that holds the scoreboard is fine by the setup's rule; a chapter's win that holds it is flagged.
    - `fixes`: the turns the 30 September fixes fired.
    - `choices`.
  - **Pages:** the resend and Try again lines, the judged options under a turn and the results on a chapter, a round's own report and index title, and a link to round 1's pages.
- **Dry run:** 187-213 story calls (typical 191), est $0.602 before retries, plus about 122 judged checks at about $0.05. The smoke (lemonade, two turns) made 5 calls and 1 judged check, as the dry run's count says, for $0.012.
- **The run** (17:40-18:10 UTC, the six stories side by side): 212 story calls plus 1 runner re-send ($0.714), and 100 judged checks ($0.033), for 313 attempts. Every story reached its ending on its turn count.
  - No turn failed, so the resend and Try again never fired live.
  - The one-sided contest repair fired once (space pirates turn 10). Switch picks were followed 39 of 39, and 43 of 43 chapter milestones landed.
  - Judged: stage checks 15 of 16, options 41 of 41, results 13 of 33, endings 6 of 10.
  - Scoreboard moves after a won chapter went toward the winner 7 of 7. Group turns offered 1 lever in 129 option sets; no chapter in any story offered a reward. Group players' own stats moved for 4 of 7.
  - Waits went over their allowance on 10 of 126 turns, 8 of them with a retry or re-send.
  - Cost per story: $0.042, $0.117, $0.161, $0.181, $0.161 and $0.053.
- **By hand (all six read):**
  - **Exploration choices now lead where they say;** I found no mismatch. A challenge option pulled against its favourable result once (New Avalon 22 → 23).
  - **New:**
    - aftermath last chapters in New Avalon, food trucks and estate agents, because all outcomes completed before the last switch;
    - the space pirates setup's integrity rule held two chapters on the complete ship, leaving Pip's outcome 1 of 2;
    - both group contests settled their last stage with one side absent: the estate agents' challenge written by the planner, and the space pirates' converted contest, whose "captain's camp" wording puts the captain in the scout's galley scene while he is on the docks and the ship is in the pylons;
    - the estate agents' ending wrote milestones on four complete outcomes;
    - planted details repeated and never paid off in every story;
    - the kids story reads at grown-up length and vocabulary;
    - one-paragraph first replies rose to 13 of 126 turns, and two short retries were used, one of them the food truck story's first turn for both players.
  - **Endings:** I agree with the judge's four fails (complete outcomes told as open). I read New Avalon's Orin line as partly softened.
- **Readings fixed after the first render:** a step's win that holds a scoreboard was first flagged as a problem (`held after a win`). The setup's rule moves a scoreboard only after a chapter, so it now reads `held after a step win` (fine) or `held after a chapter win` (flagged), test-first. The files were re-rendered with `--report-only`.
- **Report:** `DOCS/2026-09-26_gpt6-text-eval/2026-09-30_playthroughs-2-report.md`, with pages in `stories/round2/`. `.context/text-model-eval.md` gains the round-2 mode and results, and `.context/story.md` the constant's new home. `.context/ai-transparency.md` was checked: nothing to change, since this is eval-only with no new AI use. No browser check (Playwright didn't connect). `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,489, client 256).

## 2026-09-30 (late evening): the review of the second playthroughs (no calls, $0)

The review verified nine findings on the round-2 report. All nine hold; three are overstated in part (noted below). The report is corrected in place, with a new section 12 listing the corrections.

- **Production fix, no calls (test-first): a lever charged twice.**
  - **What happened:** in 2 of the 7 levers taken, the turn after the payment took the same cost again.
    - New Avalon: turn 2's bracing (-15% Personal Reserve) was paid at turn 3 (60 → 45). Turn 4 took it again (45 → 30), its reply reading "Jun spent 15% of his reserve bracing the control ring". Without it Jun would have been at 30%, not 15%, at turn 18, and the stat's "below 30%" -10 on one option at turns 18-19 wouldn't have applied.
    - The mouse story: turn 9's crumb wedge was paid at turn 10 (3 → 2). The ending took it again (2 → 1): "Bran used a Pantry Crumb as a wedge in the previous beat".
    - The cause is the request. The history shows only the last beat's chosen option (a normal one), beside a summary that narrates the cost, and nothing says the cost is paid. The harness read only the turn after the choice, so it counted both as paid.
  - **The fix:** `leverPayments.ts` (new; `leverStatOf` moved there from `beatRepairs.ts`). `beatStep.apply` records on each chooser's new beat the lever its turn paid (`Beat.paidLever` in `core/types/beat.ts`: kind, group, stat and the step; percentage and number stats). `repairBeatReply` then drops, on the turn after, the first change that moves that stat by the same step (`leverChargedAgain`), one per payment.
    - Kept: a charge a lever on the last beat now owes (any player's), another size or the other way, a late payment (no record), and later turns.
    - The risk, written down: a same-size change a stat rule asks for on that very turn is dropped too.
    - On the stored runs (replayed, no calls), the repair drops exactly round 2's two double charges and nothing in round 1 as far as it replays. The request is unchanged; the prompt line that would tell the next turn is a paid measurement.
  - **Tests:** 10 tests failed first: 7 repair and record tests, 1 stored-run test, 2 harness readings. Then they passed. Round 2's 126 turn requests replay byte for byte (a new test), so the replay the reading rests on is the played story.
- **Harness readings, no calls (test-first):**
  - `leversPaid.chargedAgain`: each kept reply replayed through production's current repairs. `leverChargedAgain` joins the fixes.
  - `ownStatsLine` lists "moved and back where they started" apart.
  - Round 2 was re-rendered with `--report-only`: the pages list New Avalon turn 4 and the mouse story's turn 11, and Nia's credibility (40 → 40, 2 turns) as back where it started.
- **Report corrections:**
  - **Milestones:** 44 of 44, not 43. The same slip is corrected in `.context/text-model-eval.md`; the commit message of `5ec550a` keeps 43.
  - **Group own stats:** 3 of 7 from start to end, and 2 of 5 on round 1's premises, not 4 of 7. Nia's one change was a contest loss the ending reversed.

The mouse setup's Cat's Nearness is higher-is-worse, so its "sacrifice" moved the cat away, a second benefit beside the +30. New Avalon's Heartwell Feedback is backwards too but was never used. The setup prompt's examples and the schema's lever wording pull that way, and nothing checks direction. The fix is paid.

Turn 23's plan and text told result 1 (not until verified), the milestone blended them, and the ending told result 1. The judge passed it. So the hand count is 4 of 10 endings pass, 1 partly and 5 fail, not 5 of 10.

His favourable roll turned her unfavourable one into the mixed result her milestone records. The rolls are pooled by design, and the owner rule covers exploration steps only. It is a design call for the owner, not a bug.
- **Not built, and why:**
  - **Dropping milestones on outcomes no ended chapter pushed** (report 6.3) is no-call, but it reverses the TR-4 rule and its test that keep them. It waits for the owner's OK.
  - **The owner's roll for group challenges on an own outcome:** the owner's call.
