## 2026-09-30 (night): fix 1, the turn that completes an outcome and the ending (stage `outcome-settled`, $0.362 of $0.39; not adopted)

The coordinator's fix 1 of the review's paid list: a complete outcome is never told as still open, stored facts give way to milestones, and a stat is not set against the milestone written in the same reply. Its own stage, capped at $0.39 (the $0.30 estimate plus 30%), the reason recorded in `budget.ts`.

- **The cause, in the stored stories and production's request:**
  - The space pirates' switch turn 14 wrote the treasure claim's completing milestone (2 of 2), but its texts told the claim "open and unsettled" and it stored the fact that the crew's "provisional galley record … does not settle Pip's repair-reserve proposal", beside older facts calling it provisional. The ending's request said the claim was complete, yet all three endings followed the facts.
  - New Avalon's switch turn 16 wrote the parting milestone (the outcome's third resolution, 2 of 2) and, in the same reply, set Orin's relationship to "Deeply Trusted" (the first resolution's level), reasoning from the stat's own rule ("one step up after a favorable thread that offers honest care").
  - The request: a switch turn is told to add each ended chapter's milestone, and the state shows the outcome at "1 / 2"; nothing says this milestone completes it, while the chapter's last turn, the earlier milestone and the stored facts all call it open. Nothing ties a stat change to the milestone beside it. The ending reads every fact and summary written before.
- **The variant** (`outcomeSettled`, `storyTextRounds/outcomeSettled.ts`, eval only, test-first; production's turn today byte for byte elsewhere):
  - on a switch turn after a chapter and at the ending, a stat line after the thread-resolution lines: no stat change contradicts a milestone the beat adds, even where the stat's adjustments after threads point elsewhere (a bond does not rise to its closest level beside a parting);
  - on a switch turn whose milestones complete an outcome, the outcomes it completes with the game's count (`outcomesCompletedThisBeat`, no aftermath) and that such an outcome is settled: its milestone written without softening the resolution, every player's text and every fact told settled; earlier milestones, facts and summaries that call it open were written before;
  - at an ending with a complete outcome: its milestones hold over an earlier milestone, fact, summary or stat level that calls it open.
- **Cases** (`outcomeSettledCases.ts`, `--build-settled-cases`, no calls): 12 turns of the second round's stored runs, replayed, each only where its request is the one production sent there, byte for byte (`choiceResultCases` gained a category parameter). Seven switch turns completing an outcome (the defect's space pirates 14 and New Avalon 16; estate agents 15, food trucks 20, lemonade 4, the mouse story 5, New Avalon 23) and five endings (space pirates, estate agents, New Avalon, lemonade, food trucks). Every built request was read end to end.
- **Checks:**
  - A new judge, `completedToldSettled` (`outcomeSettledJudge.ts`), one Luna low call per completing switch turn: the completed outcomes, other milestones, stat changes with levels and values before, facts and every player's text. Calibrated on 22 hand-read items (11 yes, 6 no, 5 partial: stored turns, the run's own replies by output id, read before the judge ran, and two constructed failing versions of stored turns). **Not reliable:** v1 15 of 17 with the hand (it passed a pending rival proposal as "a separate issue" and a credibility stat cut by 30 as "no bearing"), samples 19 of 22; its one fix, v2's decision rule, 16 of 17 (it now fails a ship outcome whose own milestone leaves it needing refuge), samples still 19 of 22 (86%, floor 90%).
  - The endings: the ending's calibrated `outcomesToldAsLeft`, reused (prompt v1).
  - The automatic checks in `results.md`.
- **The run** (19:49-20:08 UTC): dry run 48 jobs, est $0.19; the smoke's 2 jobs as counted, then 46; every reply valid, production's once after a reply cut at its output cap. Then the retest's 19 jobs, as the dry run counted.
- **Readings:**
  - **By hand, switch turns:** production told a completed outcome as open on 2 of 14 (the space pirates 14 sample 1, "the repair-reserve proposal remains unresolved" in its milestone and a fact; New Avalon 23 sample 1, facts "leaves the bypass's oversight arrangements unsettled"); New Avalon 16's stat was never set against the milestone (no stat change in either sample). The variant 0 of 14, but its stat line moved Orin to "Wary" beside "they part on good terms" in 1 of 2 replies (the retest in 2 of 2), a move production never made there.
  - **The judge, indicative only:** v1 production 12 of 14, the variant 14 of 14 (within the noise); v2 8 of 14 against 14 of 14 (moved higher, p 0.008) and the retest 11 of 14.
  - **Endings (reliable judge):** 4 of 10 → 6 of 10, beyond the noise, not moved (groups 0 of 6 → 2 of 6, one player 4 of 4 both). On these replays both arms resolve the treasure claim. The rest fail in both arms for other causes: the space pirates' ship (complete at 4 of 2 after two aftermath chapters, told as stranded; fix 8), Rory's principle retold as a lesson after later chapters moved him, Suri's result (fix 2).
  - **Wrong way:** `milestoneNotCopied` one player 91.7% → 25.0% (moved lower, p 0.001): "Write its milestone as the thread's resolution settles it" wrote the plan's words instead of the specific milestone B1 asks for. `youParagraphShare` moved higher (p 0.095 and 0.072).
  - **Waits and cost:** within allowances, level with production.
- **The one fix-and-retest** (`outcomeSettledB`, test-first): the settled line asks for the milestone "made specific from the thread's text as the line above asks, keeps everything the thread's resolution settles and softens none of it", and the ending line's "follow the milestones" became "tell the outcome as its milestones leave it". Twice on the 7 switch turns, once on the 5 endings. `milestoneNotCopied` still moved lower on one player (90.0% → 40.0%, p 0.029); `youParagraphShare` no longer moved; the target within the noise.
- **Verdict: not adopted.** The target never moved under the stop rule: production showed the defect on 2 of 14 replayed switch turns (not the 1 in 1 the stored stories suggested), too few to move on this sample. The switch-turn judge could not be made reliable in its one fix. And both forms moved `milestoneNotCopied` the wrong way. The stat line also brought a new questionable move ("Wary"). Production is unchanged, so the byte-for-byte test and its deltas are untouched.
- **What the owner could weigh instead:**
  - The ending line alone (4 of 10 → 6 of 10, not moved) touched no milestone check on groups but its single-player endings copied too (3 of 4 against 1 of 4, a small sample).
  - The ship-and-aftermath failures belong to fix 8, which will re-measure endings on whole stories.
- **Spend:** $0.362 of $0.39: 68 turn attempts $0.274, 185 judge records $0.088. The ledger $38.35 of $42; fixes 2-8 have about $2.04 of the workflow's $2.40.
- **Docs and checks:**
  - `.context/text-model-eval.md` gains the stage (commands, variant, arms, budget, the run).
  - `.context/story.md` is unchanged: production is.
  - `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,549, client 256).

## 2026-09-30 (night): fix 2, the next turn tells the result the game recorded (stage `recorded-result`, $0.168 of $0.26; not adopted)

The coordinator's fix 2 of the review's paid list: the turn after an exploration choice tells the result the game recorded (food trucks turn 22 → 23). Its own stage, capped at $0.26 (the $0.20 estimate plus 30%), the reason recorded in `budget.ts`.

- **The cause, in the stored story and production's request:**
  - Turn 21: Suri chose result 1 of the cabinet chapter's first step (make verified operation a condition and wait). Turn 22's text carried it out ("Nothing goes in until we know what those readings mean") and stored facts that the ingredients stay outside and the demonstration stays paused.
  - Turn 22: she chose result 2 of the last step (use the cabinet only after documenting and disclosing the reading, within stated safeguards), and the game recorded it (`BeatResolutionService`: option n is result n).
  - Turn 23's request (the stored prompt, hash `92f918…`) shows "CHOSEN OPTION: Proceed only after documenting the uncertain readings…" and "RESOLUTION: RESOLUTION2. Suri uses the cabinet only after…", and the thread's milestone line. The reply's own plan read it as "making clear that the cabinet will not be used unless its operation is verified"; its text wrote "No ingredients stored here until the operating temperature is independently verified", its fact kept the ingredients out until verified, and its milestone blended both. The ending followed the milestone.
  - Why: the turn is told to continue exactly where the previous beat ended, that whatever the beat text established stands, and to make the milestone specific "based on the thread's narrative text". Nothing says a choice that changes course holds over the earlier text and facts that point the other way.
  - How common: of about 25 turns in round 2 where a recorded exploration result changed direction from the step before, this was the one told as the other (read on every such turn, replayed).
- **The variant** (`recordedResult`, `storyTextRounds/recordedResult.ts`, eval only, test-first; production's turn today, `choiceResultRequest`, byte for byte elsewhere):
  - where the turn narrates an exploration step's recorded result (a chapter step after one, a switch turn or ending after an exploration chapter), a line after the narrative-feedback line: the chosen resolution is what the player did, the text shows it and the facts follow it; where the earlier texts, results or facts point another way, the player changes course, shown, never folded back or blended;
  - on the switch turn and the ending, a line after the milestone lines: the milestone is the chosen resolution made more specific from the thread's text, which holds where that text points another way.
- **Cases** (`recordedResultCases.ts`, `--build-recorded-cases`, no calls): 8 turns of the second round's stored runs, each a change of direction, replayed, each only where its request is the one production sent there, byte for byte. Food trucks turn 23 (the defect) and seven ordinary ones: the space pirates' switch turn 14 and step 25 (three players, each changing direction), the estate agents' switch turn 15, lemonade step 7, New Avalon step 11 and switch turn 16, the mouse story's switch turn 8. The built requests were read end to end (production's and the variant's; the variant adds its one or two lines only).
- **The check:** a new judge, `recordedResultTold` (`recordedResultJudge.ts`, `--judge-recorded`), one Luna low call per player of an exploration thread whose recorded result the turn narrates: the step with its three results (the recorded one marked) and the option picked, the earlier steps' results, the milestone on the thread's outcome, the facts and the player's text. Calibrated on 25 items read by hand before any judge call: 13 stored turns that changed direction (hand yes), food trucks 23 (hand no), six constructed failing versions of stored turns (the text carrying on the earlier course, a blend, a milestone and a fact telling another result), three of the run's own replies read after the run (yes) and two partials. **Reliable:** 22 of 23 (yes 15 of 16, no 7 of 7), samples 23 of 25. The miss fails the space pirates' stored turn 9 on a fact it reads as a blend.
- **The run** (20:41-20:46 UTC): dry run 32 jobs, est $0.13; the smoke's 2 jobs as counted, then 30; every reply valid on its first attempt.
- **Readings:**
  - **By hand (before the judge ran):** production told the recorded result on 16 of 16 replies, food trucks turn 23 included (2 of 2: "then use the cabinet only within the safeguards you have stated"). The variant 15 of 16 and one partial, on the defect case (sample 2: "nothing goes into the cabinet until its operation is checked and the handling conditions are clear", never shown using it, its milestone result 2 word for word).
  - **The judge:** pooled production 13 of 16, the variant 12 of 16 (within the noise); one player 6 of 8 → 7 of 8 (beyond the noise, not moved, p 0.50); groups 7 of 8 → 5 of 8 (within the 25-point noise). It is stricter than the hand: of its seven fails on the arms, the hand passes six (facts read as another result, a step's promise read as not guiding the expense, a result shown only through another character).
  - **Automatic checks:** nothing moved the wrong way. Groups `words`, `paragraphs` and `turnSentences` moved higher because production's replies came back one paragraph a player on the space pirates' step 25 (both samples) and for Suri on food trucks sample 2 (production's retry would ask again in play). `milestoneNotCopied` beyond the noise, not moved.
  - **Waits and cost:** within the allowances; one player faster (chapter steps p95 34.0 → 26.8 s), groups slower where production's replies were one paragraph (two-player switch 32.9 → 43.0 s, three-player step 30.0 → 38.0 s); cost per call level ($0.0032 → $0.0030 one player, $0.0053 → $0.0055 groups).
- **Verdict: not adopted, and no fix-and-retest.** Production didn't repeat the stored error on its own state (0 of 2 on the defect case, 0 of 16 overall), so there is nothing for the stop rule to see move at this size; a retest could not pass it either, whatever its wording. The one reply where the line mattered, the defect case, is where the variant slipped toward the earlier course. The money is better kept for fixes 3-8. Production is unchanged, so the byte-for-byte test and its deltas are untouched.
- **What the owner could weigh:** the stored error reads as a rare slip (about 1 in 25 changes of direction in round 2, 0 in 16 replays). Measuring a line against it would take many samples of states like the food trucks' turn 23, more than this workflow's budget holds. The variant and its judge stay in the eval for that.
- **Spend:** $0.168 of $0.26: 32 turns $0.136, 86 judge records $0.032. The ledger $38.52 of $42; fixes 3-8 have about $1.87 of the workflow's $2.40.
- **Docs and checks:**
  - `.context/text-model-eval.md` gains the stage (commands, variant, arms, budget, the run).
  - `.context/story.md` is unchanged: production is.
  - `outcomeSettledJudge.ts` now exports `paragraphsOf` and `factLines`, which the new judge reads; nothing else in it changed.
  - `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,585, client 256).
  - **Correction (the review of the fixes, 2026-10-01):** commit `0fd6327` says "the variant, cases, judge, prep and budget tests failed first", and "test-first" above says the same; the variant's suite (`recordedResult.test.ts`, 18 tests) was not run before `recordedResult.ts` existed, and its 2 "eval variant" tests (`requestFor("recordedResult", …)`) failed only after the module was written, until the variant was registered in `variants.ts`. The history stays as it is.

## 2026-09-30 (night): fix 3, a sacrifice runs the right way on a stat where more is worse (stage `lever-direction`, $0.176 of $0.26; not adopted)

The coordinator's fix 3 of the review's paid list: a setup's sacrifice costs the player and its reward helps, also on a stat where higher is worse. Its own stage, capped at $0.26 (the $0.20 estimate plus 30%), the reason recorded in `budget.ts`.

- **The cause, in the stored setups and production's request:**
  - The mouse story's setup (round 2) made Cat's Nearness a stat where more is worse (at 70% the next switch must offer hiding) and wrote "Give up 10% Cat's Nearness by making a noisy distraction that draws Marmalade away" as its sacrifice. Bran took it at turn 2: +30 points, and the cat moved away (50% → 40%). New Avalon's Heartwell Feedback was written the same way.
  - It isn't rare in production's stored setups (setup round 3's form and the final check's, 77 setups, read by stat name and then by hand). On 19 stats named for a pressure both levers run backwards, against 7 the right way (the round-2 pirates' Patrol Heat among them). Backwards: Family Pressure and Family Questions (Casablanca, most samples), Storm, Dust and Danger, Pursuit Pressure, Outlaw's Alertness, Eclipse Strain, Corporate Scrutiny, Application Paperwork Load. Right way: Department Strain, Civic Tension, Administrative Scrutiny, Imperial Patrol Heat ("Add 10% Patrol Heat by making a conspicuous move").
  - The request pulls this way in three places:
    - the lever fields say "What the player gives up from this stat" and "What the player gains of this stat";
    - every example is a stat where more is better ('Spend 10% fuel', 'Regain 10% health', the worked example's Public Support and Fervor);
    - the stat rules ask for the premise's "pressures" ("burnout for an activist").
  - Nothing says which way a lever runs on a stat where more is worse, so "give up" lowers it, which is a benefit.
- **The variant** (`leverDirection`, `storyTextRounds/leverDirection.ts`, eval only, test-first). It is production's setup request with two changes and is byte for byte production's elsewhere; a test holds that over every player count, mode, length and the kids budget.
  - One line after "Most player stats can be spent or earned in a scene…": a sacrifice always costs the player and a reward always helps. On a stat where more is worse, the sacrifice raises it and the reward lowers it ('Let the guards' Suspicion rise 10% …'; 'Lower Suspicion 10% by lying low …').
  - The two lever fields' first sentences are reworded the same way: the sacrifice "always leaves the player worse off", the reward "better off", with an example for each kind of stat. The rest of each field stays as production has it.
- **Cases:** six premises. Every built request was read end to end; the variant differs only in the line and the two field sentences.
  - The mouse story's setup is a new round case, `round-setup-kids-mouse` (`leverDirectionCases.ts`, `--build-lever-cases`, no calls). It uses the round's merged premise, age 5, 10 turns, with production's kids setup. A test holds its request to the prompt hash the round sent.
  - New Avalon's frozen premise, whose request is the round's Avalon setup byte for byte (a test holds that too).
  - The bounty hunters, Casablanca and the secret society, where production's stored setups wrote pressures backwards.
  - The ER doctor, where they wrote them the right way.
- **The check:** a new judge, `leversRunRightWay` (`leverDirectionJudge.ts`, `--judge-levers`). It makes one Luna low call per setup and reads every stat with a lever: whose it is, type, values, tooltip, effects, thresholds, changes after chapters, sacrifice and reward.
  - Calibrated on 19 stored setups read by hand before any judge call: 9 no (the round's mouse and New Avalon setups among them), 9 yes (three that raise a pressure as the sacrifice among them), 1 partial.
  - **v1** asked what each lever does to the player, then yes or no. It agreed on 16 of 18 (yes 7 of 9, no 9 of 9), samples 15 of 19: not reliable. It read "Spend 10% Market Buzz by making a bold offer" (a stat where more is better) as helping the player, taking the story action over the stat's move.
  - **v2, its one fix,** asks only which way is better and which way each lever moves its stat, read from the lever's words. The game then works out what the lever does. v2 agreed on 17 of 18 (yes 8 of 9, no 9 of 9), samples 18 of 19: **reliable**. Its miss is a named-step ladder read the wrong way round (Hospital Oversight "toward Formal Review").
  - v1's file is kept as `judged-levers-v1.md|json`.
- **The run:** the dry run planned 24 jobs, est $0.12. The smoke ran the dry run's 2 jobs, then the other 22. Every setup was valid on its first attempt.
- **Readings:**
  - **By hand (all 24 read before the judge ran):**
    - The variant wrote every lever the right way (12 of 12 setups), and it wrote more pressures, each raised as the sacrifice. Examples: "Let Cat Nearness rise 10% by making a quick, noisy move" (both mouse samples), Civic Strain, Team Strain, System Overload, Trail Fatigue, Frontier Heat, Family Questions, Prophecy Panic, Meridian Instability.
    - Production got 11 of 12. Its one backwards setup is the defect premise again, the mouse story's sample 2: "Spend 10% Cat's Curiosity by making a tempting distraction", on a stat that is -10 above 70% and rises after an unfavorable thread.
    - This time production wrote few pressures, and none on the bounty hunters, Casablanca or the secret society.
  - **The judge (v2):** production 9 of 12, the variant 11 of 12, within the noise (17 points).
    - Beside the hand's fail, it failed production's secret-society Public Alarm (read as worse high; that setup's own effects make it better high, so a reader could go either way) and Casablanca's Family Ease ladder. It also failed the variant's New Avalon Crisis Composure ladder (Steady → Overwhelmed).
    - Both ladder fails are v2's known misread.
    - Its labels read levers on stats where more is worse, run the right way, at 6 of 14 → 18 of 18 (moved higher, p < 0.001). That figure is inflated by the ladder misreads. By hand it is 6 of 8 → 18 of 18, inside production's own two-sample noise (50 points).
  - **Automatic checks:** nothing moved either way.
    - `noProgressMeter` fell 91.7% → 75.0%, within the noise. Its "how close" pattern flags a pressure's tooltip ("how close the tabby is to the mice"), and the variant writes more pressures.
    - `distinctIdentities` and `noSlotNames` fell 100% → 91.7%: beyond the noise, not moved (p 0.5).
  - **Waits and cost:** level. p50 53.6 s → 51.2 s, p95 67.0 s → 67.5 s, $0.0056 a setup on both.
- **Verdict: not adopted, and no fix-and-retest.**
  - The variant has nothing on its target to fix.
  - Production erred in 1 of 12 setups by hand this time, against about three in four pressures in its stored setups. The setup-level check can't move at that rate.
  - More samples of the same two arms within the $0.08 left in the cap (about 12 setups) couldn't move it either: 3 of 18 against 0 of 18 would read p 0.11.
  - Production is unchanged, so the byte-for-byte setup test and its deltas are untouched.
- **What the owner could weigh:**
  - The variant costs nothing in waits or money, moved nothing the wrong way, and wrote every lever right, including every pressure it added. Adopting it anyway is your call.
  - If adopted, two production pieces assume a sacrifice lowers its stat and would need a test-first change:
    - `leverPayments.ts` records a sacrifice's payment only as a fall, so a raised pressure's double charge would go unseen;
    - the turn's option types define a sacrifice as giving up some of a stat.
  - Whether turns then apply a rising sacrifice correctly is unmeasured.
  - A decisive measurement would need about six samples a side on the premises where production erred most (the mouse story, Casablanca), about $0.13.
- **Spend:** $0.176 of $0.26: 24 setups $0.134, 100 judge records $0.042 (v1's calibration 38, v2's 38, the stage's 24). The ledger is $38.69 of $42; fixes 4-8 have about $1.69 of the workflow's $2.40.
- **Docs and checks:**
  - `.context/text-model-eval.md` gains the stage (commands, variant, arms, budget, the run).
  - `.context/story.md` is unchanged: production is.
  - Test-first: the variant's, cases', judge's and prep's suites could not load until their modules existed, and v2's five reading tests failed before v2 was written; `armsBudget.test.ts` carries the new cap. `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,624, client 256).

## 2026-10-01: fix 4, parallel threads in one world and contests with both sides (stage `parallel-threads`, $0.147 of $0.195; the switch planner's line adopted, the rest not)

The coordinator's fix 4 of the review's paid list: parallel threads keep people and places consistent across players; a converted contest is worded as one side's challenge; a contest's last stage keeps both sides present. Its own stage, capped at $0.195 (the $0.15 estimate plus 30%), the reason recorded in `budget.ts`.

- **The cause, in the stored stories and production's requests:**
  - **The space pirates' third chapter (turns 10-14).** The switch at turn 9 gave each player three topic directions, one of them "take the Wayward Comet toward the Gloam Reach's pylons". The pilot took it, the captain chose the Needlepoint dockhands, the scout the galley-table claim. The chapter planner gave each thread its own place: the captain at Needlepoint's docks, the pilot flying the ship through the pylons, the scout at the galley table "before the ship leaves Needlepoint". The group turn (one call for all three) followed the plan: in the same turns the captain stands on the docks in his text, speaks from the bridge channel in the pilot's and stands at the galley table in the scout's.
  - **The converted contest.** Only the scout picked the treasure claim, at its last stage (1 of 2). The planner wrote a contest with "the captain's camp represented by its stated claim"; the plan check's repair made it her challenge; its steps still asked how "Pip and the captain's camp" argued, and its unfavorable result, "Bex's captain's camp secures the agreed captain's extraordinary share", settled the claim for a player who wasn't there.
  - **The estate agents' fourth chapter (turns 13-14).** The switch at turn 12 offered the sale's last stage (2 of 3) as one direction among others: to Nia as "Join Rory at a neighborhood open house". Rory chose the archive. The chapter planner followed the picks: Nia alone at the open house, its unfavorable result "Mara signs an offer through Rory". At turn 14 the buyer is at the archive in Rory's text and at the open house in Nia's, Rory beside her.
  - **In the requests:** the switch planner's coordination examples teach "opt-in grouping" and nothing keeps a contested outcome's decisive stage from being one side's direction; the chapter planner must follow the picks and is told nothing about where parallel threads happen or what a contest one side alone chose becomes; the group turn's consistency lines cover only players "in the same thread or switch".
- **The variant** (`parallelThreads`, `storyTextRounds/parallelThreads.ts`, eval only, test-first; production's requests byte for byte elsewhere, a test holds it on every frozen switch, chapter and turn case):
  - the switch planner, where a contested shared outcome's next thread settles its last stage (a contest game after the opening): offer it only as a grouped thread, a flavor switch on it for every player, never as one direction among others;
  - the chapter planner, where the picks set several outcomes: parallel threads happen at the same time in one world, each person, crew and vehicle in one place, a thread that moves a shared place moves it for everyone, a player only in their own thread, each plan saying where it happens and who is there; and where only some players picked a contested outcome: a one-sided thread is that side's challenge, the other side neither in its scenes nor winning or losing in its results;
  - the group turn on a chapter step with several threads: the threads happen at the same moment, everyone in one place across the beats, a player only in their own thread's beats, a shared place in one state.
- **Cases** (`parallelThreadsCases.ts`, `--build-parallel-cases`, no calls): from the second round's stored runs, replayed, each only where its request is the one the run sent. Three switch plans before a contest's last stage (space pirates 9, estate agents 12, food trucks 16, where production had offered the contest as a direction; `choiceResultCases` gained the switch role) and three chapter openings run as chains, each side's chapter planner into its own group turn (space pirates 10 and estate agents 13, the defect; food trucks 13, ordinary). The built requests were read end to end (the space pirates' chapter planner and the estate agents' switch planner in full, the others at their insertions).
- **The checks:**
  - **A new judge, `placesConsistent`** (`parallelThreadsJudge.ts`, `--judge-parallel`), one Luna low call per group turn: who is in which thread and every player's text; it lists everyone in more than one text and where each puts them. Calibrated on 19 group chapter steps read by hand before any judge call: 9 yes (ordinary parallel chapters of all three group stories), 5 no (space pirates 10-13, estate agents 14), 4 constructed failing versions (a player shown in another's scene while their own text has them elsewhere), 1 partial (space pirates 23, two threads at one galley table, neither seeing the other).
    - **v1** also failed a player shown in another thread's scene: 15 of 18 (yes 6 of 9, no 9 of 9), samples 19 of 19, not reliable. Its false fails were the space pirates' galley at turns 6 and 8 (two threads at one table, every text agreeing) and the food trucks' turn 21 (each owner's own crew read as one group in two places). Its file is kept as `judged-parallel-v1.md|json`.
    - **v2, its one fix,** asks about places only: separate scenes may share a place, and a same-named group is one only where the texts make it so. 18 of 18 (9 and 9), samples 18 of 19: **reliable**.
  - **Read by the game, no calls:** how each switch plan offers a contest at its last stage (grouped, not offered, a direction among others, a flavor switch for some players); on each chain's plan as the plan check keeps it, the absent players a one-sided contest thread names, and a contest's last stage settled by one side alone (the review's proposed plan check).
- **The run** (2026-09-30, 22:20-22:34 UTC): dry run 12 switch jobs (est $0.02) and 12 chains (est $0.07); the smokes' 2 and 2 as counted, then 10 and 10; every call valid on its first attempt.
- **Readings:**
  - **The switch plans (the game's reading, checked by hand):** the contest's last stage offered as a direction one side could take alone: production 5 of 6, the variant 1 of 6 (grouped 4, not offered 1). Grouped or not offered: 1 of 6 → 5 of 6, **moved higher (p 0.040, noise 33 points)**. The automatic checks: fewer switches per plan (2.17 → 1.33, the grouping itself), `directionsDistinctOutcomes` 3 of 5 → 0 of 2 topic switches, within the noise (the food trucks' Jo had one open outcome besides the contract, so his directions repeat it). Faster (p50 10.6 → 8.5 s), $0.0016 → $0.0015 a plan.
  - **The chapter openings, by hand before the judge:** production 3 of 6 consistent, the variant 5 of 6.
    - Production's failures: the space pirates' sample 2 (the captain on the docks, in the command chair on the bridge intercom and at the galley table); its sample 1 (the captain and the dockhands at the galley table while the scout argues at the same table, its slate clean in one text and written on in the other); the estate agents' sample 2 (Rory in the archive and at the open house).
    - The variant's failure: the space pirates' sample 1, whose own plans put the Comet at its berth in the scout's thread and in the pylons in the pilot's, with "Bex and Pip remain aboard".
    - **The judge (v2):** production 4 of 6, the variant 5 of 6, within the noise (production's own two samples differ by 67 points; it passed production's two gatherings at one table, which the hand fails).
  - **The one-sided contest (the game's readings):**
    - A one-sided thread on a contested outcome named the absent side's player in every plan that had one: production 0 of 3, the variant 0 of 4 ("Mara signs an offer through Rory", "Bex's recognized claim stands").
    - A contest's last stage settled by one side alone: production 3 of 6, the variant 2 of 6 (within the noise).
    - Production once pulled Rory into the sale's decisive contest by overriding his pick (the estate agents' sample 1: "the chosen sale outcome requires both agents in the contest"). The one-sided line talked the variant out of that every time. **The line backfired.**
  - **Automatic checks on the chains:** nothing moved the wrong way (`newElements` moved lower, 0.83 → 0.00). One-paragraph first replies: production 2 of 6 turns, the variant 1 of 6 (the game's retry would ask again). Chapter openings p95: two players 55.0 → 56.1 s, three players 72.1 → 75.8 s (both arms over 60 s there); $0.0046 → $0.0047 a call.
- **Verdict:**
  - **Adopted: the switch planner's line** (`CONTEST_LAST_STAGE_LINE` and `contestAtLastStage` in `SwitchPromptService.ts`, test-first). It passes the stop rule on its target, moves nothing the wrong way beyond the noise, and is no slower. It is the cause-level fix for "a contest's last stage keeps both sides present": the chapter planner can only follow split picks, so the decisive stage has to be grouped at the switch.
    - The kept tests were updated deliberately: `adoptedPlanners.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts` read production's switch planner as planner v2b with production's threads that fit plus the measured line (`withContestLastStage` in `adoptedDeltas.ts`, not a delta). A new test holds production byte for byte to the variant's switch request, and production's constant to the variant's text.
    - The variant now takes the line out of production's request before inserting its own, so it builds as measured. The stage's switch cases rebuild against production as the round sent it (`playthroughs2Sent`).
    - Its cost: fewer topic switches at such a switch (the players lose that switch's choice of outcome, and keep their approach), and where the contest is held back a player with one other open outcome gets repeated directions.
  - **Not adopted: the chapter planner's two lines and the group turn's line.**
    - The parallel line is within the noise on the reliable judge (4 → 5 of 6; by hand 3 → 5 of 6).
    - The one-sided line backfired on its own reading, and with the switch line adopted, split picks no longer reach a contest's last stage.
  - **No fix-and-retest.** The cap left about $0.05, six chains at most. At production's 67-point noise on the judge, no retest of that size could move, not even 6 of 6. The variant's lines stay in the eval.
- **What the owner could weigh:**
  - The switch line takes away the players' choice of outcome at the switch before a contest's decisive stage: every player gets a flavor switch on it, or it waits. That carries out "two-sided competition only". It can be undone, test-first.
  - The review's plan check, which would reject a one-sided last stage and retry, isn't built. Given split picks, the retry can only satisfy it by overriding a player's pick, since `threadsKept` would undo a double listing. The switch line keeps the split from arising.
  - The space pirates' split has a second upstream cause the stage didn't measure: a single player's direction that moves the shared ship ("take the Comet toward the pylons") while the others' keep them at the station. A switch-level rule for directions that move a shared place is the next thing to measure if that recurs.
- **Spend:** $0.147 of $0.195: 12 switch plans $0.019, 12 chains $0.086, 100 judge records $0.042 (v1 50, v2 50). The ledger is $38.84 of $42; fixes 5-8 have about $1.55 of the workflow's $2.40.
- **Docs and checks:**
  - `.context/text-model-eval.md` gains the stage (commands, variant, arms, budget, the run) and the adoption (the kept tests, the switch planner).
  - `.context/story.md` gains the switch planner's line under "Contests have two sides".
  - `jobPlan.ts` interleaves a stage's pipeline chains the way `stageInterleavesArms` interleaves its arms (only this stage has both).
  - Test-first: the variant's, cases', judge's and prep's suites could not load until their modules existed; the v2 test failed before v2 was written; the kept planner tests failed before the production line existed. `armsBudget.test.ts` carries the new cap. `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,675, client 256).

## 2026-10-01: fix 5, challenge and contest results that tell how the attempt turns out (stage `challenge-results`, $0.137 of $0.195; adopted)

The coordinator's fix 5 of the review's paid list: challenge and contest results describe how the attempt turns out, not the player's approach (planner v2f's rule; only 13 of the second round's 33 chapter plans passed `resultsFitKind`). Its own stage, capped at $0.195 (the $0.15 estimate plus 30%), the reason recorded in `budget.ts`.

- **The cause, in the stored plans and production's requests (read with each plan's stored prompt, `prompts/<hash>.txt`):**
  - **After a flavor switch the results restate the switch's approach.** 14 of the 16 chapters planned after a flavor switch failed `resultsFitKind`, against 6 of the 17 after a topic switch. Each failure after a flavor switch writes the approach the players picked at the switch into its step results and milestones: the space pirates' turn 19 picks "have each person name one risk", "map a safe sequence for venting", "mark a clear, grit-free access lane" came back as "Bex gets each crew member to name a risk their work must avoid, Jori marks the drive-idle points in a clear venting sequence, and Pip lays out a grit-free tool lane" (turn 2: "Bex coordinates the work while Jori confirms the right fittings and Pip pinpoints the heat pattern"); the estate agents' turn 2 "separate documented defects from what no one can verify" / "distinguish what records establish from what remains rumor" as "Rory's careful separation of documented defects" / "Nia's distinction between records and rumor" (turns 9, 16, 20, 23 the same way; turn 20's "Nia keeps the resident's signed note out of the sales summary" is her pick word for word); food trucks 9 and 24, lemonade 2, the mouse story's 9 likewise. After a topic switch the failures name the approach a direction or a step's question carries ("hear how the delayed queue affected nearby workers" as "Suri listens to the waiting workers and adapts").
  - **In the request:** PLAYER DECISIONS tells the chapter planner, for a flavor pick, "The choice sets the approach, not the outcome.", beside the reminder that the thread must continue "based on the players' choices", and nothing says the results don't restate it; planner v2f's rule has one weak example, a different decision ("Rikkit bribes the guard instead"), not the chosen approach carried out well; and the challenge and contest milestone fields ask for "an event that happened, naming who did what".
  - **Where it carried into play:** a rolled result that tells the approach makes the next turn narrate it whatever the player picked; in the estate agents' story every result fixed both rivals' approach as careful honesty (the report's "rivals who play alike"). New Avalon 22 → 23 is a different slip: the step's favorable result (the Compact's consent-based bypass) was an outcome, and the player's option argued for the opposite; that is the turn's options against a result, not a result naming the approach, and this stage doesn't touch it.
- **The variant** (`resultsAsOutcomes`, `storyTextRounds/resultsAsOutcomes.ts`, eval only until adopted; production's request byte for byte elsewhere, a test held it on the tests' stories and every frozen chapter case): after the results rule's challenge sentence, the approach chosen at the switch and the one a step's question names are where a thread starts, and no result restates them, not even as the way the player succeeds (a group's: or why a side comes out ahead), with a weak and a good pair; the flavor pick's line reworded the same way; the challenge and contest milestone fields' "naming who did what" going on "what was won or lost, or what others did in answer, never how the players went about it". My calls: the fix at all three places the request pulls toward the approach, measured together; the rule's existing weak/good pair kept; the examples in the prompt's worked threads untouched.
- **Cases** (`challengeResultsCases.ts`, `--build-challenge-cases`, no calls): 15 chapter plans of the second round, replayed, each only where its request is the one the run sent. Eleven where the stored plan failed (nine after a flavor switch; one player, two players, three players; challenges and contests) and four that passed (New Avalon 21, the plan behind turn 22; the mouse story's 2; food trucks 21; the space pirates' 6). My call on size: 15 cases ×2 per arm kept the stage inside its cap with room for a retest (all 33 ×2 would have been about $0.29).
- **The check:** the choice-result stage's calibrated `resultsFitKind` (reliable there, 16 of 17, samples 19 of 20), reused, not recalibrated. Before any call of this stage I read the round's 33 stored plans by hand on the same standard (`ROUND2_RESULTS_HAND`: 10 yes, 20 no, 3 partial) and the judge's stored readings agree on 30 of 30 decided plans (the partial ones it passed). Beside it a pooled share of the judge's own labels (challenge and contest results labelled the player's choice), and the automatic plan checks.
- **The requests, read end to end:** production's estate agents' turn 2 request (the stored prompt, identical to today's build) and the variant's lemonade turn 2 request in full; the variant against production on the estate agents' turn 2 as a diff (the rule sentence, the two flavor lines, six milestone descriptions, nothing else).
- **The run** (2026-10-01): dry run 60 jobs (est $0.11); the smoke's 2 as counted (the estate agents' turn 2: production again wrote "Rory's careful distinctions reassure Mara", "Rory earns Mara's stronger confidence by clearly separating documented defects"; the variant "Mara treats Rory's account as the more reliable starting point", "Mara trusts Rory's account more"; the judge agreed on both), then 58; every plan valid on its first attempt.
- **Readings:**
  - **By hand (all 60, before the full judge run):** production 8 of 30, the variant 23 of 30. The variant's misses are mild: lemonade 2 in both samples (the budgeting milestones still name Theo recording his expenses and setting a reserve), the estate agents' 16 in both ("The neighbor accepts Nia's distinction"), 23 once, food trucks 9 once, the space pirates' 6 once.
  - **The judge:** 8 of 30 → 22 of 30, **moved higher (p < 0.001, noise 13 points)**; groups 7/20 → 17/20 (p 0.002), one player 1/10 → 5/10 (p 0.070), after a flavor switch 5/22 → 16/22 (p 0.001), after a topic switch 3/8 → 6/8 (beyond the noise, not moved). Results labelled the player's choice 76 of 261 → 18 of 237 (moved lower). Per plan the judge agrees with my reading on 21 of 30 in each arm, stricter or more lenient in both directions: the direction and size match the hand.
  - **Kind:** the variant planned the mouse story's 9 (Pip's trust, a three-path outcome) as exploration in both samples, where production and the stored run planned a challenge; production once and the variant twice planned food trucks 6's Tavi outcome (favorable/mixed/unfavorable) as exploration. `kindFollowsOutcome` 90.0% → 93.3%. I read this as noise around a real kind confusion on those two outcomes, not as the variant dodging its rule.
  - **Automatic checks:** nothing moved either way; beyond the noise, not moved, `noRepairs` 100% → 93.3% (code repaired a written duration twice and a repeated last step once) and `milestoneKindConcrete` 100% → 96.7%.
  - **Waits and cost:** p50 8.7 → 9.7 s, p95 15.6 → 15.4 s (timeout 30 s); reasoning +32%, visible −9%; $0.0018 a plan both.
  - **Not measured:** the judged stage check (the cap's remainder, and the variant changes no stage wording; the heuristic `stepsWithinNamedStage` rose 31% → 62%); what the turns write from the new results (a chapter-into-turn chain run would show it).
- **Verdict: adopted** (passes the stop rule on its target, nothing moved the wrong way, waits level). No fix-and-retest.
  - **Production** (test-first; the kept tests failed first, 9 failures, then passed): `ThreadPromptService.ts` prints `STEP_RESULTS_APPROACH` after the results rule's challenge sentence and `FLAVOR_APPROACH_LINE` in a flavor pick's PLAYER DECISIONS line; `plannerReplies.ts` builds the challenge milestones per player count (`challengeMilestones`) and words the contest milestones as measured. Production is the variant byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema.
  - **The kept tests, changed on purpose:** `adoptedPlanners.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts` read production's chapter planner as planner v2f with the measured edits (`withResultsAsOutcomes`, `withResultsAsOutcomesSchema` in `adoptedDeltas.ts`, not deltas); a new test holds production byte for byte to the variant and production's constants to its text. The variant takes production's copies out before inserting its own (`measuredBase`), so it builds as measured; its tests read planner v2f as the measured base (its single-player topic story now names its directions' outcomes, since production's fallback outcome, which planner v2f lacks, would otherwise differ).
  - **The second round's stored chapter plans** now rebuild as planner v2f (`playthroughs2Sent`); the fake-run tests pass `productionSends`, and a new assertion holds that today's chapter planner is not what that round sent.
  - **What a later paid run needs:** `adopted12`'s production chapter records are the planner before this adoption, so a paid run of production's own chapter planner takes a new prompt-state tag.
- **What the owner could weigh:** the variant's contest results read more alike ("Mara gives Rory's view more weight" / "… Nia's view"), since the side's manner no longer tells them apart; the story's texts now carry that difference. The budgeting milestones of a learning story still name the bookkeeping (lemonade 2 in both samples); fix 7 (money and counts) is the place for that.
- **Spend:** $0.137 of $0.195: 60 plans $0.110, 60 judge records $0.027. The ledger is $38.98 of $42; fixes 6-8 have about $1.41 of the workflow's $2.40.
- **Docs and checks:**
  - `.context/text-model-eval.md` gains the stage (commands, variant, arms, budget, the run) and the adoption; `.context/story.md` the planner's new wording.
  - `armsBudget.test.ts`: the old invariant (every feedback stage's cap fits the $42 from the $31.99 when they opened) failed with a fifth review cap (42.175), since the review's stages spent less than their caps; it now reads the stages before the review from $31.99 and the review's from the ledger when it opened (`LEDGER_WHEN_REVIEW_OPENED`, $37.99), each with the stalled $1.3 on top.
  - Test-first: the variant's registration test, the cases', prep's and arms' suites failed before their code existed (the variant module was written beside its first test). `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,713, client 256).

## 2026-10-01: fix 6, read-with-kids turns shorter and simpler for the child's age (stage `kids-turns`, $0.072 of $0.195; adopted for a single player)

The coordinator's fix 6 of the review's paid list: read-with-kids turns shorter and simpler, for the reading age the setup states. Its own stage, capped at $0.195 (the $0.15 estimate plus 30%), the reason recorded in `budget.ts`.

- **The cause, in the stored mouse story and production's request:**
  - **What the child heard:** every turn of the second round's mouse story (read with a five-year-old, 10 turns) ran about 300 words in five or six paragraphs of 15-22-word sentences (Flesch-Kincaid grade 6-9 on production's replays), the same as a grown-up story's, with words above the age ("a faint, irregular tapping", "still have to be reckoned with", "mingling", "tremor") and grown-up options ("Compare the floor-level draft with the marks on the crumb map, using your passage knowledge to trace where the trail bends").
  - **In the request:** nothing in a turn says a child is listening. Production's kids setup (`KIDS_STATS`) changes the stats only; the story's guidelines carry a gentle tone, not a length or a vocabulary; the premise, the one place the child's age is stated ("How old is the child?: 5", the client's merged form), never reaches a turn, and the story did not record it; and every turn is told three times to write "5-6 paragraphs with 3-5 sentences each" (the context, the shouted repeat "Otherwise, there simply isn't enough text…", and the text field's description with its own repeat).
- **The variant** (`kidsTurn`, `storyTextRounds/kidsTurn.ts`; production's request byte for byte on every other story, a test holds it on the tests' stories and every frozen turn case): on a read-with-kids story, the context's count as "3-4 short paragraphs of 2-3 short sentences"; a block after the last-paragraph rules for "a child aged <age>" ("a young child" where none is recorded): 3-4 short paragraphs of 2-3 sentences, about 80 to 140 words; sentences of about 5 to 12 words, one thing happening in each; everyday words, no grown-up or abstract words or figures of speech, a new word explained in its sentence; short, lively speech; the options and interludes the same (a lever still naming its stat and amount); the shouted repeat and the text field's count and repeat in the same terms; and a retry of a one-paragraph reply asking for the short count (the request's `shortTextCount`, which the eval's checked retry sends; `beatReplyProblem` takes it as `textCount`). The age: the story's `readingAge` (new, optional, in core), read from the premise by `readingAgeFromPremise` (only an age or a range, since it goes into every turn). My calls: one length for every age (the age keys the vocabulary; a 10-14-year-old gets the same short turns, which the owner may want longer); the planners left alone (their directions become the topic options and their milestones the switch turn's, so "gardenward" and "awkward" still reach the child); the case words not quoted in the prompt.
- **Cases** (`kidsTurnCases.ts`, `--build-kids-cases`, no calls): six of the mouse story's turns, replayed, each only where its request is the one the run sent: the first turn, a challenge chapter's opening and step (its stored first reply one paragraph, retried), the switch turn after it (retried too), an exploration step, the ending; each recorded as the game now records a read-with-kids story (the category, which the run recorded too, and the age "5", which it did not). Beside them the frozen first turn of a template tagged Kids (`first-tpl-54a4e23b-p1`, no age, images on): "a few ordinary ones" are otherwise every non-kids turn, where the variant is production byte for byte, so no call measures anything there. No other stored story is read with a child.
- **The check:** deterministic (`kidsReadability.ts`, no judge calls): words, paragraphs, sentences, words per sentence, syllables per word (a vowel-group count), long words (3+ syllables, capitalised words out), the Flesch-Kincaid grade, and "reads for a young child" (`KIDS_LIMITS`, set before the run: at most 160 words, 12 words a sentence, grade 4, from the variant's own ask and read-aloud picture books' grade 2-4), with the options' and interludes' mean words; the stop rule on means (`meanMove`) and the pass rate (`rateMove`), production's two samples as the noise. Not calibrated as a judge is: it counts, and it misses an idiom of short words ("reckoned with"), so I read all 28 kept replies by hand for vocabulary and continuity.
- **The requests, read end to end:** the variant's mouse step 3 request in full; production against the variant as a diff on steps 3, the ending and the template (the context line, the block, the repeat, the text field's count and repeat; nothing else).
- **The run** (2026-10-01): dry run 28 jobs (est $0.09, plus up to 28 retries); the smoke's 2 as counted (step 3: production 298 words, grade 6.7; the variant 99 words, grade 4.1, the chosen paper rustle and its result in the first paragraph), then 26; every reply valid on its first attempt; production's check retried 1 of its 14 first replies (one paragraph), the variant's none.
- **Readings (`kids-turns.md`):**
  - **Length and plainness of the replies kept:** words 317 → 102, paragraphs 5.2 → 3.9, words per sentence 18.7 → 12.2, syllables per word 1.34 → 1.27, long words 4% → 2%, Flesch-Kincaid grade 7.5 → 4.2, words per interlude 20 → 14, every one **moved lower**; words per option 16.6 → 12.6 beyond the noise, not moved. Reads for a young child 0 of 14 → 5 of 14, **moved higher (p 0.020)**. The variant's nine misses sit just past the limits (words per sentence 12.6-15.2, or grade 4.2-5.3); none on length (71-131 words). The first replies read the same.
  - **By hand (all 28):** the variant's turns follow on as production's do: the chosen action and its result in the first paragraph (steps 3 and 7, both samples), the mixed milestone told at the switch turn, all three outcomes at the ending, the exploration options in their results' order, the topic options carrying their directions. Words above a five-year-old are few ("notch", "hem", "crinkle", "smudged", "parchment" on the template, and the planner's "gardenward" and "awkward" carried into the text and the topic options) against production's "irregular", "reckoned with", "mingling", "delicate", "tremor". Third-person texts (a single player's turn is told as "you"): production 3 of 14, the variant 1 of 14.
  - **Automatic checks:** `paragraphs` and `sentences` (production's 5-6 of 3-5) moved lower, by design; `youParagraphShare` 11% → 32% moved higher, which I read as the variant keeping to the second person the turn asks for, not a defect; `anomalyWords` 1.64 → 0.36 moved lower; nothing else moved (`chapterSacrifices` 1.00 → 0.75 and `leverShare` 50% → 25% within reach of the noise, not moved, on 4 lever turns).
  - **Waits and cost:** level or shorter on every turn kind, all within the allowance (first turn p95 29.6 s → 19.4 s, production's with its retry; chapter step 29.7 → 27.3 s; opening 35.1 → 34.9 s); $0.0027 → $0.0025 a turn; reasoning +34%, visible −23%.
- **Verdict: adopted for a single player** (the target moved, nothing that matters moved the wrong way, waits level). No fix-and-retest: the stop rule passed on the target; the misses are at the limits, not a direction the variant got wrong.
  - **Why a single player only:** the stage measured single-player turns (Luna medium); a group's turn (Luna low) with the same lines is unmeasured, and no stored group story is read with a child. A group's read-with-kids turn keeps production's count. Measuring it would need a built group kids case (a setup and its first turns), about $0.03-0.05.
  - **Production** (test-first; the kept tests and the new suites failed first, 3 failing tests, then passed): `kidsTurnRules.ts` (`takesKidsRules`, `kidsListener`, `KIDS_CONTEXT`, `kidsRules`, `kidsRepeat`, `kidsFieldCount`, `KIDS_TEXT_COUNT`, `beatSchemaForKids`, `beatCheckOptions`); `BeatPromptService` prints the context line, block and repeat; `beatStep.request` edits the text field after the option rules; `AIStoryGenerator` and the playthrough harness pass `beatCheckOptions` to `checkedBeatReply`; `StoryCreationService` records `readingAge` from a read-with-kids premise; core gains `StoryState.readingAge`, `Story.getReadingAge`, `readingAgeFromPremise` and `KID_AGE_LABEL` (the client's form field now uses it, so the label cannot drift from the parser); `beatChecks.ts` takes the retry's count (`FULL_TEXT_COUNT` by default, byte for byte the old message). Production is the variant byte for byte on a single player's kids turn, prompt and JSON schema.
  - **The kept tests, changed on purpose:** `adoptedTurns.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts` read a single player's kids turn as `kidsTurn` (no delta on top, since it is built on production's turn as measured), and a group's as before; production's constants equal the variant's text. The older stages' frozen-case tests compare against production as it stood then (no turn read the category before): `choiceResult.test.ts` leaves kids turns out, `turnOptionsContinuity.test.ts` and `switchReminder.test.ts` strip the category for "production then", and the options tests read only cases the options stages could plan (`stagePlansCase`; two of the mouse cases are rolled steps). The second round's stored turns rebuild as the turn before (`playthroughs2Sent` now maps a turn to `choiceResultRequest`), and `recordedResultCases` and `outcomeSettledCases` read it too: the mouse story recorded its category, so production's turn on its replayed states now takes the kids rules.
  - **What a later paid run needs:** `adopted13`'s production records on the kids cases are the turn before, so a paid run of production's own kids turn takes a new tag. The eval's `storyFromSetup` records the age as the game does, so a new playthrough of the mouse story plays the kids turn.
- **What the owner could weigh:** one length for every reading age (a 12-year-old gets the same 80-140 words); the planners still write grown-up directions and milestones the switch turns carry into the child's text; groups unchanged.
- **Spend:** $0.072 of $0.195: 28 turns and production's one retry, no judge calls. The ledger is $39.05 of $42; fixes 7-8 have about $1.34 of the workflow's $2.40.
- **Docs and checks:**
  - `.context/text-model-eval.md` gains the stage (commands, variant, arms, budget, the run) and the adoption; `.context/story.md` the kids turn.

## 2026-10-01: fix 7, money and counts that add up in a learning story (stage `money-adds-up`, $0.158 of $0.195; not adopted)

The coordinator's fix 7 of the review's paid list: money and counts that add up in learning stories (the lemonade ledger). Its own stage, capped at $0.195 (the $0.15 estimate plus 30%), the reason recorded in `budget.ts`.

- **The cause, in the stored lemonade story (round 2) and production's request (the stored prompt of turn 3, hash `204025…`, identical to today's build):**
  - **What the ledger did:** turn 2 quoted citrus, sugar and cups (3 + 2 + 1 coins) and paid nothing. Turn 3 paid "five coins for the extra fruit" (the chosen sacrifice) and "one more coin for paper sleeves", sold cups ("leave a coin on the cart", "the cashbox fills with a handful of mixed coins"), and its stat changes took only the sacrifice's five (10 → 5). The switch turn 4 raised Profit Margin 25% → 30% with no sum in the text; its plan says "Add 5 percentage points to Profit Margin", the setup's own adjustment ("+5 percentage points after a favorable thread centered on careful pricing or expense control"). The ending counts "the four coins still there" and "your recorded 30% profit margin" after a season and a fair with no sales told.
  - **In the request:** the stat-changes section names only the sacrifice's and reward's payments; within a chapter it allows only "minor" changes to stats that can be adjusted anytime; on a switch turn it says "some meaningful stat changes might be warranted" and sends the turn to each stat's "Adjustments after threads". Nothing ties the money the text pays and earns to the stat that counts it, or a worked-out figure to the sum behind it, and the stat changes are written before the text.
  - **The setup pulled the same way:** Eli's instructions ("purchases affect the Cashbox only when the thread resolves"), the cashbox's after-chapter rule (add the chapter's "stated net profit", which no text stated), and the margin defined with fixed steps (after a favorable chapter, and as a sacrifice and a reward).
  - The first round's lemonade setup had no money stat at all (dollars in the text, a Shared Supply Reserve count), so it shows the same gap differently: its switch turn 5 cut the reserve 3 → 2 by the stat's after-chapter rule with nothing in the text using a unit.
- **The variant** (`moneyAddsUp`, `storyTextRounds/moneyAddsUp.ts`, eval only, test-first; production's request byte for byte on every other turn, a test holds it on the tests' stories and every frozen turn case): on a learning story (`learn-something`, the setup form's category) that keeps a counted (number) stat, after the first turn, one block at the end of the stat-changes section: every amount of a counted stat the text pays, spends, uses up, sells or earns moves that stat by exactly that amount in this reply's stat changes, never put off, decided before the text and nothing else paid or earned; a quote, estimate or plan moves nothing; the chosen sacrifice or reward is paid once; a stat worked out from other figures moves only to the value the text works out, with the sum (or by a chosen lever), never for how a thread went, whatever its adjustments after threads say; no stated total differs from the stat; the changes are made in full, and a counted stat that changes only at a thread's end is paid and earned only then. My calls: learning stories only, as the brief says (a story of another category with money is unchanged); every player count (the stage measures one); the setup left alone (the cause is also there, but a setup change is its own measurement).
- **Cases** (`moneyAddsUpCases.ts`, `--build-money-cases`, no calls): six turns of the round's lemonade story, the one stored learning story that counts money, replayed, each only where its request is the one the run sent, recorded with the `learn-something` category (the eval's `storyFromSetup` records none; production's turn reads none, so the request is still the run's). Where the ledger broke: 3, 4, 11 (the ending); beside them turns where money was only quoted (2, 6) or paid and counted (8). The built requests were read end to end (the variant's switch turn 4 in full; production against the variant as a diff on all six: the block only; production's turn 3 byte for byte the stored prompt).
- **The check:** a new judge, `figuresAddUp` (`moneyAddsUpJudge.ts`, `--judge-money`), one Luna low call per player's turn: the number and percentage stats with before, the turn's changes and after as the game applies them, the option chosen last, the text, interludes and facts. Calibrated on 28 items read by hand before any judge call: the round's lemonade turns 1-11, the mouse story's crumb wedge (turn 10), three of round 1's lemonade turns, six constructed versions of stored lemonade turns (a total off by one, a fee paid and a sale made with no change, a change the text doesn't show, a margin moved with no sum; one that adds up), and seven of the run's own replies, read after the run and before the judge (14 yes, 11 no, 3 partial). **Not reliable:** 21 of 25 (yes 10 of 14, no 11 of 11), samples 28 of 28. Its four false fails read a payment recalled from an earlier turn ("your one coin is paid", an interlude's "Two gone for the fee") as this turn's, so it fails every ending. **Its one fix was not run:** the cap had about $0.035 left after the retest, and a v2 calibration estimates about $0.042 (the retest went first, as the procedure's next step). The verdict rests on the hand reading of all 32 replies; the judge's readings are indicative only.
- **The run** (2026-10-01, 01:02-01:24 UTC): dry run 24 jobs, est $0.08; the smoke's 2 as counted (turn 3: production sold "three cups ... four coins of sales" into the cashbox, and bought sugar, with only the fruit's five charged; the variant added the first cup's two coins, 10 → 7, and kept the basics a quote), then 22. The variant's switch turn 4 sample 1 ran to its output cap twice before a valid third attempt (150.5 s).
- **Readings:**
  - **By hand:** production 8 of 12 add up, the variant 9 of 12, within the noise. Turn 3: production 0 of 2 (coins "click into the cashbox" uncounted), the variant 1 of 2 (sample 2 "marks each coin received" with no amount, no change). Turn 4: 0 of 2 both, the margin 25 → 30 every time. Turns 2, 6, 8 and 11: 2 of 2 both.
  - **The judge (indicative):** production 6 of 12, the variant 8 of 12, beyond the noise, not moved.
  - **Automatic checks:** nothing moved. Beyond the noise, not moved: the variant's endings wrote options (`noOptionsOnEnding` 2 of 2 → 0 of 2) and one-paragraph replies 3 of 12 against 1 of 12 (turn 6 both samples, turn 4 sample 2; production's retry would ask again in play).
  - **Waits and cost:** the variant's switch turn p95 150.5 s against production's 28.4 s (the run to the output cap), $0.0030 → $0.0042 a call (+39%); otherwise level.
- **The one fix-and-retest** (`moneyAddsUpB`, test-first: the variant's tests for B failed first, 6 failures): every amount the text pays or earns named ("two coins", never a handful), and the worked-out stat's exception placed right after the thread-resolution line that sends a switch turn or the ending to "the 'Adjustments after threads' parameter". ×2 on turns 3 and 4, ×1 on the other four; the dry run's 8 jobs as counted, all valid first time, waits within every allowance (p95 43.7 s), $0.0032 a call.
  - **By hand:** 6 of 8 (production 4 of 8 on the same pairs, within the noise). Turn 3 2 of 2, but by keeping the step's first sales out of the text ("The first order is still a question, not a sale"), though the step asked how Theo records sales. Turn 4 0 of 2: the margin 25 → 30 again in both, and sample 2 re-applied the turn before's narrated payments: the fruit's five coins (the sacrifice already paid), the sleeve and a coin, 5 → 0. The game's `leverChargedAgain` repair missed it: the sacrifice's option names "your cashbox", not "Stand Cashbox", so no payment was recorded on the beat.
  - **The judge (indicative):** 5 of 8 against production's 3 of 8, beyond the noise, not moved.
- **Verdict: not adopted.** Neither form moved its target under the stop rule. The margin, the defect's most visible half, moved by the setup's own step in all six switch turns of every arm: a turn line, in either place, loses to the stat's definition. The amounts line helped on the chapter step (production 0 of 4 against the variant 1 of 2 and B 2 of 2) but B got there partly by leaving sales out, and B's switch turn charged a paid sacrifice again. Production is unchanged, so the byte-for-byte tests and their deltas are untouched.
- **What the owner could weigh:**
  - The cause that held is in the setup: a learning setup defines a worked-out figure (the margin) as a stat with fixed steps (after a favorable chapter, as a sacrifice, as a reward), and a counted stat whose purchases a story element defers. A setup rule for learning stories (a worked-out figure gets no adjustments after threads and no levers, or isn't a stat at all; purchases are paid when the text pays them) is the next thing to measure, a setup stage with turns after it (about $0.10-0.15).
  - A no-call fix: `leverStatOf` could read a lever's stat by a distinctive word of its name ("cashbox" for Stand Cashbox), so `leverChargedAgain` sees such a payment. It would have dropped B's second charge; production never made that charge in the run.
  - The judge's one fix (a payment recalled from an earlier turn, or a thought weighing a cost, is no payment of this turn) is written down but not run, about $0.042.
- **Spend:** $0.158 of $0.195: 32 turns and 2 attempts cut at the output cap $0.113, 81 judge records $0.045. The ledger is $39.21 of $42; fix 8 has about $1.18 of the workflow's $2.40.
