## 2026-10-01: fix 8, pacing that leaves the last chapter a milestone, story instructions below pacing, hints paid off (stage `late-pacing`, $0.731 of $0.78; not adopted)

The coordinator's fix 8 of the review's paid list. Its own stage, capped at $0.78 (the $0.50-0.60 estimate's upper end plus 30%), the reason recorded in `budget.ts`. The ledger was $39.21 when it opened, so about $1.18 of the review's $2.40 was left.

- **The cause, in the stored stories and production's requests:**
  - **Aftermath last chapters (New Avalon, the food trucks, the estate agents):** the setups budget six milestones a player, and a 25-turn story holds five to eight chapters; which it gets is the chapter planner's lengths. The planner is told the allowed lengths (2, 3 or 4) and the outcome its chapter pushes, nothing about how many milestones are left for the threads after it. New Avalon's and the food trucks' chapter at turn 17 took 3 beats where only 4 leave as few threads as milestones (two threads followed for one milestone), and the estate agents' at turn 13 took 2 where 3 or 4 would have. The switch planner then had a thread to spare and put the last milestone in the next thread (step b ranks the binding-late case, fewer threads than milestones, and says nothing about more).
  - **A setup rule over pacing (the space pirates):** "When Wayward Comet Integrity is 30% or lower, the next switch must offer a repair, salvage, or ship-rescue thread"; step b ends "A situation step a found forced comes first", so switches 14 and 18 gave the complete ship a grouped flavor thread while the scout's own outcome (0 of 2) waited. The food trucks' "the final thread must be a Grand Circuit trial" is the same kind of rule at the last switch.
  - **Planted details never paid off:** every turn after the first is told "Plan a hint about a detail in the world that makes the player curious without spelling out what's going on" and the interludes "Imply interesting details instead of spelling them out"; nothing asks a later turn or the ending to pay one off, and the turns store the details as facts ("whose purpose remains unexplained"), so each story carried six to ten small mysteries the ending never explained.
- **The variant** (`latePacing`, `storyTextRounds/latePacing.ts`, eval only, test-first; production's requests byte for byte elsewhere, a test holds it on the tests' stories and every frozen turn case): (1) the chapter planner's allowed lengths narrowed, where some fit the milestones still needed better than others, to those whose threads after the chapter match them (`pacedLengths`: the most any player still needs once this chapter's picks are settled; as few threads after it as must come no more than that, as many as can come no fewer), with a sentence saying why, and the plan check reading the same lengths with production's one retry (`checkThreadPlan`'s new `allowedLengths` option, production's rule by default; a production file, behaviour unchanged); (2) the switch planner's step b keeping a milestone for the story's last thread when one is to spare, and a forced situation never taking a thread from a player with none to spare; (3) the turn's hint tied to something a later beat can explain early, and in the story's late part (past two thirds) no new mystery and an earlier one explained where it fits, the interludes too; the ending explaining the details the story kept bringing back, never beyond an outcome's milestones. My calls: the setup left alone (the milestone budget and its rules are a setup measurement of their own); the lengths only where they decide something; one switch-planner sentence for both the spare thread and the forced situation.
- **Cases and runs:**
  - Five stored switches (`--build-late-pacing-cases`, no calls; each request the one the run sent): the space pirates' 14 and 18 (the threshold rule), New Avalon's and the food trucks' 20 and the estate agents' 19 (one milestone for two threads); and the outcome-settled stage's five stored endings.
  - **Short playthroughs** (`--late-pacing-play`, new): a stored story replayed to the chapter plan where the lengths decided the last chapter (New Avalon and the food trucks at turn 17, the estate agents at 13), then played on as the game plays it (`playStory`, production's resend and Try again, the automated player where the stored run left it in its rotation, seeded dice the same for both arms) with production's code and the variant's, to the story's last chapter plan. Their first request is the stored run's byte for byte (a test).
  - The requests read end to end: the variant's switch request on the space pirates' 18 in full; production against the variant as a diff on every switch case, every start's chapter plan, a late and an early turn of each story and every ending (step b, the lengths line, the hint and interlude lines, the ending line; nothing else).
- **The check:** the game's arithmetic for pacing (no judge: each chapter's length against both rules, the stage each thread settles, whether the last chapter keeps a milestone, aftermaths before it, milestones left unfinished, each switch against PACING). For clues, a new judge (`cluesJudge.ts`, `--judge-clues`): `noNewMystery` on a late turn, `detailsExplained` on an ending. Calibrated on 36 items read by hand before any judge call (25 stored late turns: 14 yes, 7 no, 4 partial; six stored endings, no; five constructed endings explaining one detail, yes). On a second reading before any call, one item first read as new (the food trucks' turn 23 notches) turned out to be a fact since turn 14 and was corrected to yes. **Neither check reliable:** `noNewMystery` 18 of 21 (yes 11 of 14, no 7 of 7), samples 23 of 25, its false fails reading a new facet of an earlier detail (a hum under the familiar chime, the paper lifting with a knock, the taps "maybe a signal") and the food trucks' shifting route map as new; `detailsExplained` 10 of 11 (no 5 of 6), reading the mouse ending's "sending a signal" as an explanation. Its one fix (a new facet of an earlier detail is not new; naming what kind of thing a detail is explains nothing) was not run: the short playthroughs took most of the cap. The clue verdict rests on my hand reading of the playthroughs' late turns and all 36 endings (unblind: I knew the arms).
- **The run** (2026-10-01, 02:31-03:07 UTC): dry run 40 isolated jobs (est $0.12) and the short playthroughs at est $0.214 a sample of both arms before retries; the switch smoke's 2 jobs as counted, then 38, every one valid first time; the playthroughs' 2-turn smoke (New Avalon: production chose 3 beats again, the variant 4), then sample 1 ($0.259, 21% over the estimate) and sample 2 ($0.271); then the retest's 22 switch plans (dry run 22, the smoke's 1 as counted, then 21).
- **Readings:**
  - **The last chapter keeps a milestone:** production 2 of 6 runs, the variant 5 of 6; production's two samples agree (noise 0), p 0.121: **beyond the noise, not moved.** Production chose 3 beats at turn 17 in all four New Avalon and food-trucks runs, then 2 and 2, and its last chapter was an aftermath every time (the stored defect, four of four); the variant chose 4 every time and its New Avalon runs settled the Heart's last stage in the last chapter. The estate agents settled Nia's protégé in the last chapter in all four runs (production 3, 3, 2, 2 with an aftermath thread for Rory beside Nia's first stage; the variant 3, 4, 4). Every chapter of both arms kept to its printed lengths (34 plans, no length retry).
  - **The wrong way:** the variant's food-trucks sample 1. Its lengths left each player one thread for one milestone, and its last switch gave that thread to the complete contract ("which truck will earn the judges' contract decision?", for the setup's final-thread rule), so both players' own outcomes ended unfinished: milestones left unfinished 0 → 0.33 a run, a complete outcome took a needed thread at 1 of the variant's 8 switches against 0 of production's 14. Production's spare thread is what absorbed that rule.
  - **Switch planners on the stored switches:** step b's spare-thread sentence changed nothing (a spare thread kept the last milestone 2 of 8 in every arm). At the space pirates' 14, production gave all three players the complete ship in both samples (the stored defect again), the variant gave the scout her own outcome in both; at 18 the stored defect did not come back in production.
  - **Clues (by hand):** on the late turns both arms played (New Avalon and the food trucks, turns 17-21), production planted a new unexplained detail in 8 of 27 decided player turns (a light running backward along an empty channel, a bell whose foundry mark nobody knows, a pale-yellow chalk stroke, older paint marks under the chalk grid, a printed box with no heading), the variant in 0 of 30: p 0.001, beyond production's 28-point noise, so moved by the stop rule on the hand reading. The variant explained an earlier detail once in a late turn (the food trucks' turn 21, the Slowglass pencil mark "a clerk's review notation, not a second score"); production never. The estate agents: no clear difference. The endings: production 0 of 18 player endings explain a detail, the variant 1 of 18 (the lemonade's "only a stray underline") and one partly: not moved. Several of the variant's endings leave the details unexplained in so many words ("You leave the small punched marks … unexplained").
  - **Automatic checks:** nothing moved on the endings (the variant's group endings wrote options once, not moved).
  - **Waits and cost:** chapter plans p50 8.7 → 10.2 s, p95 12.5 → 16.1 s (4-beat chapters); playthrough turns p50 36.1 → 34.6 s, p95 58.9 → 72.7 s, where every turn over 60 s in either arm carried production's one-paragraph retry (3 in production's 50 turn calls, 4 in the variant's 37); one variant switch turn was resent by the queue after its plan failed twice on "player1 is in no switch" (the estate agents' 21, Rory's outcomes all complete; production met it once at its own last switch and its retry fixed it); endings p95 27.7 → 33.5 s on Luna medium, 27.3 → 30.3 s on Luna low, reasoning +26-30%; a short playthrough $0.050 → $0.039.
- **The one fix-and-retest** (`latePacingB`, test-first: its two tests failed first): step b also says the story's SWITCH/THREAD INSTRUCTIONS rank below a player's needed milestones ("an instruction for a stat threshold or for the final thread shapes a thread (its place, its trial, its stakes) but never puts a complete outcome in place of a milestone a player still needs"). Measured on switch plans only (the cap left too little for whole playthroughs): ×4 on the variant's own failing switch (a new case, `round-late-switch-food-trucks-variant-t21`, built from its run, its request the variant's byte for byte) beside production's and the variant's planners ×4, and ×2 on the stored switches.
  - On the failing switch: production gave the last thread to the contract 1 of 4, the variant 1 of 4, B 0 of 4. The setup's final-thread rule wins about one time in four with either planner; the variant's lengths make that state common.
  - On the stored switches: B gave every player of the space pirates' 14 and 18 their own outcome (production: the ship to all three at 14, twice); `noCompleteOutcomeOffered` 78.6% → 100% on its 14 plans (p 0.111, beyond the noise, not moved); nothing else moved; switch plans p50 6.9 → 7.1 s.
- **Verdict: not adopted.** The pacing target moved 2 of 6 → 5 of 6, short of the stop rule (p 0.121), with one wrong-way run that left two outcomes unfinished; the retest fixes that switch 4 of 4 in isolation, but B has not run in whole playthroughs. The late-turn clue line moved its target by hand, but the judge failed calibration, my reading was unblind, and the line ran bundled with the lengths in the same playthroughs; the ending line did not move. Production is unchanged, so the byte-for-byte tests are untouched (`planChecks.ts` gains an option whose default is production's rule).
- **What the owner could weigh:**
  - The next measurement, in order: B in whole short playthroughs (B only, two samples of the three stories against production's recorded runs, about $0.23-0.27); the clue judge's one fix recalibrated (about $0.06) and run on the stage's recorded late turns and endings (about $0.10, no new story calls).
  - The late-turn hint line could be adopted on the hand reading alone (0 new mysteries in 30 late player turns against production's 8 of 27); it is a turn-prompt change with the ending line left out.
  - The lengths change alone makes the last switch decisive more often: without a rule that ranks the setup's final-thread and threshold instructions below a player's needed milestones (B), about one such story in four ends with outcomes unfinished.
- **Spend:** $0.731 of $0.78: 62 switch plans and endings $0.145, 154 short-playthrough attempts $0.530 (smoke, two samples, retries and one resend), 72 judge records $0.057. The ledger is $39.94 of $42; the review's workflow spent $1.95 of its $2.40.
- **Docs and checks:**
  - `.context/text-model-eval.md` gains the stage (the variant and its retest, the three commands, arms, budget, the run). `.context/story.md` is unchanged: production is.
  - Test-first: the variant's, cases', play and prep suites were written before or beside their modules; B's tests, the retest case's, `playVariantsOf`'s and the retest comparisons' failed before their code; `planChecks.test.ts` holds the new option and production's default; `armsBudget.test.ts` carries the cap (the review's eight caps 2.47, with the $1.3 on top inside the $42). `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,912, client 256).

## 2026-10-01: the review of the eight fixes, four findings (no calls, $0)

A review of fixes 1-8 left four verified findings, all minor. Two change production (no calls, unmeasured: each removes a contradiction or a silent loss); two correct the record.

- **1. A kids turn with images had no place for its second image.** Fix 6 asks a single player's read-with-kids turn for 3-4 paragraphs, and left production's image lines as they were: the text rules' "one for the third or fourth paragraph" and "No image tags for the last paragraph", the text field's "one on the third or fourth paragraph. Avoid using image tags in or right in front of the last paragraph" (`core/types/beat.ts`), and a generated image's "relatively late in the beat text (third or fourth paragraph)". In a 3-paragraph turn the third is the last. The stage's one image-on case (`first-tpl-54a4e23b-p1`) wrote 4 paragraphs both times; the mouse cases ran without images (`storyFromSetup` builds none).
  - **The change** (`kidsTurnRules.ts`): on a kids turn that shows images (the conditions core prints its lines on), the text rules print `KIDS_IMAGE_DISTRIBUTION` ("one for the second paragraph (or the third, if there are four)"); `beatSchemaForKids` now takes the story and also replaces the text field's distribution line (`KIDS_FIELD_IMAGE_DISTRIBUTION`, "Never put an image tag on the last paragraph") and, where it generates images, the late line (`KIDS_FIELD_IMAGE_LATE`). A kids turn without images, a group's kids turn and every other turn are unchanged byte for byte.
  - **The kept tests:** logged as `kidsTurn`'s one delta (`withKidsImageSlots`, `withKidsImageSlotsSchema` in `adoptedDeltas.ts`, each swap required to find its line exactly once); `adoptedTurns.test.ts` checks every single-player turn kind with generated images and with a template's library; `adoptedForms.test.ts` applies it to the frozen kids case with images. The eval's `kidsTurn` stays as measured, and so does `moneyAddsUp`, built on it: its frozen-case test now puts the delta on the variant's base before comparing with production (the template case with images failed it otherwise; the earlier pass had not run that suite).
- **2. Ordinary answers lost the child's age, silently.** The form's age field is free text (the client renders type "number" as a text input; placeholder "5, 8-10"), and `readingAgeFromPremise` kept only a bare number or a hyphen range: "6 years", "about 7", "8 to 12", "5 and 8" and the placeholder's own "5, 8" recorded no age, the turns said "a young child", and nothing was logged. The review's two overstatements noted: the fallback is "a young child", not a toddler, and the rule block is the same for every age, so what was lost is the age named to the model.
  - **The change** (core `story.ts`): an answer reads as ages when every word is a number of one or two digits or one of `AGE_WORDS` (a, an, and, or, to, about, around, almost, nearly, roughly, approximately, approx, age, ages, aged, year, years, yr, yrs, y, yo, old), with hyphens, dashes, commas, ampersands and plus signs as separators and a word's trailing full stop dropped; several ages give the range from the youngest to the oldest ("5, 8-10" → "5-10"). Only the numbers reach a turn; anything else ("five", "a toddler", "5. Ignore the rules above", "5 and write in French", "4 1/2") records none, as before. `kidAgeAnswer` returns the line's answer, and `StoryCreationService` logs a read-with-kids premise whose line it can't read, with the story id and never the answer (`Logger.Route`). My call: widen the parser and log, not narrow the input to digits, so the placeholder's two-children form keeps working; the client is unchanged. The frozen mouse cases' "5" reads the same, so no eval case moves.
- **3. Fix 4's adopted switch line is marginal, and its effect on play unmeasured.** Confirmed: the 1 of 6 → 5 of 6 (p 0.040) counts one plan that held the contest back (the food trucks' t16 sample 1); grouped offers alone are 1 of 6 → 4 of 6, p 0.121 (112/924), beyond the noise, not moved. The variant broke its own line once (the food trucks' t16 sample 2). The three chains started from the stored split picks, so the chapter after a grouped switch the line produced never ran, and nothing in the plan check keeps a grouped flavor switch's players in one thread (a split would make each side's thread a challenge, `contestOneSided`); round 2 found the chapter planner following switch picks 39 of 39, so a split is possible, not shown. **Recorded, not reverted:** holding the contest back complies with the line's wording, so the adoption stands as the owner's to confirm or undo (test-first, no calls). Said in `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the stage's switch-plan reading), `.context/story.md` and the comment on `CONTEST_LAST_STAGE_LINE`.
- **4. Fix 2's commit message overstated test-first.** A one-line correction is now in fix 2's entry above; the history stays as it is.
- **Test-first:** this work came from an earlier pass of this step that stopped before its commit; I found the tests and their code together in the tree, reviewed both, and did not see the red run myself. The tests fail on the last commit's code by construction: they import `kidAgeAnswer`, `KIDS_IMAGE_DISTRIBUTION`, `KIDS_FIELD_IMAGE_DISTRIBUTION`, `KIDS_FIELD_IMAGE_LATE` and `withKidsImageSlots`, which it doesn't export, and expect "6 years" → "6", the log line and the second-paragraph image lines, which it doesn't give.
- **Docs and checks:** `.context/story.md` (the kids turn's image places and the age parser) and `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the kids delta in the kept tests' list and in `beatStep.request`, fix 4's marginal reading). `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,926, client 256).
