## 2026-10-01: the owner's decisions on the fixes, and the $45 hard cap (no calls, $0)

- **The owner's decisions (2026-10-01), verbatim where quoted:**
  - Ending milestones: "The idea was -not- for the engine to invent missing milestones for open outcomes. Unfinished outcomes should be narrated as unfinished. Only what was played."
  - Group challenges on one player's own outcome: "Yes, only count the owner's roll."
  - "do all the fixes and the measurements needed for them." "Stories saved before the fixes can be ignored." "Few bucks don't matter. I just want to make sure that we stay frugal and only run what's needed." "No need to be more frugal than before."
  - Rewards: "14 reward options in 32 choice sets is a bit too much." "At most one reward is good. Several sacrifices can sometimes make sense, but should have a strong justification starting at the second one." Options should use different stats, not differ only in risk.
  - Kids: "this should depend on the age range that should be part of kids stories settings."
  - The coordinator's calls: adopt fix 3's lever-direction line; retest fix 8's pacing and clue lines inside short whole-story runs; another attempt at the reasoning-runaway turn's cause.
- **The hard cap: $45** (`HARD_CEILING`, also the default global cap). The ledger read $39.94 of $42, so $2.06 was left, or about $0.76 if the stalled Stage 4 calls of 26 September were billed: too little for the measurements the decisions need. The reason is in `budget.ts`'s header and the constant's comment, the dry run's total line ("raised from $42 by the owner on 2026-10-01 for the fixes of that day and the measurements they need ("few bucks don't matter"), …") and `.context/text-model-eval.md`. `--global-cap` still only lowers it. Every paid run still gets its own stage, cap and reason, a smoke first and a dry-run count to compare.
- **Tests (test-first; 3 failed first, then the two older cap checks that still read $42 were moved to $45 and all passed):** `armsBudget.test.ts` (the $45 ceiling, lowering to $42, the global-cap check), `dryRun.test.ts` (the total line names $45 and why), `resultsReport.test.ts` (the total row's cap).

## 2026-10-01: only what was played gets a milestone, and the ending tells an unfinished outcome as unfinished (no calls, $0)

- **The owner's decision:** "The idea was -not- for the engine to invent missing milestones for open outcomes. Unfinished outcomes should be narrated as unfinished. Only what was played." It reverses TR-4's keep rule (the play fixes of 2026-09-27: "Milestones on known outcomes that no ended thread pushed are kept (B8 handles endings later)"), which the second playthroughs' review held for the owner's OK.
- **Every code path that adds a milestone, read:** the reply's `newMilestones` through `repairBeatReply` (group, unknown-id mapping, TR-5's planned milestone for an ended thread the reply left without one) and `ChangeService`; `ThreadResolutionService` sets only each ended thread's own milestone; `endingStates.ts` counts one per resolved thread. Only the reply could add a milestone to an outcome no chapter pushed, and the repair kept it.
- **The repair** (`beatRepairs.ts`, `onlyPlayed`, repair `milestoneNotPlayed`): each outcome keeps as many of the reply's milestones, the first written, as threads of the chapter that just ended pushed it; no other outcome gets one, unfinished or complete; a turn that ends no chapter keeps none (its schema allows none; this covers a reply that writes one anyway). At a switch and at the ending alike: "only what was played" reads the same at both, and the switch prompt already asks only for the ended threads' milestones. TR-5's safety net still adds an ended thread's planned milestone where the reply wrote none, so the chapter that just ended is always recorded.
- **The ending's prompt** (`BeatPromptService.ts`): after the milestone example on the ending only, `ENDING_MILESTONES_PLAYED` ("Only the threads that just ended get milestones: one for each thread, on that thread's outcome. Add none to any other outcome, unfinished or complete: every other outcome ends with the milestones play gave it."); the unfinished line of `ENDING_OUTCOME_KINDS` now reads "is told as unfinished, in its current state, … and the story ends with it still open", today's current-state rule word for word between. `ENDING_OUTCOME_RULE`'s "counting the milestones this beat adds" stays: the standing lines it introduces already count one per resolved thread. The switch turn's prompt and the reply schema are unchanged (both already ask for the ended threads' milestones only; the schema is core's, shared with the frozen forms, so a change there would move every measured base).
- **What play now does differently:** the estate agents' ending of the second round, replayed, keeps the last chapter's milestone and drops its four others on complete outcomes (Rory's reputation and principle, Nia's protégé and principle), so no outcome ends above its intended count from the ending; nothing else in either round's stored runs is dropped (`playthroughReplay.test.ts`). Every ending is asked to tell an unfinished outcome as unfinished. Unmeasured: a settled decision, no calls.
- **Kept tests, changed on purpose:** `adoptedDeltas.ts` logs the ending's lines as a delta on every ending, a kids ending too (`withEndingOnlyPlayed`, in `adoptedTurn`; `adoptedTurns.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts` apply it to `endingStateB` and `kidsTurn`), with its inverse for the variants measured before it (`beforeEndingOnlyPlayed`, `productionThen`: `choiceResult`, `kidsTurn`, `turnOptionsContinuity`, `switchReminder`, `recordedResult`, `moneyAddsUp` and `endingState` tests compare their endings with production as it stood then). The round-2 replay test reads today's ending as the sent one once the delta is taken out. TR-4's "keeps a milestone on a known outcome that no ended chapter pushed" test is replaced by the drops.
- **Test-first:** the new repair tests (6), the adopted-turn test and the adopted forms' frozen beat cases failed first (7 failures, the adoptedTurns suite unable to load `ENDING_MILESTONES_PLAYED`), then passed; the variant suites' ending comparisons failed after the change (20 failures) and were moved to `productionThen` on purpose; the replay reading was pinned on the stored runs (its first expectation's ids were wrong, its count right).
- **A paid run of production's own ending** takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted2`'s and later ending records are the ending before).
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (the milestones repair, the ending), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the kept tests' delta, `beatStep.request`). `.context/ai-transparency.md` and `Privacy.tsx`: nothing to change.

## 2026-10-01: only the owner's roll decides a group challenge or contest step on their own outcome (no calls, $0)

- **The owner's decision:** "Yes, only count the owner's roll." (the second playthroughs' review, estate agents chapter 6).
- **How rolls were pooled** (`ThreadResolutionService`): before each thread beat `resolveCurrentThreads` writes each thread's result for the step just played from the players' own beat results (each player's roll against their chosen option, `BeatResolutionService`). A challenge counted favorable, mixed and unfavorable over every player on side A and took an extreme result with the chance |favorable − unfavorable| / players (favorable when the favorable rolls lead), else mixed; a contest added side A's favorable and side B's unfavorable rolls against the others (a tug of war). The thread's last step result becomes its resolution and milestone. An exploration step has been the owner's choice since 2026-09-30; rolled steps weren't.
- **The change** (`rollingSides`): on one player's own outcome, with the owner in the thread and their step resolved, only the owner's roll is counted, on the owner's side. A challenge's step is then the owner's roll; a contest's goes the owner's side's way on a favorable roll, the other side's on an unfavorable one, mixed on mixed. The pooled computation is unchanged (it reads only the rolls it is given), so a single player, always the owner of their outcomes, gets the same result as before. A shared outcome, a player's own outcome its owner isn't in, and an owner whose step has no result keep pooling.
- **What play now does differently:** in a group, a challenge or contest step on one player's own outcome follows that player's dice alone; another player's roll no longer helps or hurts it. The estate agents' turn 21, replayed: Nia's unfavorable roll now decides her protégé chapter (unfavorable, its unfavorable milestone) where the pooled rolls gave mixed. From turn 22 that replay is production's story, not the one played, so the round-2 rebuild test now stops there (121 of 126 turns), as round 1's does at the owner rule for exploration steps.
- **Test-first:** `ThreadResolutionService.test.ts` gained six tests (owner's roll in a challenge, whatever the dice; pooling kept on a shared outcome and without the owner; a contest on an owner's outcome for either side and a camp; a shared contest's tug of war; an owner's step with no result; the milestone at the last step); three failed before the change, then all passed (one reworded after the change: it had also asserted that no dice were drawn, but the pooled computation draws even when one roll is counted; it now asserts the owner's result under the lowest and highest draw, which the old code fails too). `playthroughReplay.test.ts` reads the replayed turn 21 and stops the rebuild before turn 22. The harness's group-step reading still flags exploration steps only (its comment now says rolled steps follow the owner too).
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (thread steps), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the round-2 reading). Prompts unchanged: no turn or planner text says how rolls combine.

## 2026-10-01: a contest in a cooperative story becomes the group's shared challenge (no calls, $0)

- **The gap:** a template can hold a contested shared outcome in a cooperative World (the editor offers the Contest kind only where contests play, and only warns on one that's already there). The chapter planner writes that outcome's chapter as a contest with players on both sides; the plan check (PL-5) refused it ("only competitive and cooperative-competitive games have contests"), the one retry wrote the same, and the turn failed for good. PL-12 already turned a one-sided contest into that side's challenge, in a cooperative group whoever is in it; a two-sided one stayed a problem (its test said so).
- **The repair** (`oneSidedAsChallenge` in `planChecks.ts`, repair `contestInCooperative`): in a group whose mode plays no contest, a thread whose results are all contest results and that has players on both sides becomes the group's shared challenge the way a one-sided contest does: every player in it on side A (side A's first, then side B's), the stored kind "challenge", and the results turned into favorable / mixed / unfavorable with the side player1 was on winning as favorable (side A where player1 is elsewhere, as PL-11 reads side A). My call on which side is favorable: the convention the plan check, the setup and the scoreboard already use (player1's side is side A); a cooperative group has no camps, so any fixed reading is arbitrary, and this one keeps the milestone texts as written.
- **Left as they were:** a contest in a competitive or cooperative-competitive game; a cooperative thread with players on side B but challenge or exploration results (a different planner error, still a problem with its retry); a single player's contest results.
- **What play now does differently:** a cooperative story holding a contested outcome plays its chapter as a shared challenge on the first reply instead of failing the turn after one retry; the converted thread's steps keep the contest's wording (as PL-12's do, unmeasured).
- **Test-first:** `planChecks.test.ts` gained a "contest in a cooperative story" block (both sides to a shared challenge with player1's side favorable; player1 on side B and elsewhere; the first reply used, no retry; contests kept in contest games and a side-B challenge still a problem; a single player's still a problem); the one-sided block's "both sides filled stays a problem there" line and PL-5's "finds a contest in a cooperative game a problem" were changed on purpose. 4 failed before the change, then all passed. `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,945, client 256).
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (PL-12).

## 2026-10-01: fix 3's lever-direction setup adopted (the coordinator's call; no calls, $0)

- **The call:** adopt fix 3's measured variant (`leverDirection`: every lever the right way by hand in 12 of 12 setups against production's 11 of 12, the reliable judge 9 → 11 of 12 within the noise, nothing worse, waits and cost level) though its target never moved under the stop rule: production erred too rarely on the stage's states to measure, and the defect is one the playthroughs showed (the mouse story's "Give up 10% Cat's Nearness", a second benefit beside the sacrifice's +30; 19 of 26 pressures backwards in production's stored setups).
- **Production** (test-first): `LEVER_DIRECTION_LINE` after the spend-or-earn rule in `statsThatAct` (`prompts/setupPromptText.ts`), and the lever fields' first sentences `SACRIFICE_FIRST_SENTENCE` ("What the player pays in this stat … It always leaves the player worse off …") and `REWARD_FIRST_SENTENCE` ("What the player gets in this stat … better off …") in `setupSchema.ts`. A custom story's setup is the measured variant byte for byte, prompt and schema. The template editor's AI Draft and AI Iteration print the same stat rules and fields (my call: one form for every setup, so a template can't keep writing levers backwards; unmeasured there).
- **The two code pieces that assumed a sacrifice lowers its stat** (the fix's own entry above):
  - `leverPayments.ts` now reads a lever's direction from its words (`leverDirection`: the chosen option's text, else the stat's own lever rule for that kind, each only when its words say one way: "rise", "raise", "add", "more", "+10" against "lower", "drop", "spend", "lose", "burn", "−15"; else a sacrifice down and a reward up). `paidLevers` records the change that moves the stat that way, so `Beat.paidLever` and `leverChargedAgain` cover a rising sacrifice and a falling reward. Every lever the stored runs paid reads as before (the replay tests, unchanged, still drop exactly round 2's two double charges).
  - The turn's option types and stat-change lines ("should lose whatever was sacrificed", "a sacrifice (losing a stat …)", core's "Sacrificed stats are always lost") are turn prompts: unchanged, since a turn change needs a measurement. The turn sees the stat's own lever rule and the option's words ("Let Suspicion rise 10% …"); whether it pays a rising sacrifice the right way is unmeasured, and the next playthroughs' lever readings will show it.
  - The eval's lever readings (`ratingMechanics.ts` `leverStatus`, which the rating pages and the playthroughs' "levers paid" read) use the same direction, so a rising sacrifice paid as a rise reads "applied", not "the other way".
- **Kept tests, changed on purpose:** `adoptedDeltas.ts` gains `withLeverDirection` and `withLeverDirectionSchema` (not deltas: the measured variant's line and fields on setup round 3's measured form, wherever a setup, template or iteration request carries them), read by `adoptedSetup.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts`; `adoptedSetup.test.ts` holds a custom story's setup byte for byte to `leverDirectionRequest` and production's constants to the variant's text. The variant now takes production's copies out first (`measuredBase`), so it builds as measured (`leverDirection.test.ts` reads production before the adoption as its base); the stage's case test hashes production as it stood before (`leverDirectionCases.test.ts`).
- **Test-first:** 22 failures (the adopted-setup suite unable to load the new constants, the adopted forms' setup, iteration, template and chain cases, the variant's 14 base tests, three `paidLevers` direction tests, the rating reading), then all passed; the test of the old default (no direction words) passed before and after.
- **A paid run of production's own setup** takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted10`'s production setup records are the setup before).
- `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,958, client 256). Docs: `.context/story.md` (the lever record), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the stage's adoption).

## 2026-10-01: a lever's direction read only from words about its stat (review finding; no calls, $0)

- **The finding (verified, important):** `leverDirection` read the chosen option's whole sentence, and a direction word on one list only won over the stat's own lever rule. An option describes an action, so its other words ("Expend an additional 10% of your Personal Agency … greatly increasing disruption"; "Put 20 resource credits toward … stipends so more delegates can stay") turned an ordinary stat's sacrifice into a rise. A correct -10 then wasn't recorded (`paidLevers` needs the step's sign to match), so `leverChargedAgain` had nothing to compare and the next turn's second charge was kept, the very double charge the repair exists for; and the eval's `leverStatus` read the correct payment as "changed the other way", mixed into the signal the next playthroughs are to read (does a turn pay a rising sacrifice the right way). Before a754b58 these read by their kind and were right, so this was a regression the adoption's log line ("every lever the stored runs paid reads as before") missed: it held only for the playthroughs' chosen levers.
- **The change** (`saidOfStat` in `leverPayments.ts`; the order stays the chosen option's words, else the stat's lever rule's, else a sacrifice down and a reward up): a word counts only when it speaks of the stat, within two words of its name (or a word of the name of four letters or more, "stability" for Timeline Stability, which rules use) or one of an unsigned amount. A word that can tell what happens to the stat itself counts on either side ("let Suspicion rise", "a 10% increase in", "increasing your Stress Level"); one that acts on it or compares an amount of it ("spend", "gain", "add", "lower", "use", "paid", "more", "less") only before it, since after it it speaks of something else ("10% harmony to gain a boost", "credits … so more delegates"). A signed amount counts only right beside the name ("(+10% Suspicion)", "+20% in Family Pressure"), so a rule's "+30 bonus" says nothing. A direction word inside the stat's own name doesn't count ("Rising Panic"). My calls: the option still goes first (a rule written backwards before the adoption shouldn't turn a rise the option says), and the rule is read the same bound way, since its purpose clauses ("to gain a boost", "a paid errand") misread just as often.
- **Measured on what's stored, no calls** (temporary Jest probes over the DOCS outputs and both playthrough rounds, deleted after): of the 769 distinct sacrifice and reward options on percentage and number stats, the whole reading turned 11 from their kind's way, 5 wrongly (Personal Agency twice, Resource Credits, Media Savvy's "a lower chance of success", Timeline Stability by its rule's "+30 bonus"); the new reading turns exactly the 6 right ones (Stress Level ×5, Hunger's "Let your Hunger rise by 10%") and no other. Read alone, the 3,483 distinct stored stat rules: 111 wrong directions dropped, 4 right ones added, 6 right ones the whole reading had only by accident lost ("Can accept a temporary spike (+20%) for a one-time bonus" with no name beside it, "an added interruption", "for a +20 bonus" on Outlaw Alertness and on Stress, "escalate city tension", "a slower, less direct lead" on Corporate Scrutiny), one ambiguous. The setups adopted that day write the bound form ("Let Suspicion rise 10% …", "Lower Suspicion 10% …").
- **Test-first:** a new `leverPayments.test.ts` (the five stored misreadings, a rule's purpose clause and paid errand, the readings kept on Stress, Hunger, "Suspicion to rise", a signed amount beside the name and a direction word inside the name, the rule where the option is silent, the option before a backwards rule, the kind's default), `storyTextSteps.test.ts` (the blackout option's Personal Agency 60 → 50 recorded as paid) and `ratingMechanics.test.ts` (the delegates option's Resource Credits 60 → 40 read "applied"): 10 failed before the change, then all passed; no kept test changed.
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (the lever record), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the stage's adoption, with the stored counts).

## 2026-10-01: the read-with-kids setting takes an age or an age range, recorded with the story; Kids templates get it too (no calls, $0)

- **The owner's decision:** "this should depend on the age range that should be part of kids stories settings." The turn rules and the stat budget by band are their own measured stage (kids-ages, below); this entry is the setting itself.
- **What was there:** the setup form's read-with-kids field was configured as type "number" with the placeholder "5, 8-10" (rendered as a text input), merged into the premise as "How old is the child?: …", and the server read the child's age back out of that premise line (`readingAgeFromPremise`) into `StoryState.readingAge`, a string. Templates had no age at all ("a young child").
- **The setting** (core `story.ts`): `KidAges` `{ min, max }`, whole ages from 2 to 14 (`KID_AGES_MIN`, `KID_AGES_MAX`); `parseKidAges` takes one age or a range ("5", "8-10", "8 - 10", "8–10", "8 to 10") and nothing else, the youngest first; `kidAgesFrom` reads a stored or requested value back (a valid pair or nothing); `kidAgesText` writes "5" or "8-10"; `kidAgesFromPremise` reads a premise's line through `readingAgeFromPremise`; `KID_AGES_HINT` is the form's message. `KidsBand` and `kidsBandOf` (the youngest child's band: up to 5, 6-8, 9 and older) are for the stage below. `StoryState.kidAges` is the record; `Story.getKidAges()` reads it, else a story's older `readingAge` (stories saved between the kids-turns stage and this, and the frozen eval cases), and `getReadingAge()` now names the ages from it, so every turn reads the same text it did ("a child aged 5").
- **The setup form** (`StoryInitializer`): the field is type "age": a text input with placeholder "5 or 8-10"; a value `parseKidAges` can't read shows `KID_AGES_HINT` under it (`aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`) and keeps Create Story (and Draft World) disabled; an empty field stays optional, as before. The request carries `kidAges`, and the premise line carries the ages as the setting writes them ("8-10"), which the setup reads (`readKidAgesField`, `client/src/shared/utils/kidAgesField.ts`). Every read-with-kids suggestion fills the field with ages it reads (a test).
- **The server:** the route keeps only a valid pair (`kidAgesFrom`); `StoryCreationService` records `kidAges` on a read-with-kids story, the request's setting, else (a request without it: an older client) the premise line's ages; an answer it can't read is still logged with the story id only. `readingAge` is no longer written.
- **Kids templates:** `StoryTemplate.kidAges` (or null, which clears it on save). The template editor's Meta tab shows a "Children's ages" field while the template carries the Kids tag (`BasicInfoTab`, `KidAgesField`, through `useBasicInfoTab`'s `handleKidAgesChange`), with the same parsing and hint. `TemplateService` keeps only a valid pair on create and on update (an update that carries the key replaces it, null or an invalid value removes it, one that leaves it out keeps it); `createStoryStateFromTemplate` gives a story from a template tagged Kids the template's ages. My call: the AI Draft and AI Iteration are unchanged (template generation knows no category, so no kids budget).
- **Also:** `templateValidation.ts`'s four "a player slot is an object" checks now exclude null (`typeof null === "object"`; the template's new nullable field made the test type check fail on them).
- **Test-first:** the core setting's suites (`readingAge.test.ts`, which could not load), `StoryCreationService.setupStatus.test.ts` (the setting recorded, over the premise, none on another category, the premise fallback), `StoryStateFactory.test.ts`, `TemplateService.backgroundValues.test.ts`, `kidsTurnRules.test.ts` (the listener from the setting), `playthroughs.test.ts` (the eval's `storyFromSetup` records the setting as the game does, an input's own setting first); client: `kidAgesField.test.ts` (could not load), `StoryInitializer.test.tsx` (the range shown, the hint and the disabled button for "five", the empty field optional), `BasicInfoTab.test.tsx` (the field on a Kids template only). 10 server tests failed first and the core suite could not load; 6 client tests failed and the field's suite could not load; then all passed. `npm run check:all` clean (server 3,996, client 266).
- **No browser check:** Playwright failed to connect this session and ToolSearch found no Playwright tools, so the form and the editor were checked by their first-paint tests only.
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (the kids turn's age). `Privacy.tsx` and `.context/ai-transparency.md`: nothing to change (the age was already part of the premise; no new tool or practice).

## 2026-10-01: the kids-ages stage: read-with-kids turns and setups by the children's age band, measured and adopted ($0.245)

- **The cause, in production's request:** the kids-turns stage gave a single player's read-with-kids turn one length for every age (3-4 short paragraphs, 80-140 words, sentences of 5-12 words), measured on a five-year-old's story at Flesch-Kincaid grade 4.2: a picture book's listener got an early chapter book's turn, and a ten-year-old the same. A group's read-with-kids turn kept the grown-up "5-6 paragraphs of 3-5 sentences" (`takesKidsRules` excluded groups). The kids setup budget (two visible shared, two visible player stats) was the same at every age; the owner rated a setup for 7-10 year olds as having "too many stats" before it existed, and decided "small up to about 10 ... a little more for older children".
- **The bands** (the youngest child's, `kidsBandOf`; 6-8 where no age is recorded), from what children's books of each age do (sources in `kidsAges.ts`'s header): 3-5, picture books (Mary Kole Editorial's length guidelines: picture books, ages 3-7, at most about 600 words; publishers' guides: 250-600 words for ages 2-5, 10-60 a page): 2-3 very short paragraphs of 1-3 sentences, about 40-90 words, sentences of 4-8 words, everyday words and simple feelings, sounds and repetition welcome. 6-8, early readers and early chapter books (Kole: early readers 5-7 at most 1,500, chapter books 7-9 4,000-15,000): the kids-turns stage's turn word for word. 9-12, chapter books and young middle grade (Kole: 9-11 15,000-35,000; chapter-book guides keep the average sentence "in the teens", Harry Potter's about 12; the Common Core band for grades 4-5 reads Flesch-Kincaid 4.51-7.73): 4-5 paragraphs of 2-4 sentences, about 150-230 words, sentences of 8-15 words, a new word allowed where the sentence makes it clear. Picture places per band, never the last paragraph (3-5 the first, and the second only of three; 6-8 the second, or the third of four; 9-12 the first and the third). Setup: a third visible player stat for 9-12 only.
- **The variant** (`storyTextRounds/kidsAges.ts`, `kidsAges`): on a read-with-kids story of every player count, production's turn without its kids lines (the story with its category taken out) with the band's context count, rules block, shouted repeat, text field count and repeat, picture places and retry count; production's kids setup with the 9-12 edits. Production's request byte for byte at 6-8 for one player, without an age, and on every other story (tests).
- **The cases** (`kidsAgesCases.ts`, no calls): the kids-turns stage's six mouse turns recorded with the setting at 4 and at 10 (none at 7: the variant is production's request there); the two-player cooperative animal rescue from setup round 3's stored chain (no stored group kids story existed), its first turn and its switch turn after the first chapter at 4, 7 and 10, rebuilt from the chain's own states (their requests on the chain's form hash to the prompts it sent, a test); the mouse and animal-rescue setups at 10 (none below 9: production's setup byte for byte). 18 turn cases, 2 setup cases.
- **The stage** (`kids-ages`, under `adopted16`, interleaved, production's checked retry in the loop): capped at $0.39 (the estimate plus 30%; the ledger $39.94 of $45). Read before paying: the dry run (76 jobs, est $0.26 before retries) and one variant request of each kind end to end (a 3-5 single-player chapter step, a 9-12 group switch turn, the rescue setup at 10). The readability limits (`KIDS_BAND_LIMITS`: 3-5 30-100 words, at most 9 words a sentence, grade 3; 6-8 the kids-turns stage's; 9-12 130-260 words, at most 15, grade 6) set before the run.
- **The run:** 72 turn jobs (a smoke, then the rest), every reply kept valid; production's checked retry re-sent one group turn (a short reply), and one of production's single-player endings was cut at its output cap (89 s) and re-sent, then valid; the variant needed neither. The 4 setup jobs (a smoke of the mouse premise, then the rescue), every one valid first time. Spent $0.2448 (turns $0.2254, setups $0.019); the ledger $40.18 of $45.
- **Readings (turns that read for their band, deterministic):** one player at 4: 1 of 12 → 6 of 12, moved higher (p 0.034); words 104.5 → 75.9, words a sentence 11.9 → 8.4, grade 3.6 → 1.9, words an option 13.9 → 9.9, all moved lower. One player at 10: 1 of 12 → 6 of 12, moved higher (p 0.034); words 109 → 182.5 and words a sentence 12.7 → 14.5 moved higher (the band asks for both); grade 4.7 → 5.3 not moved; of the six misses, five sit at 15.5-17.4 words a sentence (four of them also above grade 6) and one is 2 words under the band's 130-word floor (t2 s1: 128 words, 12.8 words a sentence, grade 5.2; corrected by the review, below). Two players at 4: 0 of 4 → 3 of 4 (p 0.071, moved), words 317 → 68, grade 7.3 → 1.5; at 7: 0 of 4 → 2 of 4 (p 0.214, not moved), words 300 → 109 and grade 6.8 → 3.2 moved lower; at 10: 0 of 4 → 4 of 4 (p 0.014, moved), words 295 → 189, grade 7.0 → 4.2; the groups pooled 0 of 12 → 9 of 12 (p < 0.001, moved).
- **By hand (all 72 kept replies, through a temporary dump test, deleted):** continuity holds in both arms (the chosen action and its result open the turn; the group switch tells both players' actions and the chapter's mixed milestone; both players in one scene). Third-person slips in a second-person turn: production 4 of 24, the variant 2 of 24; endings that wrote options: production 3 of 4, the variant 1 of 4. The variant's own defects, each once or twice unless said: a single-player turn titled with the step's title (t7 at 4, s2); milestones copied from the plan, 3 of its 8 single-player milestones by the automatic check against none of production's 8 (`milestoneNotCopied` 100% → 62.5%, p = 0.100 exactly, on the stop rule's p < 0.10 line, so not moved only at the boundary), all three at 4 (corrected by the review, below); a 3-5 lever option still in the stat's jargon ("Give up one step of Mouse Smarts (Passage Expert to Clever Planner)"); the group's first turns at 4 naming Pip, Juniper and Moss without introducing them (`introductionDropped` repairs 0.08 → 0.50, not moved); one group switch at 10 swapping its third planned direction for an invented one (1 of 6, production 0 of 6). `explorationOptionsFollowResults` 100% → 50% (a heuristic, 4 turns): by hand all four follow their results.
- **Setups by hand (×1 at 10):** production two shared and two player stats in both (Cat's Attention, Burrow Supplies, Nerve, Helpful Finds; Supplies, Storm, Energy, Skills), the variant two shared and three player stats in both (Burrow Safety, Cat's Curiosity, Map Notes, Nerve, Village Trust; Trail Safety, Rescue Kit, Energy, Helpful Skills, Calm): each its band's budget, plain names, no hidden stats, every lever the right way (a pressure raised as the sacrifice: Cat's Curiosity; safety lowered: Burrow Safety, Trail Safety). The third stat was a three-step string both times. One oddity: the variant's mouse setup marked both shared stats as not changed in turns while giving them lever texts (spendable share 75% → 50%, one sample; levers still on Map Notes and Nerve).
- **Waits and cost:** level or shorter (group switch p95 70.8 → 40.5 s; single chapter step 52.4 → 43.6 s; production's ending 119.5 s from its cut attempt, the variant's 39.8 s), except the single player's switch turn, p95 32.2 → 49.7 s, over its 45 s allowance (4 turns, one slow reply at 3-5; production's single-player chapter steps and ending were over it in this run instead). $/call −13% on both turn models; setups level.
- **Verdict: adopted, turns and setup** (the stop rule on the band reading, the hand reading, nothing worse). One player: 3-5 and 9-12 moved (6-8 is production's own turn). Groups: every band, on the pooled reading and each band's words and grade; the 6-8 group's passes alone did not move (2 of 4, p 0.214), and its words and grade did. The variant's few defects are each matched or outnumbered by production's (third-person slips, endings with options) or not moved (the copied milestones only at the boundary). No fix-and-retest: no specific cause stood out (five of the six 9-12 misses are sentence length at the band's edge, one is 2 words short of the 130-word floor, and the band's passes moved). Setup: the owner's "a little more for older children", followed in both setups with nothing worse by hand.
- **Production** (test-first): `kidsTurnRules.ts` holds each band's text (`KIDS_BAND_TURNS`, `kidsBand`, `kidsTurnText`); `takesKidsRules` is every read-with-kids story, every player count; `BeatPromptService` prints the band's context, rules, repeat and picture line; `beatSchemaForKids` the band's text field; `beatCheckOptions` the band's retry count. Setup: `OLDER_KIDS_STATS` and `takesOlderKidsBudget` (`setupPromptText.ts`), the inventory's "Three visible stats…" (`StorySetupPromptService`) and the player stats' field (`setupSchema.ts`) where `kidAges`'s youngest child is 9 or older; `SetupPromptOptions` and `SetupSchemaOptions` take `kidAges`; `StoryCreationService` works out the ages before the setup and passes them (`{ kids, kidAges }`). The eval's `adopted` setup reads the ages as story creation does (the setting, else the premise's line). Production is the variant byte for byte: every band, every turn kind, one to three players, prompt and JSON schema, and the setup at every age; with images, apart from the picture places by band, which no case measured (a logged delta since the review, below).
- **Kept tests changed on purpose:** `adoptedTurns.test.ts` (a kids turn of every player count is `kidsAges`; a single player's 6-8 or ageless turn still `kidsTurn` with its logged image places; each band's text from production's constants), `adoptedForms.test.ts` (a kids turn and a 9-12 kids setup read against `kidsAges`), `adoptedSetup.test.ts` (new: production is the measured kids-ages setup at every age), `kidsTurn.test.ts`, `moneyAddsUp.test.ts` and `kidsTurnCases.test.ts` (the variants measured before compare with production as it stood: `productionBeforeKidsAges` in `adoptedDeltas.ts`), `jobPlan.test.ts` (the kids-turns stage's production retry now asks for its band's count), `kidsAges.test.ts` and `kidsAgesCases.test.ts` (production now equals the variant). Test-first: the rewritten `kidsTurnRules.test.ts` and `adoptedTurns.test.ts` could not load before the change; 10 setup tests failed (`adoptedSetup.test.ts` 8, `StoryCreationService.setupStatus.test.ts` 2); then all passed.
- **No browser check:** nothing on screen changed in this step, and Playwright failed to connect this session (ToolSearch found no Playwright tools).
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (the bands, the older children's setup budget, the readings), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the variant, the cases and report modes, the stage's cap and spend, its arms and readings, production text calls). `Privacy.tsx` and `.context/ai-transparency.md`: nothing to change (no new tool or practice; turn length is not a disclosed practice).

## 2026-10-01: review of the kids-ages adoption and the read-with-kids setting: picture places logged as unmeasured, the template editor's ages field, Draft World's ages, the summary corrected (no calls, $0)

Four verified findings, fixed test-first; no paid run (the ledger stays $40.18 of $45).

- **Picture places (important):** no measured request carried the band picture lines. All 18 turn cases of the kids-ages stage have `generateImages` false and an empty image library (no template), and the variant prints its picture places only where a turn shows images, so the 3-5 lines, the 9-12 lines and a group's 6-8 lines (until then the grown-up "third or fourth paragraph") were never sent. Yet the kept test held production to them as "the measured kidsAges", and this log said "with and without images". Most real kids stories take that path: the Read with Kids link opens with images on, and a template's story counts as showing them. **My call: log them as an unmeasured delta, the way `withKidsImageSlots` was, rather than pay for a stage with images.** They only move where the image tags go, never onto the last paragraph, in turns whose length the stage did measure; the kids-turns stage's one case with images wrote 4 paragraphs both times. A small paid stage with images on stays possible if a kids playthrough shows pictures misplaced. Production is unchanged.
  - `adoptedDeltas.ts` now has `GROWN_UP_PICTURE_PLACES`, `KIDS_BAND_PICTURE_PLACES` (the delta's text), `kidsAgesAsMeasured` (the variant's request with its own picture places swapped back to the grown-up lines it was built on) and `withKidsBandImageSlots`.
  - `adoptedTurns.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts` read a kids turn as `withKidsBandImageSlots(kidsAgesAsMeasured(...))`, and production's band image constants against the delta, not against the variant.
  - New tests: the delta acts only where a turn shows images, every band and player count, and without images production is the measured request byte for byte. Where the eval's folder exists, every kids-ages turn case shows no images and production sends its measured request byte for byte.
  - Test-first: both suites could not load before the helpers existed.
- **The template editor's Children's ages field (minor):**
  - **Stale value after Discard or Revert.** The field kept its own text, reset only by `templateId`, so Discard changes and Revert to a save left it showing what was typed before. Now `useTemplateForm` holds the text (`kidAgesInput`, `handleKidAgesInput`) and drops it whenever the form takes a whole template: loaded, discarded, reverted or drafted. The field (`BasicInfoTab`'s `KidAgesField`, now controlled) then shows that template's ages (`kidAgesFieldText`).
  - **A typo deleted the stored ages.** An unreadable value ("8-1") sent null, and Save went ahead, deleting the stored ages while the screen showed the typo. Now `handleSubmit` refuses to save while a template tagged Kids holds unreadable ages (`unreadableTemplateKidAges`): an error notice, and the Meta tab opens on the hint. This matches the setup form, which keeps Create Story disabled. My call: refuse on submit rather than disable the Save buttons, so the reason is shown from any tab (Enter in the field submits too). The server's handling of null is unchanged.
- **Draft World dropped the ages (minor).** The form checked the ages and blocked Draft World on an unreadable value, then sent only the premise, so the drafted template got no Kids tag and no ages. The verification corrected the finding's consequence: without the tag the template's stories got no kids rules at all, not the 6-8 band. Now:
  - `StoryInitializer` sends the category and the ages read in Draft World's options too.
  - `handleAIDraftSetup` merges `draftedKidsSetting` (new `resources/templates/utils/templateKidAges.ts`): on read-with-kids, the Kids tag once and the form's ages; an empty field keeps the template's own; any other category changes nothing.
  - The editor's tag list (which Save sends) takes the tag too.
  - Template generation itself still knows no category (the earlier call stands for the generation prompt).
  - Test-first: the new `templateKidAges.test.ts` could not load, and 2 new `BasicInfoTab.test.tsx` tests failed (the typed text over the template's ages, the hint for "8-1"); then all passed.
- **The summary (minor), corrected above.**
  - **The 9-12 misses.** Five of the six one-player misses at 10 are sentence length (15.5-17.4 words a sentence, four also above grade 6). One, t2 s1, is 128 words, under the band's 130-word floor (12.8 words a sentence, grade 5.2). The verdict's "no specific cause" stands: the band asks for "about 150 to 230" words, and one reply 2 words under the floor is not a cause.
  - **The copied milestones.** These were "each once or twice" in the summary, but the automatic check flagged 3 of the variant's 8 single-player milestones against none of production's 8. The one-sided Fisher p is 56/560 = 0.100 exactly, on the stop rule's strict p < 0.10 line, so "not moved" holds only at the boundary. A temporary probe over the stored replies (no calls, deleted after) found all three at 4, none at 10:
    - t5 s1: the plan's milestone word for word;
    - t11 s1: one verb changed;
    - t5 s2: its first clause copied, the rest reworded.
  - Production's milestones reword the plan's opening but keep much of its wording: both arms end t11's with the plan's "Pip trusts Bran as a close partner who shares discoveries and advice". **My call: no fix-and-retest.** Production asks for no own-words milestone, and a split by age is post hoc on 4 turns a side. It is a lean for the kids playthroughs to watch: do 3-5 turns copy the plan's milestone?
- `npm run check:all` clean (server 4,091, client 276). **No browser check:** the Playwright server failed to connect this session and ToolSearch found no Playwright tools, so the editor's field, its Save refusal and Draft World were checked by their tests and the type check only.
- **Docs:** `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the delta in the kept-test paragraph and production's turn, the stage's adoption line, the 9-12 misses and the copied milestones); `.context/story.md` (the template editor's field and Draft World, the unmeasured picture places). `Privacy.tsx` and `.context/ai-transparency.md`: nothing to change (no new tool or practice).

## 2026-10-01: the group-levers stage: group sacrifices, rewards and players' own stats, measured, one fix-and-retest, adopted ($0.373)

- **The brief:** the second playthroughs' review, section 4.4: "1 sacrifice in 129 group option sets", no reward, and group players' own stats rarely moved (3 of 7 ended elsewhere, the space pirates' none). B6 was never built or measured for groups. Find the cause in the group turn's request and the option rules, build at it, measure on the stored group cases twice against production.
- **The cause, read from the second round's stored group stories (temporary Jest probes over `playthroughs-2.json`, replayed with `replayRun`, deleted):**
  - Every group setup had own and shared stats with lever rules (food trucks: District Know-How, Owner Stamina, Business Reputation, District Safety, Neighborhood Confidence; the estate agents: Professional Credibility, Useful Contacts, Nerve, two shared; the pirates: Personal Quota, Personal Contacts, Ship Integrity, Patrol Heat). So not the setup.
  - The option rules are a single player's only (`takesOptionRules` returns false for a group). A group's challenge or contest step therefore has no computed lever line ("Sacrifice or reward: one fits this turn if a stat allows it"), keeps the plan question's "(Many beats are better without any sacrifice or reward options.)", and has no reward exception, so a reward breaks "Don't … suddenly do something else, or derail the core theme".
  - Counts: 105 player option sets sat on a rolled step in the three group stories; B6's line would have invited a lever in 103 of them (the pirates' pilot's two after his one lever said none); 1 carried one (the pirates' turn 11, 10% Ship Integrity). Every group plan answered the lever question "None" (174 of 175 option sets, the one other the turn-11 lever), in that question's frame: "No sacrifice or reward is needed for this final assessment", "A sacrifice or reward would distract from the evidence-focused contest", "A records explanation is more interesting without spending credibility or contacts", "a repair reward would prematurely grant a result that belongs to the thread resolution". The single-player stories of the same round, with the line, offered a lever each of the 6 times it invited one.
  - Own stats: a player stat moves by its levers, by a minor change where it may change any time, and by its after-thread rule at a switch. With no group levers the groups' own stats moved only at switches and endings (food trucks 6 changes, estate agents 4, the pirates none in 25 turns, their rules tied to narrow thread kinds); Avalon's single player, by contrast, moved Personal Reserve and City Contacts through levers. So the lever drought is the own-stat cause too; the one group lever was on a shared stat.
- **The variant** (`storyTextRounds/groupLevers.ts`, `groupLevers`): production's group turn with B6's lever parts for each player in a rolled thread (`groupLeverSlots`): the reward exception after the rule it excepts, after the "0 or 1 sacrifice/reward option" rule each such player's computed line (`sacrificeRewardLine` on their own history, "----- player2 (Jo Park): one fits this turn if a stat allows it."), one sentence for a group (my call: a shared stat's lever spends or gains for everyone, so it goes to one player a turn, which production's `sharedLeverRepeated` repair otherwise enforces by dropping a later copy and leaving that player two options; and the player's own stats first, at the own-stat cause), `NO_DOUBLE_SACRIFICE`, and in those players' fields B6's field edits (the options field's reward exception, the plan question without "(Many beats …)"). Not B6's three ways, base-point scale or two-bonus cap (my call: variety and odds, not levers; they would have moved other readings in the same run). Production's request byte for byte elsewhere; slots sharing one schema instance keep sharing one, so an all-rolled turn's JSON schema keeps production's shape.
- **The cases** (`groupLeversCases.ts`, no calls): twelve group chapter steps of the second round's runs, each request the one the run sent (and production sent the same then): food trucks 2, 10, 13, 15, 21; the estate agents 10, 16, 20 (their replay is the story played only up to 21); the pirates 2, 10, 12, 16 — two and three players, openings and later steps, shared contests, shared and own challenges, players exploring beside, one computed line of none, one preferring a reward.
- **The stage** (`group-levers`, under `adopted17`, interleaved, production's checked retry in the loop, deterministic readings, no judge): capped at $0.36 (estimate plus 30%; the ledger $40.18 of $45). Read before paying: the dry run (48 jobs, est $0.18) and three variant requests end to end with their diffs against production (a two-player contest step, the pirates' mixed opening, the pirates' step with Jori's line at none; prompt and JSON schema): nothing contradicts (the reward exception only widens the derail rule, the lines sit under the 0-or-1 rule they refine, the plan question still reads the stats' lever rules).
- **The run** (07:20-07:30 UTC): a 2-job smoke (the pirates' turn 10; the schema accepted, the variant's first reply short and retried), then the other 46; every kept reply valid. The run's variant against production (50 sets each): sets with a lever 0 → 11 (moved, p < 0.001), on the player's own stat 0 → 10 (moved), rewards 0 → 1 (not moved), none where the line said none (0 of 2), every lever on a stat whose rule allows it, no shared copy dropped; automatic checks nothing worse. First replies retried 3 of 24 against production's 1 (not moved), so its p95 waits with the retry read over the 60 s allowance on chapter openings (two players 69.4 s, three 101.3 s), the first reply alone within.
- **The fix-and-retest, at a specific cause:** where the line invited a lever the variant offered one in 11 of 48 sets (a single player's B6 in the playthroughs 6 of 6). Of the 37 sets without one, 33 plans declined in the plan question's own frame (it asks whether a lever "would be sensible and interesting for this beat … Otherwise, simply say 'None'", and the plan is written before the options: "No sacrifice or reward is needed for this card-review contest beat", "would distract from the resident's boundaries"), 2 cited the no-repeat rule, and 2 planned a sacrifice the options then wrote as a normal option at base +30. `groupLeversB` asks the rolled players' plan question from their line instead ("where the line says one fits this turn, describe exactly one sacrifice or reward option … on one of this player's own stats where one allows it, and the reason this scene gives for it. Say 'None' only where the line says none this turn, or where no stat allows one."); the rest byte for byte the run's variant. Once on the twelve cases (a 1-job smoke, then 11; $0.059): 11 of 25 against the variant's first sample's 6 of 25, beyond the noise, not moved (p 0.116), every target moved against production. My call: its second sample, since the stage measures twice and it was the form to adopt: 12 more jobs under a stage cap raised to $0.43 for its arm (`--stage-cap 0.43 --arms gpt-6-luna@low/groupLeversB`, the reason in `budget-overrides.jsonl`; $0.059).
- **Readings, `groupLeversB` ×2 (50 sets) against production and against the run's variant:** sets with a lever 0 → 25 (moved, p < 0.001) and 11 → 25 (moved, p 0.003); on the player's own stat 0 → 21 (moved) and 10 → 21 (moved, p 0.015); sacrifices 0 → 17; rewards 0 → 8 (moved) and 1 → 8 (moved, p 0.015); on a shared stat 1 (Ship Integrity, one player); none where the line said none (0 of 2). Automatic checks against production: `sameStatsOnlyRiskShare` 88% → 74% and `oddsWithin5Share` 58% → 30% moved lower (options told apart by more than risk), nothing the wrong way. No first reply retried (0 of 24). Waits: p50 41.0 → 41.5 s, p95 56.5 → 56.1 s, a three-player opening at 65.6 s (one of four, at the run's slowest pace); reasoning tokens +27%; $/call −4%.
- **By hand (every lever of all three arms, and the variants' plans; a temporary dump, deleted):** B's 25 levers each sit on a stat whose rule allows that lever and fit their scene ("Spend 10% Owner Stamina to stay beside the active orders, checking each one …", "Pay 10% of your Personal Quota for a fresh diagnostic probe …", "Burn your salvage-pilot contact on a one-time remote fitting consultation …"); its 8 rewards are each the turning aside the stat's own rule names ("Accept a less convenient follow-up appointment with the heritage volunteer … gaining one new contact"; "Admit the card needs verification …, delay relying on it, and gain 10% Professional Credibility", the rule's "admitting a mistake and delaying the pitch"). The game's text reading misses three of them (`leverStatOf`: "Ship Integrity" for Wayward Comet Integrity, twice "salvage-pilot contact" for Personal Contacts), so those levers' payments go unrecorded for the double-charge repair, an existing reading gap (the round-2 report's three "stat unnamed" levers). Levers come by turn: where a turn has one, most of its rolled players do (the food trucks' two owners both spend stamina at turns 10 and 15; the estate agents' two both take credibility rewards at 10 and 16), and in the estate agents' case both rivals take the same kind of cautious reward. A lever written as a normal option at ±30 (the game counts the points and charges nothing): production 1 of 50 sets, the variant 2, B 2, not moved; a no-call repair could recognise it (an option at exactly ±30 whose text spends or gains a stat that allows that lever), left for the coordinator.
- **Verdict: adopted, `groupLeversB`.** It moved every target against production twice over (levers, own-stat levers, rewards) and moved the run's variant on all three, with nothing worse and two variety checks moved the right way. In play the line spaces levers (none in the player's next two rolled turns after one), so at about half of invited sets a rolled player meets about one lever a chapter, as a single player does; rewards at most one a chapter per player. The run's variant (A) passed on levers and own stats but not rewards.
- **Production** (test-first): `optionRules.ts` holds the text (`GROUP_SHARED_AND_OWN`, `GROUP_LEVER_QUESTION`) and `groupLeverSlots`, `takesGroupLeverRules`, `groupSacrificeRewardLines`, `beatSchemaWithGroupLevers` (slots sharing one instance keep sharing one); `BeatPromptService` prints the reward exception and the players' lines on a group's rolled step; `beatStep.request` puts the fields in the schema. A group's rolled step is `groupLeversB` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema; every other turn unchanged. The variant now builds on production with those lines and fields taken out (`groupLeversBase`), so it still builds as measured.
- **Kept tests changed on purpose:** `adoptedTurns.test.ts` (a group's rolled step, mixed turns included, is `groupLeversB`; production's constants are the variant's text) and `adoptedForms.test.ts` (the frozen group rolled steps against `groupLeversB`); `adoptedDeltas.ts` gains `beforeGroupLevers`, and `productionThen` gives a group's rolled step as production stood before (prompt and schema), which the variants measured earlier compare with (`choiceResult`, `kidsTurn`, `moneyAddsUp`, `parallelThreads`, `choiceResultCases` tests); `productionBeforeKidsAges` reads a group's step before it too; `playthroughReplay.test.ts` reads the round-2 group rolled steps (49 up to the estate agents' turn 21) as production sent them then; `groupLevers.test.ts` and `groupLeversCases.test.ts` read the variant's base and production since the adoption. Test-first: the optionRules, adoptedTurns, groupLevers and groupLeversCases suites could not load before the production exports and `groupLeversBase` existed, and `adoptedForms` failed on a frozen group step; after the change 13 comparisons in the earlier variants' tests and the round-2 replay failed, and were moved to production as it stood on purpose.
- **Unmeasured:** whether the next turn pays a group player's lever as it does a single player's (the playthroughs paid 7 of 7, one of them a group's); a read-with-kids group's rolled step, which takes the lever lines beside its band's (no case of the kids-ages stage was a rolled group step); the players' own stats' after-thread rules at switches (moved in 2 of 3 stories without help; not this stage's target).
- **A paid run of production's own group turn** takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted17`'s production records are the group turn before).
- **Spend:** $0.373 (the run $0.256, the retest $0.117); the stage's cap $0.36 (estimate plus 30%), raised to $0.43 for the retest's second sample with its reason recorded (`budget-overrides.jsonl`), and kept as the stage's default since (`budget.ts`, so the ledger reads the stage within its cap); the ledger $40.56 of $45 (about $41.86 if the stalled Stage 4 calls were billed).
- **No browser check:** nothing on screen changed. **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (a group's levers), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (production's turn, the kept tests, the cases and report modes, the stage's cap and spend, its arms and readings). `Privacy.tsx` and `.context/ai-transparency.md`: nothing to change.

## 2026-10-01: the short-replies stage: turns that come back as one short paragraph, measured and adopted ($0.366)

- **The brief:** the second playthroughs' one-paragraph turns: 13 of 126 first replies, 2 short again after production's retry and used (the food trucks' first turn for both players, the mouse story's switch turn 5). Read those replies and their requests, find the cause, fix it there, measure on cases built from them plus ordinary ones.
- **The cause, read before the build (temporary Jest probes over `playthroughs-2.json` and every stored GPT-6 turn reply of the eval, deleted):**
  - Each of the 13 is one ordinary first paragraph (45-82 words, no line break) covering the plan's `showDontTellPreviousDecision` and first show-don't-tell item: the chosen action and how it plays out (New Avalon turn 2: the descent and the arrival, the controls and Mara's warning never come). The plans were as long as full replies' (three show-don't-tell items); nothing else in the reply was short. In each group reply every player's text was short.
  - Over the eval's 3,784 stored GPT-6 turn replies (4,564 player texts outside the kids stages) the paragraph counts are bimodal: 147 texts of one paragraph, none of two, 3 of three, 15 of four, the rest five or six. Every short text is 45-96 words. So the reply doesn't write a shorter turn: it closes the text string where its first paragraph break would go. In a group the later players' texts follow the first's (31 of 34 short group replies short for every player).
  - The request says five times "5-6 paragraphs", says what the first paragraph must do and what the last must not, and nothing about going on after the first, nor how the paragraphs are written inside the reply's one string (the client splits at line breaks).
  - Rates: 2.9% of stored Luna medium replies, 3.6% of Luna low; about 10% on cases from long stored stories (the second playthroughs 13 of 126, recorded-result 5 of 32, late-pacing 10 of 115) against 1-2% on the early frozen cases (options-continuity 6 of 373). Some states draw it more: synth-8988006e-t3's step 13 of 56 over every form, the first round's New Avalon turn 6 3 of 11. Production's retry, which repeats the count at the end of the prompt, was short again on 4 of the 16 playthrough turns it retried (both rounds).
- **The variant** (`storyTextRounds/shortReplies.ts`, `shortReplies`): production's turn, every turn kind, player count and form, with one line in the text rules right before "- Show, don't tell." ("- The text never ends after its first paragraph: write a blank line and go on with the next paragraph, until every paragraph is written.") and one in each player's text field right before "- Start exactly where …" ("- All the paragraphs go in this one text, a blank line (\n\n) between each and the next. The text never ends after its first paragraph: write the blank line and go on."). No count named, so a kids band keeps its own (my call). The retry unchanged (my call: it is the safety net, and the target is the first reply).
- **The cases** (`shortRepliesCases.ts`, no calls): the round's short turns no stage had frozen (New Avalon 2, 8, 12, 14, 24; the food trucks' 1), each request the one the run sent; beside them the round's short turns earlier stages froze (mouse 3, 5; food trucks 21, 26; space pirates 16; estate agents 15; their 22 follows the owner's roll at 21, so the replay no longer reaches its state), the seven stored turns that came back short most often (synth-8988006e-t3, the first round's New Avalon 6 and 11, lemonade 4, 6, 11, space pirates 25), and four never short in 5-6 earlier replies (New Avalon 23, lemonade 2, estate agents 10, space pirates 2). 15 single-player, 8 group (my call on the mix: the target's base rate is about 10-20% on these states, so 46 turns an arm can show a drop to near zero).
- **The stage** (`short-replies`, under `adopted18`, interleaved, production's checked retry in the loop, deterministic readings, no judge): capped at $0.60 (estimate plus 30%; the ledger $40.56 of $45). Read before paying: the dry run (92 jobs, est $0.34 before retries) and five variant requests end to end with their diffs against production (a single-player switch turn, the food trucks' first turn, the mouse story's 3-5 switch turn, a single-player ending, a three-player rolled step): one line in the prompt and one per text field, nothing else; nothing contradicts (a kids band's 2-3 paragraphs and "never ends after its first paragraph" agree).

No text of two or more paragraphs fell below its form's count and none went above it, either arm. Every automatic check: nothing moved, first replies or replies kept. No new specific cause, so no fix-and-retest (my call; a structural fix, the text as a list of paragraphs with a minimum count, would force the break, but the Stage 4 rewrite's `minItems` made GPT-6 pad blank items and hang on 43 of 149 attempts).

Marginal, and said so: neither turn model moved alone, and the sticky state still went short, the retry too. Unmeasured: a whole story's rate with the lines.

(the space that ends the line before it kept, as it always was); `beatStep.request` applies the field line last, after B6's, the group levers' and the kids band's edits. ## 2026-10-01: the runaway-2 stage: the runaway turn's cause, a second attempt (measured, not adopted; $0.171)

- **The brief:** another attempt at the reasoning-runaway turn's cause (story 8988006e's turn 4 and any other runaway in the stored records): a new hypothesis from the requests and the reasoning token counts, tested with a few replays against production; adopt a general fix if one emerges.
- **What the stored records say (temporary Jest probes over `calls.jsonl`, `playthroughs.json`, `playthroughs-2.json`, `late-pacing.json` and the stored prompts and replies; deleted; no calls):**
  - **Where:** every GPT-6 reply the eval ever cut at the output cap fell on a turn that closes a chapter (a switch turn after a chapter, or the ending). First tries on Luna medium since 29 September: 15 of 192 closing turns (12 of 121 switch turns, 3 of 71 endings), 0 of 639 chapter steps and openings, 0 of 40 first turns; 2 more in the playthroughs (the first round's New Avalon switch turn 5, the second round's mouse ending). Luna low: 0 of the 145 closing turns ever sent (88 since 29 September).
  - **Not one moment:** besides Novi Reg after its first chapter (switch, pregeneration, ending), the lemonade story's switch turn 4 (the money-adds-up variant, twice in a row) and ending 11, the mouse story's switch turn 5 and ending 11, New Avalon's switch turn 5. The 30 September report's "one moment of one story" was the records up to then.
  - **When:** before 29 September 1 of 143 closing first tries ran away (B9's split form, 28 September); since, 15 of 192. On Novi Reg's switch turn the request production sends answered 2 of 2 on 28 September and ran away 4 times in 7 after; every other form of that moment ran clean from 26 to 28 September. On identical prompts sent before and after 29 September the answered replies came back about a quarter faster after (chapter steps' medians 45-55 s → 35-40 s) and reasoned somewhat less: something changed on the server side then.
  - **A loop, not a hard question:** answered closing turns reason less than chapter steps (median 1,479 switch turns, 1,390 endings, against about 1,900; the longest 3,025); a runaway uses all 12,000.
  - **What only a closing turn's request carries:** two blocks of the stat-changes section, THREAD RESOLUTION (the after-thread stat audit, "all stats can change"; the statChanges field's after-thread sentence, which every turn carries and only a closing turn meets) and NEW MILESTONES (the milestone written into `newMilestones`; every other turn sends ""). The first turn carries neither and never ran away; a chapter step carries "Only stats … 'Can be adjusted anytime' can be changed" instead.
  - **Ruled out on the data:** caching (1 of the 15 replies cut on Luna medium since 28 September had cached input, 45 of 1,698 answered ones); the stat audit's instability as the trigger (the lemonade ending and the mouse switch turn ran away though every answered reply there changed no stat).
- **The hypothesis:** the loop needs one of the two closing blocks. **The test** (my design): two diagnostics, each taking one block out, beside production on the case that ran away most; not forms to adopt. `storyTextRounds/closingTurn.ts`: `noThreadAudit` (the audit replaced by a chapter step's stat line, the field's sentence cut) and `noNewMilestones` (no milestone block nor the ending's played line, `newMilestones` ""), production's live single-player request byte for byte otherwise and on every other turn. Sixteen samples an arm (my call: at the case's rate since 29 September, about 40%, a diagnostic that never runs away moves once production runs away 4 times in 16, about nine times in ten; the first replay's three could show nothing), first tries only (no checked retry), a smoke, then samples 2-8, and samples 9-16 only if production ran away in the first eight (set before the run).
- **The stage** (`runaway-2`, under `adopted19`, interleaved, production's single-player limits): capped at $0.60 (estimate with runaways and their retries and room for one fix-and-retest, plus 30%; the ledger $40.93 of $45). Read before paying: production's request on the case is the one that ran away on 30 September plus the short-replies lines (a test holds its hash), and end to end with its JSON schema; each diagnostic differs from it by its one prompt block and one schema field (the diffs read). Dry run 48 jobs, est $0.17 before runaways.
- **The run** (09:58-10:10 UTC): the smoke (3 jobs, all answered), samples 2-8 (production's sample 5 cut at the cap at 86.8 s, its retry answered), samples 9-16; 48 jobs as counted, 49 attempts, no transport failure.
- **Readings (`runaway-2.md`):**
  - **Runaways:** production 1 of 16, `noThreadAudit` 0 of 16, `noNewMilestones` 0 of 16: within the noise (production's odd samples 1 of 8, even 0 of 8), Fisher p 0.5. Not moved.
  - **Reasoning tokens, answered replies (mean):** production 1,767, `noThreadAudit` 1,758 (within the noise), `noNewMilestones` 1,417, **moved lower (2.9 SE)**.
  - **By hand:** without the audit's instruction the model audits anyway (the surveillance stat's after-thread +5 in 16 of 16, as production's 15 of 15; 2.5 stat changes a reply against 2.7): the stat definitions carry each stat's rule, so the instruction is not what the model deliberates over. Every production milestone records the exposé's release, beyond the chapter's planned "uncovers concrete evidence", the case's crossing; writing it is about a fifth of the closing turn's thinking.
- **Verdict: no general fix emerged; nothing built for production, nothing adopted.** The stored records place the failure on closing turns, on Luna medium, since about 29 September; the replay could not say which block the loop needs, because production ran away 1 time in 16 today against 4 in 7 on 30 September. No fix-and-retest (my call): at today's rate showing a fix's none would take about 60 samples an arm (0 of 60 against 4 of 60: Fisher p 0.06), about $0.45, beyond the stage's $0.43 left, and the rate swings with the server. The lead for a later attempt: the milestone block (the one block whose removal shortened the thinking). Production's request now carries the short-replies lines; whether they lowered the rate, or the server did, is unmeasured.
- **What the owner could weigh** (none built): a closing turn cut at the cap retried at Luna low (no closing turn ever ran away there; the one retry is then short and certain, at low effort's quality for that turn); a lower output cap on closing turns (cuts a runaway sooner; the longest answered single-player reply since the adoption is 6,795 tokens); the first replay's single-player cap near 9,000.
