## 2026-10-01: the options-o2c stage: option variety with fewer rewards, measured and adopted ($0.50)

- **The brief:** O2b's option variety (options draw on different stats, no risk-only sets, the strength option keeps its negative base) with a reward offered in about one chapter in two or three, only where a stat allows one, never twice a chapter, the game computing when; target about 4-6 reward sets per 32 rolled sets (O2b 14, production 2); and O2b's extra reasoning wait and odds tilt (the strength option best in 91% of sets against production's 71%) recovered if possible. The owner: "14 reward options in 32 choice sets is a bit too much. At most one reward is good. Several sacrifices can sometimes make sense, but should have a strong justification starting at the second one." Measure beside production on the 44 single-player turn cases' rolled steps ×2, adopt if it passes, rebuild the options page.
- **The cause, read from the stored replies of the options-o2 and options-continuity stages on the 32 rolled steps (a temporary Jest probe, deleted; no calls):**
  - **Rewards:** O2b's line invites a reward on every rolled step until the chapter has offered one, whatever B6's rate says. No stored chapter offered one before its turn, so it invited on all 32 and took it on 14 (13 of 24 where it said "a reward fits … No sacrifice this turn.", 1 of 8 where it added "and so does a sacrifice"). In play the same line invites on every step until one comes, so most chapters (about 80% at three steps) would end with a reward.
  - **The odds tilt:** the balance simulation's "strength option" is any normal option with a bonus. Read by kind: production's tempting option (negative base and a bonus) leads 72% of its sets without a lever; O2b's 83%. Production's sacrifice sets lead with the sacrifice (+30) 10 of 14. O2b's 14 reward sets lead with the tempting option 13 of 14: the reward (-30) takes the third option's place, where production had a sacrifice in 14 of 63 sets against O2b's 6 of 32. So the tilt rides on the reward count.
  - **The extra reasoning:** median reasoning tokens on these steps, production 1,754 (adopted3; 1,853 under adopted2), arm O (the stats line alone, beside production) +7%, O2 +14%, O2b +21% (+17% on its sets without a reward, +33% with one). The invitation is deliberated over on every step it is sent, taken or not; the stat-variety line costs the rest.
- **The variant** (`storyTextRounds/optionsO2c.ts`, `turnO2c`): production's live single-player turn (not the frozen base O2b stood on, so it carries every later adoption) with, on a rolled chapter step, O2b's stat lines and risk-only weak example word for word, and a lever line the game computes (`o2cLeverRule`): a reward only on the **reward turn** (`o2cRewardTurn`: the chapter's first step, where the player's previous chapter offered no reward, offered chosen or not, and some stat allows one; "Sacrifice or reward: offer a reward this turn if a stat allows one. No sacrifice this turn."); elsewhere "No reward this turn." with a sacrifice where B6's rate fits ("a sacrifice fits this turn if a stat allows it"), the strong-reason clause where the rate fits after a chapter sacrifice (only a four-step chapter's last step, or after options a played step typed as exploration), and today's "none this turn" where the rate gives none.
- **My calls on the design:**
  - **Which chapters:** the previous chapter offered no reward, so never two running. With a firm line taken every time that is one chapter in two; taken in a share u of chapters, u/(1+u) per chapter (u 0.6-1.0: one in 2.7 to one in 2), the brief's "about one in two or three". "Neither of the last two chapters" would give one in three to four, below the brief's 4-6 per 32 sets (32 rolled sets are about 10-11 three-step chapters).
  - **Which step:** the chapter's first, one invitation a chapter at a fixed place: the only way to cut the invitations, and their reasoning, without the turn counting anything; the reward's lower odds fall where the chapter opens, not on its decisive later steps. A middle step would invite as often in play but on 16 of the 32 stored steps (13 of them chapter 1's step 2), so the stored measurement would over-read the rate; the first step falls on 5 of 32 stored steps, about the share it takes in play (a third of rolled steps, half the chapters).
  - **A firm line on the reward turn** ("offer a reward"), so the game's placement decides and not the model's appetite (O2b's "fits" was taken 54% of the time); "if a stat allows one" keeps the model's judgment where the scene has no fit. No sacrifice beside it: one lever a turn.
  - **The strong-reason clause** only where B6's rate allows a second sacrifice: it can only tighten there (production's line invites a lever freely); O2's clause, sent where the rate gave none, read as leave and O2b dropped it. The brief lists "strong reason for a second sacrifice" as part of O2b, which it was not; this is where it can bind.
  - **Built on production's live turn**, read against production beside it in the same minutes, and O2b's stored replies as the second reference (first tries, as O2 and O2b ran: no checked retry).
- **The stage** (`options-o2c`, under `adopted20`, interleaved, production's single-player limits): capped at $0.85 (the run and its judge about $0.48 plus 30%, and room for one fix-and-retest; the ledger $41.09 of $45). Report `--options-o2c` (`optionsO2cPrep.ts`). Read before paying: the dry run (128 jobs, est $0.47) and five requests end to end with their diffs against production (a reward turn on each story, a sacrifice-fits step, the strong-reason step, a none step): only the two stat lines, the weak example and the lever line differ, the JSON schema is production's; nothing contradicts. Noted before the run: the plan's lever question still frames a lever as optional ("Otherwise, simply say 'None'"), the group-levers stage's cause, the fix-and-retest's candidate if the reward turn's uptake came in low (it didn't).
- **The run** (10:49-11:05 UTC): a 2-job smoke (`cont-checkpoi-t1-o0`: O2c's reward, production's sacrifice), then 126; 128 jobs as the dry run counted, every first reply valid, 4 network errors re-sent; then the judged checks on all 128 turns (a 4-record smoke first).
- **Readings (`options-o2c.md`), O2c against production on 64 pairs:**
  - **Rewards:** 6 → 10 of 64 sets, 5 a sample of 32, in the target band (not moved against production, which is not the target's direction); on the reward turn 10 of 10 (production 2 of 10, moved higher); anywhere else 0 of 54 (production 4 of 54, moved lower, p 0.059). Against O2b's stored sample: 14 → 5 of 32 (moved lower), off the reward turn 13 of 27 → 0.
  - **Sacrifices:** 24 → 12 sets (moved lower), second sacrifices in a chapter 18 → 6 and unreasoned ones 16 → 5 (moved lower; production broke its own "none this turn" on 12 of its 18), sacrifices where O2c's line gives none 13 → 4 of 52.
  - **Variety:** main stats distinct 13% → 47% (moved higher), O2's rule 23% → 47% (moved higher), only-risk sets 11% → 2% (within the noise); against O2b level (50% → 50%).
  - **Reasoning and waits:** reasoning tokens 1,966 → 2,025 mean, median 1,976 → 1,975 (within the noise; O2b's +21% gone, against O2b 2,149 → 1,963, 1.3 SE); chapter-step p95 47.1 → 49.0 s, both over the 45 s allowance at this hour's pace (8.1-8.6 ms a token), by sample 43.7 → 46.6 and 49.0 → 49.1 s (production's own samples 5.3 s apart); p50 35.3 → 36.5 s; openings 44.4 → 43.4 s; cost $0.0035 a turn both.
  - **Judged:** step left open 94% → 97%, concrete progress 84% → 78%, first paragraph narrates the choice 95% → 94%, all within the noise ($0.033).
  - **Automatic checks:** nothing moved the wrong way; `negativeBaseOnBonusShare` 46% → 57% moved higher (the right way); `knownIds` 100% → 95.3% beyond the noise, not moved (3 of 64 replies named an id the story doesn't hold, 2 of them a bonus's stat).
  - **The odds:** a bonus option leads the favorable chance outright 64% → 84% of sets (moved higher; O2b 91% → 88% on its pairs), the tempting one 44% → 69% (moved higher), a lever 30% → 14% (moved lower). Read apart (the same probe, no calls): the leader is mostly the **risky** option (production 39 of 64 sets, O2c 45), since risk raises the favorable chance and the unfavorable one with it; by points alone (base plus bonuses) the tempting option leads 14 of 64 sets in both arms. Within sets without a lever the tempting option leads 62% → 79%: with every normal option drawing on a stat, the sensible one draws on a weaker one, and "bare" options (production's 3 leads) vanish. And O2c's levers lead less because its sacrifices keep B6's rate: most of production's leading sacrifices were second sacrifices against its own line.
- **By hand (every lever of both arms, `options-o2c.md`'s list):** O2c's 10 rewards each turn aside for a gain on the chapter's first step ("Set aside the formal pitch to help residents prepare the kiosk area for a neighborhood meeting; gain the Activists contact.", "Set aside an hour of café prep to experiment with Sophie and gain a new signature recipe: Modern Fusion."); two read oddly ("Build a careful diagnostic model from the exposed logs instead of attempting a live intrusion; gain 10 Resource Credits." — no reason it pays; "Skip paid entertainment … saving 5% financial health" — a cheaper plan, not a turning aside). Its 6 first sacrifices on the café step where the rate fits are ordinary ("Pay to print notices for a neighborhood listening session at the café (-10% financial health)"). Its 2 second sacrifices under the strong-reason clause state no reason in the scene ("Trigger a city-wide distraction to lower City Surveillance by 10% while you enter the channel."), where production, without the clause, offered a second sacrifice in all 6 sets; its 4 sacrifices against "none this turn" none either (production 12).
- **No fix-and-retest (my call):** every target passed (rewards in the band and only where the game places them, variety, fewer and fewer unreasoned second sacrifices, reasoning level). The one reading the brief asked to recover that did not come back to production's level, the "strength option best" share, has no text cause a line could reach: it follows the variety the owner asked for (every normal option a bonus option), the risk type (the risky option leads the favorable chance), and the sacrifice limit (production's leading sacrifices broke its own line). A base-points line tying the tempting option's base to its bonuses would change B6's scale, which by points already leads only 14 of 64 in both arms.
- **Verdict: adopted, `turnO2c`.** Its targets moved and nothing moved the wrong way but the odds reading above, which is the owner's to weigh (it is on the page and in the report).
- **Production** (test-first): `optionRules.ts` holds `THREE_WAYS` with the stat lines and the second weak example (`STATS_DIFFER`, `NEGATIVE_BASE_HOLDS`, `RISK_ONLY_WEAK`), `rewardTurn` and `optionLeverLine` (production's copies of `o2cRewardTurn` and `o2cLeverLine`, a test holding them equal); `BeatPromptService` prints `optionLeverLine` on a single player's rolled step in `sacrificeRewardLine`'s place (a group's players keep the rate line). A single player's rolled step is `turnO2c` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema; every other turn unchanged. `requestFormB9.ts` moves the new line into THIS BEAT as it moved the old (B9 splits production's live form). The variant now builds on production with its lines taken out (`optionsO2cBase`).
- **Kept tests changed on purpose:** `adoptedTurns.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts` hold a single player's rolled step to `turnO2c`, and `turnO2c`'s base to `turnB6` with the deltas and the short-replies lines as before; a single player's 6-8 kids rolled step carries the lines as a logged delta (`withOptionsO2c` in `adoptedDeltas.ts`, unmeasured for kids; `kidsAges`, built on production's live turn, carries them already); `adoptedDeltas.ts` gains `beforeOptionsO2c` (idempotent), which `productionThen` and `productionBeforeKidsAges` apply for the variants measured before; `turnOptionsContinuity.test.ts`'s production-then base, `choiceResultCases.test.ts` (the fake run's rolled steps as production sent them before, from the replayed story) and `playthroughReplay.test.ts` (the round-2 rolled steps as sent on 30 September) read production without the lines; `optionsO2c.test.ts` reads the base as production minus its lines and the variant as production. Test-first: `optionRules.test.ts` could not load before the exports, and `adoptedTurns`, `adoptedForms` and `optionsO2c` failed (11 tests) before the production change; after it 28 comparisons across 8 suites failed (B9's split anchored on the old line; the variants measured before and the replays reading production as it is now) and were moved to production as it stood, or B9 to the new line, on purpose.
- **Unmeasured:** a read-with-kids rolled step with the lines; whether a reward the player takes is paid on the next turn as sacrifices are (the playthroughs paid 7 of 7 levers, none a reward); the reward rate over whole stories (projected: about one chapter in two at the measured uptake, every invited reward taken; lower where a scene has no fitting reward).
- **The page** (`rating/turns-options.html`, key `keys/turns-options-f360d667a8.json`, export `ratings-text-turn-f360d667a8.json`): production against `turnO2c`, sample 1 each under `adopted20`, `--pairwise --criteria options --frames nearer --items 14 --cases …`, the same 14 rolled steps as O2b's page (fixed before reading any output; two of them reward turns), the control `cont-8988006e-t6-o2` (production s1 against s2) and the repeat `cont-6edd813c-t3-o1`, production at A on 7 of 14. O2b's page and key moved to `rating/superseded/turns-options-v2.html` and `keys/superseded/turns-options-v2-941ca730ca.json` (its export still scores). The builder's leak checks clean, no arm name on the page, `--rerender-page f360d667a8` byte-identical. No browser check: Playwright failed to connect this session.

## 2026-10-01: the pacing-clues stage: fix 8's retest in whole short playthroughs, one fix-and-retest, both parts adopted ($1.28)

- **The brief:** fix 8's retest with the full procedure: pacing so the last chapter has a milestone left, setup trigger rules ranked below pacing, and the late-turn clue line, measured blind inside short whole-story playthroughs (about four short stories × 2 arms; stage `pacing-clues`), with a calibrated judged check for unexplained details if feasible; adopt what passes.
- **The cause** (the late-pacing stage, read again): three of the second round's four 25-turn stories had every outcome complete before their last chapter, the chapter planner choosing lengths without the milestones left; the space pirates' 30%-integrity rule gave the complete ship two chapters while the scout's outcome waited; every turn is told to plant a hint and none to pay one off (six to ten small mysteries a story the ending never explained). The late-pacing stage measured the planners and the clue line apart, the clue line by an unblind hand reading and with an unreliable judge (v1).
- **The variant** (`pacingClues`, `storyTextRounds/latePacing.ts`): latePacingB's planners (the chapter planner's paced lengths with their reason, its plan check reading the same; step b keeping a milestone for the last thread, a forced situation and the story's instructions below a player's needed milestones) and, of the turn's lines, only the late part's (no new mystery, an earlier one explained where it fits, the interludes too). **My calls:** the early hint line and the ending line left out (the first had no reading of its own, the second moved nothing, 0 → 1 of 18), so what runs is what would be adopted; the space pirates' switch at turn 14 added as a fourth start (where the threshold rule met pacing; its request is the stored run's byte for byte); the clue judge's one fix, v2 (a new side of a detail the story holds is no new mystery, nor a detail a story element's own description holds, printed beside the facts), calibrated before use and used only if reliable; the hand reading blind (runs coded by a salted hash, no arm, sample or turn printed; all 160 verdicts in `pacingCluesHand.ts` before the key was opened).
- **The stage** (`pacing-clues`, under `adopted21`): capped at $1.30 (about $1.00 plus 30%), raised to $1.50 for the fix-and-retest's eight runs (the reason in `budget.ts`). Requests read end to end before paying; a 2-turn smoke on New Avalon; then sample 1 ($0.391, the smoke's calls reused) and sample 2 ($0.380), 16 runs, every one to its last chapter's plan; the judge's v2 calibration (50 records) and, reliable (20 of 21 with the hand, samples 23 of 25), its run on the 160 window turns (about $0.17).
- **Readings (`pacing-clues.md`):**
  - **Pacing:** the last chapter keeps a milestone, production 2 of 8, `pacingClues` 5 of 8 (within production's 50-point two-sample noise, not moved). The cause of the misses: the food trucks' sample 2 gave both players' one remaining thread to the complete contract at the last switch (the setup's "the final thread must be a Grand Circuit trial"; the plan's own words "bring personal outcomes into the climax without displacing the shared contract"), 2 milestones left unfinished; both space-pirates runs spent the scout's last milestone at the switch at 17 with a thread to spare, so the last chapter was an aftermath on the ship. Step b said both; the planner did not keep them where they decide the last chapter.
  - **Clues:** late player turns with no new unexplained detail, blind by hand 68 of 73 → 80 of 80 (moved, p 0.023; production's 5 new details and 7 partials, the variant none; once it explained a penciled mark the story held); the judge v2 46 of 80 → 72 of 80 (moved, p < 0.001).
  - **Waits and cost:** turn waits p50 44.9 s both, p95 67.5 → 70.3 s; chapter-plan p50 11.3 → 13.7 s, p95 21.1 → 21.7 s; eight runs $0.425 → $0.346.
- **The fix-and-retest, at the cause** (`pacingCluesB`, my call): the same requests, the switch plan check reading the plan against PACING's arithmetic (`switchPacingProblem`: a complete outcome offered to a player with no thread to spare; a spare thread that leaves the last thread nothing to settle, only where a milestone is still needed), worth one retry told the problem, never failing the turn. On the stored runs the rule fires at exactly the switches that lost the last chapter's milestone (production's six, the variant's three) and no other. Retest: `--arms pacingCluesB`, two samples ($0.172, $0.169), every run to its last chapter's plan.
  - **Readings:** the last chapter keeps a milestone 2 of 8 → 8 of 8 (moved, p 0.003); a spare thread kept the last thread's milestone 3 of 10 → 4 of 4 (moved, p 0.035); no complete outcome took a needed thread 21 of 21 → 14 of 14; none unfinished; aftermath chapters before the last 2 of 8 both (B's are the spare threads step b asks for, the space pirates' ship). The check asked once more twice, both at the space pirates' switch at 17, and both retries kept the milestone; the food trucks' complete-contract switch did not recur in B's two runs (the rule fires on it in the stored run; untested in play). Turn waits p50 49.0 s (its own hour; the same turn requests as `pacingClues`, 44.9 s), p95 70.2 s; chapter-plan p50 13.4 s, p95 19.9 s; eight runs $0.342.

## 2026-10-01: the review of the group-levers, options-o2c and pacing-clues work: three findings fixed (no calls, $0)

A review of the day's later stages found three things; each was verified before the fix.

- **1. Group levers invited players to a lever on a roll the owner's-roll rule discards (important).**
  - **What was wrong:** the owner's-roll change (b678206) counts only the owner's roll on a group challenge or contest step on one player's own outcome, the owner in the thread. Group levers (0db76d9, four hours later) gave every rolled player B6's rate line and the lever question "describe exactly one sacrifice or reward option … on one of this player's own stats". For a player who doesn't own the outcome a lever moves a roll the game discards: a reward is a free stat gain, a sacrifice a stat paid for nothing (`paidLevers` charges it whatever the roll), and the dice view tells the player they gained an advantage. Reachable in play: the stage's own case `round-levers-estate-agents-t20` (both agents in a challenge on Nia's own protégé outcome, both lines "fits"); no arm offered a lever there, so the case never showed it, and nothing in the stage's records connected the two rules.
  - **Fixed (test-first):** `ThreadResolutionService.rollingOwner(thread, story)` (public; `rollingSides` reads it) names the owner whose roll alone decides a thread, before anyone rolls; `groupLeverLine` (`optionRules.ts`) gives every other rolled player in that thread "Sacrifice or reward: none this turn." (the measured line's own wording for none; `groupLeversB`'s replies offered no lever where it said none, 0 of 2) in place of the rate line, and `groupSacrificeRewardLines` prints it. My call: "none" rather than leaving those players out of the lever slots, since leaving them out would hand them back the plan question's old optional lever ("… if sensible … Otherwise, simply say 'None'"), which still invites one; with the line they keep the lever fields and the question answers "None". The owner, every player on a shared outcome, and every player on an own outcome whose owner is elsewhere keep the rate line.
  - **Kept tests:** a logged delta on `groupLeversB`, unmeasured (`withOwnersRollLevers` in `adoptedDeltas.ts`, applied by `adoptedTurn`); `beforeGroupLevers` takes production's lines out as production prints them; the eval's `groupLeversBase` takes production's own block out (`groupSacrificeRewardLines`), so the variant still builds as measured. `adoptedTurns.test.ts` gains a group chapter step on player2's own outcome (two and three players) and a test that only the other players' lines change; `groupLeversCases.test.ts` holds production to `groupLeversB` with the delta on the stored cases, which acts on t20.
  - **Test-first:** `optionRules.test.ts` could not load before `groupLeverLine` existed; the new `rollingOwner` test, the two own-outcome kept-test cases, the delta test, the frozen group turns of `adoptedTurns.test.ts` and the stored-case test of `groupLeversCases.test.ts` failed (6 tests in 3 suites); all passed after the change, and the full server suite with them (4,390).
  - **A paid run of production's own group turn** on such a step takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted17`'s production records there are the turn before).
- **2. The clue readings were overstated (important by the review; moderate on verification).**
  - **What was wrong:** the pacing-clues entry above, its commit (37ea9e7), `.context/story.md` and `.context/text-model-eval.md` set the blind hand reading (68 of 73 → 80 of 80) and the judge v2 (46 of 80 → 72 of 80) side by side as two confirmations ("moved by hand and by the calibrated judge"), and labelled v2 "reliable". Turn by turn on the stage's 160 window turns the two disagree on 32 (the review counted at least 30): on the 153 turns both decided they agree 121 times (79%; production 49 of 73, the variant 72 of 80), and by the eval's own rule (`isReliable`: 85% on each side) v2 is not reliable there (the hand's yes side 116 of 148, 78%). Every disagreement is the judge reading a new detail where the hand read none; 8 are the variant's (New Avalon's worktable vibration and pointer and cradle tremors in the model trial, the food trucks' two sealed folders, the unknown time a phrase reached the estate card, "The truth Pip shared remains between them"). The hand's notes read them as the scene's own business ("the pointer trembles with the pulse", "two sealed folders are the decision to come, not a mystery"). So "the variant none" is the hand's reading alone, and the calibration's 20 of 21 doesn't hold on this data. The review's "90% bar" was fix 1's judge's; the eval's per-side bar is 85%, which v2 still misses. The direction (the variant fewer new details) holds in both readings, so the adoption stands.
  - **Fixed (no calls):** `pacingCluesPrep.ts` sets the hand against the judge on the stage's own turns (`handJudgeAgreement`: per arm and pooled, the hand's partial items apart, the reading by `isReliable` with no sample pairs; `handJudgeSection`: the table, the reading and each disagreement with the hand's note and the judge's evidence), and the calibration table now reads "reliable on its calibration items". Test-first for the agreement (the suite could not load before the export); the section's rendering test came after it. `pacing-clues.md` re-rendered with `--pacing-clues-play --report-only` (no calls; only the new section and that label differ). This entry corrects the pacing-clues entry's "moved by hand and by the calibrated judge" (left as written above, as the commit message is); `.context/story.md`, `.context/text-model-eval.md` and the `lateClues.ts` and `lateClues.test.ts` headers now say the judge agrees with the hand on the direction, not turn by turn, and that v2 is not to be used alone on a later stage's turns without a calibration on those turns.
- **3. The owner's option rules hold only for single players, and the group verdict's "rewards at most one a chapter per player" was not so (important).**
  - **What was wrong:** O2c's game-placed reward, the strong reason for a second sacrifice and O2b's different-stats lines reach a single player's rolled step only; a group's players keep B6's rate line (O2c's own deliberate scope, never put to the owner as a gap). That line allows one lever in a chapter of two or three steps but two in a four-step chapter (its first and last steps; after a reward it only prefers a sacrifice, so a second reward is allowed), never looks at chapters (rewards in consecutive chapters are possible), asks no reason for a second sacrifice, and group sets carry no variety lines (only-risk sets 74% under `groupLeversB`, against 2% for O2c's single player; different case sets, so the comparison is rough). The group-levers verdict above says "rewards at most one a chapter per player"; the preference was never read (0 of 0 sets). The O2c report said only "Group turns keep their own lever line".
  - **Corrected and told (no calls):** this entry corrects the verdict; `.context/story.md` (a group's levers: what the line does not do), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (production's turn; the group-levers adoption line), the `optionRules.ts` header, and the O2c report (`DOCS/…/2026-10-01_options-o2c-report.md`, the short version and section 8) say what group stories get and what bringing them under the same rules would take.
  - **Not built, my call:** extending the rules to groups is a production prompt change that needs a measured group variant, and the stored group stories hold almost no earlier levers, so the second-reward and second-sacrifice cases would have to be built; about $0.26 for two samples beside production, capped at about $0.35, within the $45 cap (the ledger $42.87). The owner's feedback was on single-player steps; whether he wants the same for groups is his call, now put to him in the O2c report.
- **Spend:** $0; the ledger $42.87 of $45.
- **Checks:** `npm run check:all` clean (core build; server 4,393 tests; client 276, untouched). No core edits. No browser check: nothing on screen changed. `Privacy.tsx` and `.context/ai-transparency.md`: nothing to change.

## 2026-10-01: the review of the third round's report: eight findings, fixed with no calls ($0)

A review checked the round-3 report against the stored pages and the code; eight findings held (four important, four minor). Each was verified before the fix; the fixes are no-call, test-first where they carry logic. The owner's report is corrected in place (marked "(corrected)") with a new section 12; this entry corrects the round-3 entry above (its "Levers" and "Still wrong" lines), which stays as written.

- **1. Double charges the repair let through (important).** The report counted 11 (7 dropped, 4 list and plural); the pages show at least 14, 7 through. Three more kinds the repair's one-turn, number-only window missed: Tamsin's Composure at estate agents 25 (paid at 23, the repeat at 24 dropped, charged once more at 25, 45 → 35); Oren's Pirate Reputation at space pirates 17 (a ladder step, Known Hand → Feared Name a turn after the reward's Unproven → Known Hand); Davi's Nerve at space pirates 19 (50 → 40 in the reply that offered "Spend 10% Nerve", before he chose it; turn 20 charged it). **Fixed in production** (`leverPayments.ts`): `dropLeversChargedAgain` reads the payments of the chapter's earlier steps on a later step of the same chapter (`paymentsBefore`; a switch turn or the ending still reads only the last beat's, since they apply the stats' adjustments after threads), and a ladder's step (`ladderOf`: a string stat whose possible values list two or more steps, split on commas, semicolons or arrows; `WorkingValues` steps it in rungs, so `paidLevers` records it and the repair drops a repeat); `noteLeversChargedOnOffer` notes (`leverChargedOnOffer`, kept) a change its way on a lever offered in the same reply, by the amount the option names where it names one, no last-beat lever owing it. My call: a note, not a drop, for the third, as the finding proposed: such a change can't be told apart from a story event the stat's rules allow (Nerve's `canBeChangedInBeatResolutions` is true). The chapter window also covers stats that may change in beat resolutions (Composure's is true), since the stored rounds show no legitimate same-size change it would drop. **Replay** (`playthroughReplay.test.ts`): rounds 1 and 2 unchanged (exactly round 2's two, nothing in round 1; no note in either); round 3 drops exactly estate agents 25 and space pirates 17 and notes exactly space pirates 19. Lists and plurals (the report's suggestion 3) stay open. The detail of a chapter-window drop reads "the sacrifice turn 23 paid, earlier in this chapter". `core/types/beat.ts`'s `PaidLever` comment names the ladder step (comment only; core rebuilt).
- **2. Result labels in group prose, unread (important).** Six player turns this round ("The mixed result remains plain in the room", "The unfavorable outcome hangs between you", "the unfavorable finding", "the mixed reading", "The crew's mixed judgment", "favorable attention"), three in round 2's space pirates; no reading covered them (textChecks' `noMetaWords` has favorable/unfavorable but the playthroughs never run `checkBeatSet`). **Fixed:** production notes them (`resultWordsInText`, kept as written; `resultWordsIn` / `resultWordsOfBeat` in `beatRepairs.ts`: favorable or unfavorable in either spelling, "mixed" before result, outcome, reading, judgment, finding or verdict, in a beat's title, text, options and interludes), so the log counts them; the harness reads them on every round (`resultWords`). My call: the review's borderline "the mixed scan" is left out of the pattern. Removing them would be a measured turn line (the request names the kinds and bans none), not built.
- **3. The lemonade's percentage cash (important).** The round-3 setup typed Stand Cash as a percentage (50%, "Spend 10% of Stand Cash"); round 2's had a number; the prose never names a sum. Production's setup rule says money is a number. **Fixed in the harness:** `checkSetupDesign`'s `moneyIsNumber` (a non-opposites stat whose name names money, cash, coins, budget, funds, savings, credits, gold and the like, not a share such as Profit Margin, must be a number; reported only where one exists), read afresh on every round (`setupChecksFailed`): it fails the round-3 lemonade only. **Not built:** a production setup line or retry for it (the fixes report's setup rule for learning stories, about $0.10-0.15), which should come before or beside fix 7's turn line.
- **4. "Different stats, not only risk" fails on most group turns, left out of "Still wrong" (important; the review: "one notch high", the gap documented).** `primaryStatsDistinct` fails on food trucks 15 turns, space pirates 19 of 25, estate agents 16 (round 2: 18, 16, 16); whole sets carry one bonus. The "7 of 7 own stats ended elsewhere" hides back-and-forth (Omar's Service Stamina 45 → 35 → 45 → 35 → 45 → 35). **Fixed (harness and report):** `ownStats[].reversals`, printed from two ("5 turns, 4 of them the other way from the change before"); the report's short version, table, "Still wrong" and section 6 now carry it. **Not built:** O2b/O2c's lines for groups, a measured change and the owner's call (the review of 1 October's estimate, about $0.26, cap $0.35).
- **5. Sacrifices undone at the next chapter close (minor).** The mouse's Cat Attention sacrifice (45 → 55) went back to 45 a turn later; New Avalon 8 → 9 and the space pirates' Oren 24 → 26 the same. **Fixed (harness):** `leversPaid.undone`, a lever paid on its turn whose stat a turn or two later moved back (numbers and ladders the other way, a list holding a removed item again; a lever the player took on that stat in between stops it): five in round 3 (also the lemonade's reward at 3, back at the switch at 5, and the estate agents' Buyer Confidence reward at 10, back at 12), none in round 2. The report's "a real cost" is corrected.
- **6. New Avalon's three "Ari" identities (minor).** The stored `distinctIdentities` failed and nothing printed it. **Fixed in production:** `withDistinctIdentityNames` (`server/src/stories/setupIdentities.ts`, called by `StoryCreationService.generateStoryState` after the start-problem retry): a seat whose three identities share the name they are called by, the premise not naming it (a role never counts), gets one more setup, a fresh sample of the same request (requests unchanged, so no kept test moves); the second is kept only where its names differ and the story can start, else the first; a failed second call keeps the first; the log names seats and an error's class, never a name. The harness's setup does the same (`playthroughs.ts`; the call log records the line as its problem; "setup asked again" says so). My call: a fresh sample rather than a retry told the problem, as production's other setup retry does, so the setup request stays byte for byte the measured one. ## 2026-10-01 (evening): decision A's fix 1, the scoreboard repair finds a board however the setup named it, and drops a score written twice (no calls, $0)

- **What was wrong (round 3's food trucks):** the outcome reads "Scored by Contract Race", the board is the stat `shared_contract_race` named "Innovator's Lead|Circuit Caterer's Lead", and production's `scoreboardOf` matched names only, so the direction repair never saw the board. The ending then set the score twice, `setNumber 30` and `setNumber 70`, each side's share in turn; the game kept 70|30 for Amara while the ending awarded Omar the contract.
- **How setups name the board (a temporary Jest probe over every stored setup reply in `calls.jsonl` and `prep-calls.jsonl`, deleted; no calls):** 112 contested outcomes with "Scored by". On round 3's form (production's since 2026-09-28): 83 by the stat's name, 7 by its id or its id's words (`shared_bounty_claim_score`, `shared_bounty_score`, "Founder Governance", "Charter Mandate", "Lease Advantage", round 1's space pirates' "Black Star Lead", round 3's food trucks' "Contract Race"), 1 with a possessive the id leaves out ("The rivalry is scored by Youssef's Courtship." for `shared_youssef_courtship`), 1 with " stat" after the name. The other 21 are setup rounds 1-2's forms, with no opposites stat (18) or only a meter (3), which must stay unread.
- **The change (`scoreboards.ts`, `beatRepairs.ts`):** `scoreboardOf` reads the name as before, else the one shared opposites stat whose name, id or id without `shared_` equals the written name with case, a possessive's 's, punctuation and a trailing "stat"/"scoreboard" left out; two matches or none name no board, and the story's only opposites stat is never taken by default (the round 1-2 forms' meters). `scoreboardsOf` lists every contest's board. New repair `scoreboardWrittenTwice` (`withoutOtherSidesShare`, before the direction repair, after the number-as-text one): on a board, a value set after another that is 100 minus it is dropped and the first kept. My call: the first is kept, as the other repairs keep the first written; where the first is the loser's share the direction repair then turns it by the same size (70 then 30 after side B won from 45 becomes 20, not 30). Another second value, the same value twice and a meter's values stay as written.
- **What play now does differently:** a board named by its id, its id's words or with a possessive or " stat" is moved only toward the side that won, as a board named by its name already was; a reply that writes both sides' shares keeps the first.
- **On the stored stories (`playthroughReplay.test.ts`, replaying every stored reply on the state its turn saw; rounds 1-3):** round 3's food trucks ending keeps 30 (30|70, Omar's side, the winner's) where the game kept 70; round 1's space pirates' board ("Black Star Lead") is read now, and its one move (turn 9, to 65|35 after the captain's camp won) already went the winner's way; round 1's food trucks turn 22 turned around as before; round 2 named every board by its name, nothing new. Nothing else changes.
- **The harness:** its own board lookup (`boardOf`, the review's finding 7) is production's again (`contestBoardsOf`), its "production's scoreboard repair doesn't read" mark gone; `scoreboardWrittenTwice` joins the fixes that fired. The harness test that pinned the mark changed on purpose.
- **Test-first:** `scoreboards.test.ts` (new; could not load before `scoreboardsOf`) and the four new `beatRepairs.test.ts` cases failed first, then passed; the replay readings were pinned after the code, as confirmations.
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (the scoreboard bullet), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the harness reading).

## 2026-10-01 (evening): decision A's fix 2, a contest made one player's challenge stores that player's side (no calls, $0)

His chapter ended favorable, his camp's win, and the switch turn 10 wrote `addNumber 15`, 50 → 65 toward the route-reader's camp; the board ended there. A cooperative story's two-sided conversion stores it the same way. `scoreboards.ts` reads a contest's result through `boardResultOf` (a contest as before; a challenge with a stored side: favorable its side's win, unfavorable the other's, mixed none), which `scoreboardWinners` and the eval's playthroughs now share (`ContestResult.converted`).

Which camp a seat is in lives only in the setup's text (the playerRoles' "(side B)", which the stored story doesn't keep), and the planner is told player1's camp is side A. Both stored three-player conversions wrote the lone player on his camp's side (rounds 2 and 3, side B). A wrong side there would turn a correct move around; a side read from earlier contests with player1 in them would be safer but needs a contest with that seat first, which the space pirates' round 3 never had before turn 6.

- **On the stored stories (`playthroughReplay.test.ts`):** the three converted chapter plans (round 2's space pirates turn 10, Pip, side B; round 3's food trucks turn 12, Amara, side A; round 3's space pirates turn 6, Oren, side B), checked again from the reply each run used, equal the stored threads but for the side; replayed with the side stored, exactly one move changes: round 3's space pirates turn 10, 50 → 65 turned to 50 → 35, so the seal's board ends 35|65, the salvagers ahead, where it ended 65|35. Round 1 converted none.

## 2026-10-01 (evening): decision A's fix 4, the double-charge repair covers lists and plurals (no calls, $0)

- **What was wrong (round 3, the four double payments the review counted as open):** the estate agents' turn 4 (Tamsin's turn-2 sacrifice burned one of her two Trusted Contacts at turn 3, turn 4 burned the other), turn 24 (Rory's turn-22 reward added a caption card to his Presentation Kit at 23, turn 24 a reading lamp), the space pirates' turn 9 (Oren's Reach Contact reward added Niko Ren at 8, turn 9 a second contact; Davi's "gain 1 Gilded Comet Supply" paid at 8, 4 → 5, and again at 9). The repair read number, percentage and ladder stats only, so a list's payment was never recorded; and "Supply" matched no form of "Gilded Comet Supplies" ("supplie"), so Davi's lever had no stat at all (a temporary probe over the replayed paying and repeating turns, deleted, showed `paidLevers` empty at each list or plural payment).
- **The change (`leverPayments.ts`):** a list stat's payment is one item (`WorkingValues.listStep`: +1 for an item added that the list doesn't hold, -1 for one removed that it holds, as ChangeService applies them by exact text; nothing for a list the same reply both removes from and adds to, a replacement, which the turn's own stat rules ask for as removeElement plus addElement; nothing for a change that changes nothing), recorded on the beat (`paidLevers`), its repeat dropped on the next turn and later steps of the chapter like any other (`dropLeversChargedAgain`); the on-offer note reads lists without an amount, as ladders. A plural name reads in its singular forms (`singularsOf`: the old "s" dropped, "-ies" as "-y", "-ches/-shes/-sses/-xes/-zes" without "es"), in `leverStatOf` and so in every lever reading (shared levers, payments, the note). My calls: a replacement reads as no step, so a swap is never taken for a repeat (it could hide a repeat that swaps; none in the stored rounds); the singular forms are general English, not only "-ies".
- **What play now does differently:** a second item burned, called in or added for one list sacrifice or reward on the turn after its payment, or later in the same chapter, is dropped; a lever whose text names its stat in the singular is read.
- **On the stored stories (`playthroughReplay.test.ts`):** round 3 now drops exactly those four, beside the two the review's fix already dropped (space pirates 17, estate agents 25); rounds 1 and 2 unchanged (round 2's two, round 1 none); the on-offer note unchanged (round 3's space pirates 19 only); the plural reading takes no further shared lever from a player in any round (round 1's one drop is the shared-lever repair's own start).

## 2026-10-01 (evening): decision B, a lower output cap on a single player's chapter-ending turns (no calls, $0)

- **The coordinator's settlement:** decision B as (b): a lower output limit on those turns only, set from the stored answered replies with a stated margin, so a runaway is cut sooner without cutting a normal long turn.
- **The distribution (a temporary Jest probe over `calls.jsonl` and `prep-calls.jsonl`, deleted; no calls):** every single-player turn on Luna medium (judge and backfill calls left out), a closing turn read from its stored prompt (the THREAD RESOLUTION block only a switch after a chapter or an ending carries): 472 closing-turn attempts, 449 answered. Four answered ones were padded with whitespace in their content (the 2026-09-26 uncapped 24,817 and 41,190; a 7,253-token retry of 1 October, after its first try ran away; a 3,781) and are left out, as the 2026-09-27 cap derivation left padded replies out. The 445 clean ones, reasoning included: median 3,442, p90 4,306, p95 4,656, p99 4,994, max 5,853 (round 2's New Avalon switch turn 16, 3,774 of it reasoning, the most any reasoned); since 29 September 278 of them, the same max. For comparison, other single-player turns: 2,124 clean, p99 6,194, max 7,368 (untouched). Cut first tries: 19, every one a single-player closing turn (18 since 29 September), 56-89 s at 12,000 tokens, 134-214 tokens a second (median 173).
- **The limit: `CLOSING_TURN_CAP` = 7,500** (`chatModel.ts`): 28% over the longest clean closing turn and 50% over its p99; no answered closing turn stored would have been cut. My call on the margin: the 2026-09-27 caps sat about 1.8x over the longest reply of any form, which here would be about 10,500 and save little; 1.28x is below that because the population is narrow (the p99 is 1.45x the median) and a cut turn is retried, not lost; 7,000 (1.2x) would save 3 s more each time at a thinner margin. A runaway is now cut 4,500 tokens sooner: about 26 s at the median pace (21-34 s). Groups keep 14,000 and 16,000 (no group closing turn ever ran away).
- **The change:** `productionCallLimits(role, players, { closingTurn })` gives a single player's turn `CLOSING_TURN_CAP` when `closingTurn`; `beatCallLimits(story)` (`storyTextSteps.ts`) reads it from `canAddMilestones` (the ending, or a switch after the first beat); `AIStoryGenerator.generateBeatsResponse` passes it to `modelFor` (a model per limits, cached as before; pregenerated turns take the same path); the eval's `adopted` arm carries it, every other variant the cap by player count it was measured with. The timeout stays 90 s.
- **On the stored playthroughs (`playthroughReplay.test.ts`):** 38 answered single-player closing-turn attempts across rounds 1-3, all under 7,500 (the longest the 5,853 above); the three attempts cut at 12,000 (round 1's New Avalon 5 at 81 s, round 2's mouse ending at 56 s, round 3's New Avalon 17 at 78 s) would have stopped about 30, 21 and 29 s sooner, and round 3's New Avalon 13, which timed out at 90 s with fewer than 12,000 tokens, up to 34 s sooner (none if it ran below 83 tokens a second).
- **What play now does differently:** a single player's chapter-ending turn that thinks until it's cut off is cut about 26 s sooner and retried (round 3's New Avalon 17 would have waited about 87 s instead of 116); nothing else. Unmeasured: the runaway rate, which a cap doesn't change.
- **Test-first:** `chatModel.test.ts` and `storyTextSteps.test.ts` could not load before `CLOSING_TURN_CAP` and `beatCallLimits`; three `jobPlan.test.ts` cases changed on purpose (production's arm on the ending-state and both runaway stages' single-player closing turns now carries 7,500; those stages are closed); the replay reading was pinned after the code.
- **Docs:** `.context/text-model-eval.md` (production text calls, timeouts and output caps).

## 2026-10-01 (evening): the review of decision A's fixes, the lever readings (fix 4's findings; no calls, $0)

- **Finding: a singular form read as any part of the text (fix 4, 2bf2abe).** `singularsOf` added "-ies" → "-y" and "-ches" (and the like) without "es", and `leverStatOf` read every name form with `includes`. So "ability" (for a list stat "Abilities") was found inside "City Stability", and in "your ability" beside a stat the option named. Both stats allowed the lever, so `leverStatOf` returned nothing. The payment then went unrecorded and its repeat undropped, and `repairSharedLevers` kept a group's second copy of a shared City Stability lever. On top of that, "risk 10% stability" now read as Abilities. A stored setup holds exactly that pair (`outputs/d9f4409df26ad39b18f7.json`). The replay missed it because the stored playthroughs hold one plural stat name.
- **The change (`leverPayments.ts`):** a stat's names and its "s"-less form are read anywhere in the text, as before decision A (`statNameWords`). The newer singular forms (`singularWords`) count only as words of their own, four letters or more ("spy" for "Spies" is out), and only where no stat is named that way: they form a tier between the names and the held values. The direction reading (`mentionWords`) keeps every form, since it runs on a stat already chosen.
- **Finding: a list's payment is always ±1 (fix 4).** After a list reward, any new item on that list in the window has the payment's step, whatever caused it: the turn after, and every later step of the chapter. Round 3's contact lists and Presentation Kit allow beats to change them (`canBeChangedInBeatResolutions: true`), so a contact the story adds two steps later went as a repeat. Before 2bf2abe lists were never recorded, so such an add was kept.
- **The change (`dropLeversChargedAgain`, `readLaterInChapter`):** on a later step of the chapter, a list's payment is read only where the stat's rules keep a beat from changing it (`canBeChangedInBeatResolutions` false). On such a stat, a same-step change there can't be a story move the rules allow. On the turn right after the payment it is read as before, since every stored repeat on a list came there (3 → 4, 8 → 9, 23 → 24). Inside that one turn a story add of one item still goes, as a number stat's same-size story move does; that stays accepted and unmeasured.
- **My call: ladders too.** A ladder's step is one rung, so it has the same weakness, and the code now reads both alike. The stored ladder repeat (Oren's Pirate Reputation, 16 → 17) came on the next turn, on a stat beats may not change, so it is dropped as before. Lists and ladders whose rules keep beats away keep the whole window: the space pirates' Reach Contacts (false), and the stored ladders (false).
- **The finding's other remedy, not taken:** reading list repeats only on stats whose flag is false. That would have undone two of round 3's three list fixes (the estate agents' Trusted Contacts and Presentation Kit are true).
- **On the stored stories:**
  - `playthroughReplay.test.ts` is unchanged: round 3's four list and plural drops, Composure's later-step drop, Oren's ladder step, round 2's two drops, the on-offer note, and the one shared-lever drop of round 1.
  - A temporary Jest probe (deleted) read every stored lever option in rounds 1-3 with 2bf2abe's reading and with the new one: 54 options, 0 read differently.
- **Test-first:** two new `beatRepairs.test.ts` cases failed first, then passed:
  - "reads a singular form only as a word of its own, and only where the text names no stat by its name": City Stability with Abilities, "your ability" with Personal Reserve, "stability" alone, and "a new ability";
  - "reads a list's payment on a later step of the chapter only where the stat's rules keep a beat from changing it".
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (the shared-lever reading and the lever repair).

## 2026-10-01 (evening): the review of decision A's fixes, a converted contest's side from the setup's camps (fix 2's finding; no calls, $0)

- **Finding: the planner's written side was trusted (fix 2, 40146e2).** In a three-player game where player1 is not among a converted contest's winners, `boardSideOf` stored the side the planner wrote. Nothing checks that side: the planner is told only that player1's camp is side A. `boardResultOf` reads every converted challenge as oriented. Take camps player1 against {player2, player3}, with player3 written alone on side A. Then player3's favorable result named board side A, and the direction repair would turn a correct 50 → 35 into 65. That broke the guarantee that a correct move is never turned around, and the module's own rule that the planner's sides say nothing when player1 sits out. This was not seen in play: both stored conversions wrote the lone player's camp. I had accepted the risk knowingly at line 2527.
- **Why not the finding's first remedy:** storing a side only where player1 is among the winners, or in a two-player game, would bring back the bug fix 2 closed. Oren is player3 in a three-player game, so his turn-10 move would go uncorrected again.
- **The change, a camp source the stored story lacked:**
  - **The setup's camps are kept.** Setup round 3's form already asks each seat's role line to name its camp, with three players and a contest ("player3: … (side B)", `PLAYER_ROLES_CAMP`). The game dropped those lines with the rest of the plan after generation. `AIStoryGenerator.generateStorySetup` now reads them (`campsOfSetup` → `campsFromSeatRoles` in `scoreboards.ts`), and `createInitialState` stores them as `StoryState.camps` (core: `Camp`, `Camps`, `Story.getRecordedCamp`, `Story.getThreadAnalyses`; `StorySetupGeneration.camps`). The request is unchanged.
  - **When camps are kept:** only where every seat names exactly one camp, player1's is side A, and side B holds a seat. A line without "playerN:" counts as the seat at its place. Two players record none, since their camps are their seats.
  - **How a seat's camp is read (`campOf`):** player1 → A. Player2 in a two-player game → B. Otherwise the camp the setup recorded, else the side the seat played beside or against player1 in the story's earlier contests, where those agree.
  - **`boardSideOf`:** side A wherever player1 is among the winners, else the camp the winners share. Where no camp is known it stores no side, so the challenge's result names no winner (as before 40146e2).
  - **The harness:** its copy, `storyFromSetup`, keeps the camps too.
- **What play now does differently:**
  - A three-player converted contest without player1 is read by its players' recorded camp, not by the side the planner wrote.
  - With no recorded camp and no earlier contest to go on, it is not read at all. That covers a template's three-player story (a template keeps no plan) and a cooperative story (no camps).
- **How often camps are there (a temporary Jest probe over every stored three-player setup reply in `calls.jsonl` and `prep-calls.jsonl`, deleted; no calls):** every contested three-player setup on round 3's form names a camp for every seat: 10 of 10 (`setupR3` 4, `setupR3b` 1, `adopted` 1, the chain 1, the three playthrough rounds' space pirates 3). The pre-round-3 forms never named camps (0 of 11 contested). Round 2's space pirates wrote "side A's sole claimant" and "side B's claimant", which the reading accepts.
- **On the stored stories (`playthroughReplay.test.ts`):**
  - Each run's camps are added from its stored setup, as production would now start the story (`withCampsKept`). All three rounds' space pirates record player1 on side A against player2 and player3 on side B.
  - The three converted plans store the same sides as before: Pip B, Amara A, Oren B. Exactly the one move changes, round 3's space pirates turn 10.
- **Tests:**
  - **Changed on purpose (`planChecks.test.ts`):**
    - The fix-2 case that expected a lone player2 written on side A to store "sideA" now reads the setup's camps, and stores "sideB" whichever side was written.
    - The cooperative two-sided conversion with player1 elsewhere now stores no side; before, it stored the written side A.
  - **Test-first, failed first:** `scoreboards.test.ts` (could not load `campsFromSeatRoles`, `campsOfSetup` and `campOf`), the two `planChecks.test.ts` cases, and `setupChain.test.ts`'s storyFromSetup case (camps kept).
  - **Pinned after the code:** the replay reading (camps per round).
- **Not done, and open:**
  - The camps are not read for a contest player1 sits out. Its sides stay the planner's, unread by the scoreboard repair. Orienting those by the recorded camps would extend the repair; it is unmeasured on stored play.
  - The planner is still not told the camps. Telling it would be a measured prompt change.
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (PL-12 and camps, the scoreboard bullet, PL-11's note), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the harness's `storyFromSetup`).

## 2026-10-01 (evening): decision A, the owner's option rules for group turns, measured and adopted (stage `group-options`, $0.258 of $0.35)

- **The brief:** the owner's option rules for group turns (one reward a chapter, a strong reason for a second sacrifice, options on different stats, not only risk) with the full procedure: the cause first, a group variant at it (the owner's rules, the game-computed lever line per player and chapter), cases where earlier sacrifices exist built from round 3's group stories, measured beside production twice (stage `group-options`, about $0.26-0.35).
- **The cause, read from the third playthroughs' group stories (a temporary Jest probe over `playthroughs-3.json`, replayed with `replayRun`; every group turn's request today's byte for byte; deleted; no calls):**
  - The owner's O2c rules reach a single player's rolled step only (`optionLeverLine`, `THREE_WAYS`). A group's rolled players got B6's rate line (`groupLeverLine`: a lever fits when none was offered in the player's last two challenge turns, the other kind preferred), which never looks at chapters, and a group's option instructions carry no variety line at all.
  - On round 3's 103 rolled group sets: 14 rewards, 8 of them where the owner's rules give none (a chapter's later step after B6's "prefer a reward": the space pirates' Davi and Oren at turn 7, Tomas at 13 and 21, the estate agents' Tamsin at 18 and 25, the food trucks' Amara at 24; or the chapter after one with a reward: Omar at 19); 5 sacrifices on what would be a chapter's reward turn; "prefer the other kind" turned one stat both ways (Omar's Service Stamina 45 → 35 → 45 → 35); 95 sets failed `primaryStatsDistinct` and 78 had two options only risk tells apart (base 0 and the same whole-step bonus, "+10 from the contract race" on all three, or no bonus: "do not invent stat bonuses").
  - The strong-reason state (a second sacrifice B6's rate allows) came once in the round: a four-step chapter's last step after a first-step sacrifice (Tamsin at 25). B6's rate already keeps most second sacrifices out of a group's chapters.
- **The variant** (`storyTextRounds/groupOptions.ts`, `groupOptions`): production's group turn with, on a group's rolled step: each rolled player's line computed per player and chapter as O2c computes a single player's (`groupOptionsRule`: the reward turn on the chapter's first step where that player's previous chapter offered no reward and a stat allows one, no sacrifice there; elsewhere no reward and a sacrifice on B6's rate, a second in the chapter only for a strong reason; none where the rate gives none; none where the step discards the player's roll, as production; O2c's own words); O2b's stat lines and risk-only weak example word for word under a lead for a group's rolled players, after the option examples where a single player's three ways go; and the plan's lever question asked from those lines. **My calls:** not B6's three ways or base-point scale (never measured for groups, and not the owner's rules); the plan question reworded (`groupLeversB`'s "Say 'None' only where the line says none" would have asked for a second sacrifice wherever the strong-reason line stands): one where the line offers a reward or says a sacrifice fits, a second only where the scene gives a strong reason, "None" where it says none.
- **The cases** (`groupOptionsCases.ts`, no calls; `--build-group-options-cases`): twelve group steps of round 3, two and three players, openings and later steps, contests and challenges, where the earlier levers make the owner's rules read differently from B6's rate: the food trucks' 12, 15, 19, 22, 24; the space pirates' 6, 7, 11, 19; the estate agents' 18, 22, 25. Per sample 24 rolled sets: 8 reward turns (5 where B6's rate gives none), 12 where a sacrifice fits and no reward (B6's rate invites either kind on all 12, a reward on 5), the round's one second-sacrifice state, two none, one discarded roll; every rolled player was offered a sacrifice before. Each request the one round 3 sent (production's turn was unchanged since that run).
- **The stage** (`group-options`, under `adopted23`, interleaved, production's checked retry in the loop, deterministic readings, no judge): capped at $0.35 (the status note's estimate's cap; the reason in `budget.ts`; the ledger $43.61 of $48). The report `--group-options` (`groupOptionsPrep.ts`) reads each rolled set against the owner's rules and B6's rate, its lever and stat, second sacrifices, a lever written as a normal option, the variety per set (`optionSetVariety`, newly exported from `turnDesignChecks.ts`) and the odds for that player (`favorableChances` takes a slot now). Read before paying: the dry run (48 jobs, est $0.20) and four cases' requests end to end with their prompt and schema diffs against production (the estate agents' 25, the space pirates' 19 and 11, the food trucks' 12): only the variety block, the players' lines and the plan question differ; nothing contradicts (the strong-reason line beside `NO_DOUBLE_SACRIFICE` means a second sacrifice on another stat, as a single player's).
- **The run** (20:06-20:15 UTC): a 2-job smoke (the space pirates' turn 11), then 46; 48 jobs as counted, every reply valid, one retry of a short first reply an arm.
- **Readings (`group-options.md`), 48 sets an arm:** rewards off the reward turn 9 of 32 → 0 (moved lower, p 0.001); rewards on the reward turn 4 of 16 → 11 (moved higher, p 0.016); sacrifices on it 0 → 0; sacrifices where one fits 9 of 24 → 19 (moved higher, p 0.004); levers where the rules give none 0 of 6 both; main stats distinct 1 → 11 of 48 (moved higher, p 0.002) and O2's rule 2 → 19 (moved higher); only-risk sets 36 → 18 (moved lower, p < 0.001); second sacrifices none either arm; levers 22 → 30 and rewards 13 → 11 (within the noise); the odds level (mean favorable chance 29.2% → 30.5%); reasoning tokens 180 → 235 a turn (beyond the noise, not moved, 1.8 SE); first-reply waits p50 40.1 → 41.8 s, p95 53.4 → 55.1 s; cost +1% a call. Automatic checks: `leverFollowsRateLine` and `leverFollowsChapterLine` moved lower by design (they read the lines the owner's rules replace: the variant's rewards on reward turns where B6's rate says none), as O2's run read them; result words in three of the variant's turns and two stat readouts, beyond the noise but not moved (below).
- **By hand (every lever of both arms, and the strong-reason state's plans):** the variant's 11 rewards all on reward turns, 7 a turning aside ("Let your crew handle the routine anchoring while you rest your hands and regain 10% Service Stamina"), 4 a gain for doing the scene's own thing (a public commitment raising Pirate Reputation, a recipe quality check raising Craft Reputation twice), as two of O2c's single-player rewards were; its 19 sacrifices ordinary, on own stats but two (Buyer Confidence; "Spend 1 Supply", the crew's Gilded Comet Supplies, which the game's text reading doesn't find); production's 9 off-turn rewards all where B6 preferred a reward. At the strong-reason state the variant's plans declined twice ("the final scene gives no strong reason for another sacrifice, and no reward this turn"); production offered a reward there once. Three times at the space pirates' turn 7 the variant wrote its sacrifice as a normal option at +30 ("Hold your nerve … (-10% Nerve)"), which the game counts and never charges (production 1 of 48, the group-levers stage 1 and 2 of 50: an old failure, not moved). The three result-word turns ("The favorable impression settles over the room") are the known group leak decision A's result-words line addresses; none sits in the variant's new lines.
- **No fix-and-retest (my call):** every target moved and nothing moved the wrong way but the two checks that read the replaced lines. The residue: 13 of the variant's 18 only-risk sets are the space pirates', whose stats give no bonus at their values in those scenes ("do not invent bonuses"), so the options sit at base 0 and differ only in risk; the next lever would be a base-point line for groups (B6's scale), unmeasured for groups and beyond the owner's rules. The second-sacrifice clause had one state, twice; thin but followed.
- **Verdict: adopted, `groupOptions`.**
- **Production (test-first):** `optionRules.ts` holds `GROUP_OPTION_VARIETY` (O2b's lines from the single player's constants under the group lead), `groupRewardTurn`, `groupLeverRule` and `groupLeverLine` (the owner's rules per player and chapter, the strong-reason wording shared with `optionLeverLine` through `strongReasonLine`, the single player's line byte for byte as before), and the reworded `GROUP_LEVER_QUESTION`; `BeatPromptService` prints the variety block after the option examples on a group's rolled step. A group's rolled step is `groupOptions` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema; every other turn unchanged. The variant builds on production with its lines taken out (`groupOptionsBase`, `withoutGroupOptions`; `groupRateLine` is the line production printed before), and `groupLeversBase` takes out the variety block too, so both still build as measured.
- **Kept tests changed on purpose:** `adoptedTurns.test.ts` (`expectGroup`: a group's rolled step is `groupOptions`, its base `groupLeversB` with the deltas and the short-replies lines; the lever-parts and owner's-roll tests read the new lines and question) and `adoptedForms.test.ts` (the frozen group rolled steps against `groupOptions`, its base checked against `groupLeversB` as before); `adoptedDeltas.ts` gains `beforeGroupOptions`, which `productionThen` applies first; `optionRules.test.ts` (the group lines' expectations: a sacrifice fits and no reward where B6 said one fits; a new block holding production's functions and texts equal to the measured variant's), `groupLevers.test.ts` (production before the group-options adoption is the measured `groupLeversB`), `groupLeversCases.test.ts`, `choiceResultCases.test.ts` and `playthroughReplay.test.ts` (round 2's group rolled steps read production as it stood, `beforeGroupOptions`), `groupOptions.test.ts` and `groupOptionsCases.test.ts` (the variant is production now; the base and `playthroughs3Sent` production before). Test-first: `optionRules.test.ts`, `groupOptions.test.ts` and `groupOptionsCases.test.ts` could not load before the exports, and `adoptedTurns` and `adoptedForms` failed (10 tests) before the production change; after it 5 comparisons in 5 suites failed and were moved to production as it stood on purpose.
- **Unmeasured:** a read-with-kids group's rolled step, which carries the lines too (`kidsAges` is built on production's live turn); whether turns pay a group player's lever on the next turn (unchanged); the reward rate over whole group stories (projected about one chapter in two per player at the measured uptake).
- **A paid run of production's own group rolled step** takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted23`'s production records are the group turn before).
- **Spend:** $0.258 of the stage's $0.35 (the smoke $0.011); the ledger $43.86 of $48 as the dry run prints it (about $45.16 if the stalled Stage 4 calls were billed). The build commit's message (e11e8d6) rounds it to $43.87.

No browser check: nothing on screen changed (Playwright failed to connect this session). **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (a group's levers: the owner's rules), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (production's turn, the kept tests, the variant, the cases and report modes, the stage's cap and spend, its arms and readings; the stale "`--global-cap` can only lower the $45" now $48). `Privacy.tsx` and `.context/ai-transparency.md`: nothing to change (no new tool or practice).

## 2026-10-01 (evening): decision A, shared scenes in group stories, measured and adopted where it passed (stage `scenes`, $0.372 of $0.40)

- **The brief:** consistent shared scenes across players in group stories (a person in one place per turn across all players' texts): a retest of the parallel-thread lines with the cause re-read from round 3, the state rendering (where each person was last placed) considered beside wording, the calibrated judged check `placesConsistent`, measured on round-3 group chains and turns twice (stage `scenes`, about $0.15-0.20 in the status note).
- **The cause, re-read from round 3 (the story pages and the replayed requests; no calls).** Every split came from a thread one player picked that held a player of another thread, or one of the people another thread needed:
  - the estate agents' turns 6-7: Tamsin picked "Meet Rory away from Gloam House" (the shared friendship), Rory the conservatory staging; the chapter planner wrote Tamsin's thread with Rory in every step and result ("Tamsin and Rory agree …"); her text sat Rory across the Amber Cup's table while his own restaged the conservatory. Turns 9-11 the reverse (Rory's "Return to the Amber Cup", Tamsin at the café in his text and with the buyer and Mara in her own).
  - the food trucks' turns 12-15: the scarce-slot contest, Amara's pick alone, planned with both trucks in its steps ("How do Amara and Omar serve the first guests"), made her challenge by `contestOneSided` with Omar in its results; her text put Omar's station and crew across the inspection lane while his own loaded the truck with Jo in the yard.
  - the space pirates' turn 6: Oren picked the switch's overlap direction "Join Tomas and Davi at the chart table", the others their own outcomes; his text has him join them there while Davi's is at the auxiliary engine's service panel.
  - newly found: the estate agents' turn 17, the buyer at the nursery door beside Rory in his text and at Noor's plan table in Tamsin's (both threads' steps name "the buyer").
  - In production's requests: the chapter planner follows the picks, and its "Possible player configurations" say nothing about where parallel threads happen or who is in them; a thread's own `plan` field is stored and never shown to a turn, so fix 4's "each thread's plan says where it happens and who is there" could never reach one; the group turn sees each thread's players and steps, and its consistency lines cover only players "in the same thread or switch". Fix 4 measured its lines on three chapter openings: production's own two samples were 67 points apart on the judge, so nothing could move.
- **The variant** (`sharedScenes`, `storyTextRounds/sharedScenes.ts`, eval only, test-first; production's request byte for byte elsewhere, a test holds it on every frozen case): (1) the chapter planner where the picks set more than one outcome: fix 4's parallel line reworded at round 3's cause (every player in exactly one thread even where a pick names another player or the outcome is shared with or contested by their side, so the thread plays without them, reaching them by message or leaving them for later; everyone else and every key object in one thread's scene; a shared place moved for everyone), and **a scene for each thread** in the reply right after its players (where it happens, who and what is there besides its players), which the plan keeps: the state rendering, since the turn never saw a thread's plan; (2) the group turn on a chapter step with several threads: "WHERE EVERYONE IS THIS TURN" before the thread configuration (each thread's players by name and its scene where the plan holds one; a player only in their own thread's scene, never shown in another's text even where a choice or a step names them; everyone else in one scene at a time), and fix 4's consistency line pointing at it. My calls: no one-sided-contest line of its own (fix 4's backfired; the presence clause covers a contest's absent side), no switch change (the overlap direction that names another player is a switch-level cause too, left for later), the NPC places as a planner-written scene rather than names matched in earlier texts (a name in a text is not presence).
- **The cases** (`sharedScenesCases.ts`, `--build-scenes-cases`, no calls), from round 3's stored runs, each request the one the round sent: chains on the chapter openings where a person came to be in two places (the estate agents' 6 and 17, the food trucks' 12, the space pirates' 6) and an ordinary one (the food trucks' 9); turns on the plans the round stored (the estate agents' 10 and 18, the food trucks' 14, the space pirates' 9) and an ordinary one (the space pirates' 16).
- **The check:** fix 4's `placesConsistent` v2, unchanged, recalibrated on 13 round-3 turns read by hand before any judge call (7 no, 5 yes, 1 partial; the buyer at the estate agents' 17 a new find) beside round 2's 18 stored verdicts: round 3 alone 11 of 12 (one false pass, the food trucks' 15, Omar's station "across the bay" read as not Omar), with round 2's 29 of 30, samples 30 of 32: **reliable**.
- **Run** (21:13-21:36 UTC, under `adopted24`, interleaved, the turns with production's one checked retry): requests read end to end before paying; a 2-job turn smoke (the food trucks' 14) and a 2-chain smoke (the estate agents' 6), then the rest; 40 jobs, every reply valid; seven chain planner calls hit production's 30 s timeout (the space pirates' 6 at sample 2 three times in both arms at one slow moment, the food trucks' 12 once), each answered on the runner's retry.
- **Readings, `sharedScenes` against production:**
  - **Chains** (the planner, then its group turn on the chapter's opening; 5 cases ×2): 5 of 10 → 9 of 10 consistent on the judge, **moved higher (p 0.070)**; by hand 5 → 7 of 10. Every chain it left split was its own planner writing one person or group into two threads' scenes: the space pirates' Captain Ves in all three scenes in both samples, and at the food trucks' 12 "a rival showcase crew and its truck" at the opposing station while Omar's own crew and truck stood in the loading area. The plan proxy (no thread's steps or results name another thread's player) 5 → 7 of 10, within the noise. `waitingCloseShare` 9.1% → 36.4%, moved higher (the wrong way).
  - **Turns alone on the round's stored plans** (5 later chapter steps ×2): 5 → 4 of 10 on the judge, 4 → 4 by hand, within the noise: a block cannot undo a plan that puts one person in two threads (both arms' splits are the plans' own). `openingNotReused` on the kept turns 100% → 60%, **moved lower (p 0.043)**; on the first replies 90% → 60%, beyond the noise, not moved: the variant's turns re-anchored their opening on the last beat's ("You keep Noor's comparison sheet beside the original plan" after "You lay Noor's comparison sheet beside the original plan…"), four of ten kept turns against none.
- **The fix-and-retest (`sharedScenesB`, my call, its cause the chains' own splits):** one planner bullet before the scene bullet: when two threads would need the same person (a captain, a buyer, a judge) or the same crew, vehicle or object, only one thread's scene has them, the other plays without them or reaches them by message; a contest only one side chose stands no one in for the absent side. Its turn is the variant's. Its chains twice on the five openings (cap raised from $0.36 to $0.40 for it, recorded): **9 of 10 on the judge, moved higher (p 0.070)**; **8 of 10 by hand**: the showcase without a stand-in in both samples (the judge's one fail there is a false one: Amara's own truck under the arch, Omar's at the crossing), the space pirates' 6 still split in both samples, its plan keeping Ves in two of the three scenes, Tomas's and Oren's, where the variant had her in all three; the judge passed both (the ready room and the chart table never named as apart). Proxy 6 of 10, within the noise; no automatic check moved (`sentences` 100% → 70% beyond the noise, p 0.105; `openingNotReused` 100% → 100%). Planner median about 21 → 24-25 s; three-player planners about 29 s in every arm, near production's 30 s timeout; cost about +2% a chain.
- **Verdict (my call): adopted where it passed.** The chains passed under the stop rule in the fix-and-retest's form, so production takes `sharedScenesB`'s chapter planner (the line with the one-scene bullet, each thread's scene kept on the plan) and its turn on a **chapter's opening step**, the turn the chains measured. A later chapter step keeps production's turn: measured alone there, the block moved no split and told openings again more often (moved lower on the kept turns), so it did not pass. I found that `openingNotReused` reading only while adopting (the run's report lists it under the turns' automatic checks), so the first adoption, which put the block on every chapter step, was narrowed before any commit.
- **Production (test-first):** `game/services/sharedScenes.ts` (`SHARED_SCENES`, `takesScenesPlanner`, `takesScenesBlock`: a group chapter's opening step with more than one thread, `sceneOf`, `scenesBlock`, `withSceneField`); `ThreadPromptService` prints the planner line; `plannerReplies.ts` asks each thread for its `scene` where the planner line applies and `storedThread` keeps it; core's `Thread.scene` (core rebuilt); `BeatPromptService` prints the block before the thread configuration and the consistency line. Production equals `sharedScenesB` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema, on the chapter planner and a chapter's opening, and its turn as before on a later step. The variant builds on production with the insertions taken out (`sharedScenesBase`, `withoutSharedScenes`, `withoutSceneField`; `withSharedScenesLines` puts them on any form).
- **Kept tests changed on purpose:** `storyTextRounds/sharedScenes.test.ts` (the variant against its base; production is `sharedScenesB` on built stories and every frozen planner and opening case, its base on every frozen later step); `adoptedPlanners.test.ts` and `adoptedForms.test.ts` (the chapter planner and a chapter's opening read with the insertions, `withSharedScenes` and `withSharedScenesSchema` in `adoptedDeltas.ts`); `adoptedTurns.test.ts` (`asAdopted` likewise); `adoptedDeltas.ts` gains `beforeSharedScenes`, applied first in `productionThen` and in `productionBeforeKidsAges`; `groupLeversBase` takes the insertions out on a rolled step, `playthroughs3Sent` rebuilds round 3's requests without them; `groupLevers.test.ts`, `groupLeversCases.test.ts`, `groupOptionsCases.test.ts`, `resultsAsOutcomes.test.ts`, `playthroughReplay.test.ts` and `sharedScenesCases.test.ts` read production as it stood before or with the insertions as measured. Test-first: the opening-step condition's two tests failed before the change; the first adoption moved 14 tests in 12 suites (one the stage's cap), each updated on purpose.
- **Unmeasured:** a read-with-kids group's chapter opening, which carries the block too (`kidsAges` is built on production's live turn); scenes on whole stories (the next playthroughs will show whether a chapter's later steps stay consistent from its opening); the switch's overlap direction that names another player ("Join Tomas and Davi at the chart table"), left as it is.
- **What is left, for later:** a person every thread wants (the space pirates' captain) still lands in two scenes; now that each thread writes its scene, a plan check could flag a named character in two scenes and retry once with the problem (no calls to build; unmeasured).
- **A paid run of production's own chapter planner or group chapter opening** takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted24`'s production records are the forms before).
