## 2026-10-01 (evening): decision A, a contest's deciding chapter decides it (the seal), measured and adopted (stage `contest-settled`, $0.0766 of $0.20)

- **The brief:** a complete contest told as settled (the space pirates' command seal: complete by count, but its milestones only settled who may shape the discussion; two endings called it unassigned): find whether the cause is the planner's stage plan for a contest's last stage or the ending, fix there, measure on built cases twice (stage `contest-settled`, about $0.30 in the status note).
- **The cause: the plan of the contest's last stage, not the ending** (read from round 3's stored plans, requests and story page, and every stored last-stage plan of rounds 1-3 by a temporary Jest probe over `playthroughs.json`, `-2.json` and `-3.json`, replayed; deleted; no calls):
  - The seal ("Which camp will decide the fate of the Star-Crown command-seal?"; Side A or B takes custody, mixed: Ves's custody under a shared-use pact; 2 milestones). Its first chapter (turn 6) named the stages "1. the camps establish their first claims; 2. one camp takes custody, or both agree to Ves's shared-use pact" and played stage 1 as it should. Its turns then recorded facts that the seal stays unassigned while the camps argue ("The chart-table record can mark which camp is invited to guide a later discussion without assigning custody"). The switch at turn 10 grouped the deciding stage, as `CONTEST_LAST_STAGE_LINE` asks, but its flavor question put it off: "… and what concrete terms will make its case credible without assigning custody or binding the crew?". The chapter planner, told in PACING "this thread settles stage 2 of 2, the last", renamed stage 2 "the crew's confidence settles on a camp to shape the next custody discussion, without assigning custody", asked "which camp will earn the crew's confidence to frame the command-seal's next custody discussion", and wrote three milestones that each put the decision off. Turn 14 wrote the mixed one; the endings told the seal as its milestones left it, which is what the owner's decisions ask of an ending. The request's own lines pull that way: the thread's question is "nearer than its outcome's", "never the outcome's question again", and nothing says the nearer question's answer at a contest's last stage is the contest's result.
  - The same round's food trucks' contract (turn 17, Omar's pick alone): a brace check whose stage "informs the final Festival Circuit contract decision"; the contract was named by the aftermath chapter's fourth milestone.
  - Every other stored contest last stage settled its contest (6 of 8 in rounds 1-3); of about 38 other outcomes' last stages one put its question off (the estate agents' advocate standing), so the variant reads contests only (my call).
- **The variant** (`storyTextRounds/contestSettled.ts`, `contestSettled`, eval only, test-first; production's request byte for byte elsewhere): where a pick sets a contested shared outcome whose thread settles its last stage (`contestsDecidedHere`: a game that plays contests, one milestone still needed, whoever picked it), PACING carries before "Recent threads:" one line per such outcome: "Deciding thread: <id> ("<question>") gets its last milestone here, so this thread decides the contest", its last stage the outcome's own question, its three possible milestones its three resolutions (listed) as the thread's situation settles them; the question stays about the situation, but each milestone settles what its resolution settles, none puts the decision off to a later discussion, vote, review or agreement or settles only who may shape it; a switch question, earlier milestone or fact saying otherwise was written before this last stage; and, where not every player picked it, a thread holding one side only is that side's challenge (favorable its resolution, unfavorable the other side's). My calls: the line in PACING (computed, only where it applies) rather than in the stage item; no switch-planner change (the chapter planner writes the milestones; a deferring switch question is one of the cases); the completing turn and the ending untouched (they followed the milestones). Read before paying: the seal's whole request and every case's inserted line; one change made before the smoke: "no milestone leaves the outcome's question open" became "each possible milestone settles what its resolution settles", since three setups' mixed resolutions themselves leave something unresolved ("leaving the full contract undecided").

Beside it a word heuristic (`deferralWords`), which reads the seal's three milestones and none of the six settled plans' but cannot read a milestone about something else (the food trucks' brace check); no judge (none calibrated for this, and 32 plans read by hand).

Production's chapter retry catches that problem; logged as the one reading to watch.

## 2026-10-02: decision A, money that moves in a learning story, measured and adopted (stage `money-2`, $0.3623 of $0.39)

- **The brief:** the third playthroughs' lemonade stand never sold a cup and its cash never moved; its setup typed Stand Cash as a percentage. A setup rule (counted resources as numbers, not percentages, where the premise is about money or counts) and fix 7's turn line if still needed; setups ×2 on learning premises and turns on built cases (stage `money-2`, about $0.25-0.30 in total).
- **How it ran, and the interruption.** The stage ran in full before the workflow was interrupted: the setups and short playthroughs 2026-10-01 23:41-23:53 UTC (a one-turn smoke of the lemonade, both arms, then the rest), the turn line 2026-10-02 00:08-00:12 (a 2-job smoke, then 30), its fix-and-retest 00:41-00:46 (a 1-job smoke, then 7); the blind hand readings were in `money2Hand.ts` and the report (`money-2.md`) was written at 00:52. Nothing was committed and this log had no entry; the adoption had been started test-first (the `adoptedSetup.test.ts` and `StoryCreationService.setupStatus.test.ts` tests written, failing). The interrupted run's own notes on reading the requests and on its dry runs were lost with it; the call records show the three smokes. Resumed with no calls ($0): `armsBudget.test.ts` still failed (its list of stages that carry the checked retry missed `money-2`; fixed), then the verdict and the adoption below.
- **The cause, setup:** production's setup request says money is a number (the stat type catalogue), but every example a setup copies is a percentage ('Spend 10% fuel', 'Above 70%: +10', the worked example's meters), and the adjustments field asks every stat for "one or two changes after threads, each keyed on the kind of thread and its result". So a setup's money moves by a fixed step for how a thread went, not by what the story pays and earns: 2 of the 5 stored lemonade setups typed the cash as a percentage (setup round 3's sample 1, the third playthroughs'), and all 5 moved it after threads by a fixed step or by "the thread's recorded profit", which no turn records.
- **The cause, turn (read from the stage's own played turns):** with the money setup's number stats, production's turn still left the text's payments and sales out of the stat changes in 5 of the 7 played lemonade turns whose text paid or sold ("You pay $3.55 for the supplies, add the $2 permit" with the cash unmoved; "a few more neighbors buy cups … two more coins than before", unmoved; a 75-cent sale moving the cash by a reward's 2 coins). Nothing in the turn ties the money the text pays and earns to the stat that counts it; fix 7's diagnosis stands.
- **The variants** (eval only, test-first; production's request byte for byte elsewhere):
  - `moneySetup` (`storyTextRounds/moneySetup.ts`): on a learning story (`SetupInput.learning`, the setup form's learn-something category), one line as the "This setup" block's last: money and counted things (stock, supplies, people, animals) in number stats in their own units, never percentages, effects, thresholds and levers in those units; adjustable anytime, moving by exactly what the story pays, spends, uses up, sells or earns in the beat where it happens; no fixed amount after a favorable or unfavorable thread, no payment put off to a thread's end; no figure worked out from others (a margin, a price per item) as a stat. Its examples come from no measured premise.
  - `moneyTurn` (`moneyAddsUp.ts`): fix 7's block B and its thread-resolution line, word for word as measured on 2026-10-01, on production's turn of the day. `moneyTurnB`, the one fix-and-retest: one line after the amounts line (a sale, purchase or fee the text tells in a summary is named and counted too; a cost only worked out moves nothing; no sale left out of the scene to keep the changes simple).
- **The cases:** five learning premises ×2 a setup arm (`MONEY_2_SPECS`: the lemonade at 10 turns, a campaign's budget, wolves and deer, a shared eco business, two players; peer review as the control, about neither), each lemonade setup played on through its first chapter (5 turns) on production's planners and turns; eight turn cases from those runs (`money2Cases.ts`, `--build-money-2-cases`, each request the one the run sent): the money setup's two runs at turns 3-5 and production's setup's second run at turns 4-5. Retest cases (`MONEY_2_RETEST_CASES`): the four where the first form's replies still failed or kept a sale out, and production's supplies-and-permit turn.
- **The check:** blind hand readings (`--money-2-blind`, `--money-2-turns-blind`; verdicts by code in `money2Hand.ts` before the keys were opened), no judge (fix 7's `figuresAddUp` judge read unreliable). Beside them the setup flags (`moneyIsNumber` and the stage's counted-stat flags).
- **Readings (`money-2.md`):**
  - **Setups, by hand:** counted in number stats that move by what the story pays and earns, production 0 of 7, `moneySetup` 8 of 8, **moved higher (p < 0.001; noise 0)**; the control 2 of 2 both (nothing counted forced in). Flags: levers in units 3/7 → 8/8 (moved, p 0.026), no fixed step after a thread 2/7 → 8/8 (moved, p 0.007), counted stats as numbers 3/7 → 7/8 and adjustable anytime 5/7 → 7/8 (beyond the noise, not moved), money-named stats as numbers 3/6 → 5/6 (within the noise; the one left, the president's "Council Budget Climate", is a stance beside a counted fund). Reasoning tokens 88 → 131 (moved higher); waits p50 65.8 → 70.3 s (the longest call 78.4 → 91.7 s, inside the 120 s setup timeout; one setup an arm asked twice); cost $0.0057 → $0.0061 a setup. Odd but counted: the wolves-and-deer setups' levers on animal counts ("Record 3 fewer estimated deer" as a sacrifice).
  - **Played turns** (production's turns on each arm's setup, 10 an arm): money adds up 8/10 against 7/10, names a sum 3/10 against 4/10 (within the noise). With the money setup both lemonade runs sold cups in their first chapter; with production's setup one run sold nothing in five turns and the other paid supplies and a permit and told its sales without figures.
  - **Turn line:** `moneyTurn` against production on the eight cases, adds up 10/16 → 13/15 (one partial left out; within the noise, 25 pts), names a sum 11/16 → 9/16. Its failures: sales and a fee told in a summary with no figures (the fair, both samples), "a few small purchases follow", an ingredient cost subtracted that the text only worked out; and on one step both samples kept the waiting customer from buying (adding up by leaving the sale out).
  - **The fix-and-retest** `moneyTurnB` on its four cases: adds up 3/8 → 7/8, **moved higher (p 0.059)**; names a sum 8/8 → 7/8 (not moved). Its one failure: the fair's switch turn, sample 2, told sales and the fee with no figures. Reasoning tokens 3,121 / 3,537 / 3,403 a turn (within the noise); waits p50 40.3 / 47.2 / 50.2 s, p95 73.9 / 70.4 / 61.6 s, inside the 90 s turn timeout; no retries; cost $0.0040 → $0.0042 a turn.
  - **How far the retest reading goes:** production's 3/8 comes from the first run's records on those four cases (the same prompt state, not re-run); the cases were chosen where the first form failed and, one of them, where production failed both samples, so the comparison is somewhat in the variant's favour; 8 replies an arm.
- **Verdict: both adopted.** `moneySetup`: the target moved and nothing moved the wrong way. `moneyTurnB`: it passed the stop rule on its cases after one fix-and-retest at a specific cause (summaries without figures); the first form alone did not.
- **Production (test-first):** the setup: `setupPromptText.ts` `MONEY_SETUP_LINE`; `SetupPromptOptions.learning`; `StorySetupPromptService` prints the line after the "This setup" block where the flag is set (a story only; template drafts and AI Iteration get no category, so no line); `StoryCreationService` passes `learning` for a learn-something story; the eval's `adopted` setup passes the flag where its input carries one. The turn: `moneyTurns.ts` (`MONEY_TURN_TEXT`, `takesMoneyRule`, moved from the eval module), printed by `BeatPromptService` at the end of the stat changes and, on a switch turn and the ending, after the adjustments line. The variants build on production with the lines taken out (`moneySetupRequest` on production without the flag; `moneyTurnBase` and `productionTurnMeasured` through `withoutMoneyTurnLines`), one copy of each text. Production's setup on a learning story is `moneySetup` byte for byte, and its turn there `moneyTurnB`, prompt and JSON schema, every turn kind and player count.
- **Kept tests, changed on purpose:** `adoptedSetup.test.ts` (new: a learning story's setup is `moneySetup`, the line from production's constant), `StoryCreationService.setupStatus.test.ts` (new: a learn-something story's setup gets `learning`), `adoptedTurns.test.ts` (new: every learning turn kind and player count is `moneyTurnB`, its base the same turn without the category; `expectSinglePlayer` and `expectGroup` route a learning turn that counts there, the frozen cases too), `adoptedForms.test.ts` (a frozen learning turn that counts reads against `moneyTurnB`, its base against the case without the category), `adoptedDeltas.ts` (`beforeMoneyTurn` in `productionThen` and `productionBeforeKidsAges`), `moneyAddsUp.test.ts` (`moneyTurn` against `moneyTurnBase`), `moneySetup.test.ts` and `playthroughs.test.ts` (production's setup on a learning input is the money setup now), `money2Cases.test.ts` (the fake run rebuilds with `productionSends`; the stored runs with `money2Sent`, production's turn without the lines), `armsBudget.test.ts` (the stage's checked retry). Test-first: the adoption tests failed or could not load before the production change (8 suites, 3 assertions beside the load failures); after it all passed.
- **Unmeasured, logged:** a group's turn carries the block too (every player count, as the variant was built; measured on single players); the line on a learning premise about neither money nor counts (the control: nothing forced in, 2 of 2 an arm); the setup line's levers on counts of animals; the two together in a whole story (no confirming round played).
- **A paid run of production's own setup or turn on a learning story** takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted26`'s production records are before the adoption).
- **Spend:** $0.3623 of the stage's $0.39 (setups and play $0.198, the turn line and its retest $0.165), above the brief's $0.25-0.30 and inside the cap: the turn line ran production beside it on all eight cases twice, where the cap's reason priced it at about $0.07. The ledger about $44.68 of $48.

- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (money and counts in a learning story), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (the kept tests, both variants, the stage's modes, cap, spend, arms and readings; fix 7's entry points at it). `Privacy.tsx`: nothing to change (no new tool or practice). No setting and no log line changed.

## 2026-10-02: decision A, no game words in the story text, measured and adopted (stage `result-words`, $0.1666 of $0.26)

- **The brief:** no game words in the story text such as "the mixed result" (six group turns in round 3; the game already logs them): find the cause (which request lines put result kinds where the text model reads them as narration), fix there with a turn line or by changing what is shown, measure on built group cases twice beside production (stage `result-words`, about $0.10).
- **The cause** (a temporary Jest probe over `playthroughs.json`, `-2.json` and `-3.json`, replayed with `replayRun`, each turn's sent request read from `prompts/` through `prep-calls.jsonl`; deleted; no calls):
  - Production's note finds a result's kind in 9 of about 500 group player texts and none of 133 single-player texts: 6 of 229 on a group's later chapter steps, 3 of 123 on group switches after a chapter, none of 123 on a chapter's opening step (which narrates a choice, no rolled result), none of 19 endings (which carry "the text never mentions milestones, outcomes or resolutions" since 2026-09-30). Round 1 none; round 2's space pirates 8 and 18; round 3's food trucks 14 and space pirates 3, 13, 14 and 16.
  - Every one renders the previous result's label and its description as one sentence. The request shows each result to narrate under its label: "RESOLUTION: MIXED. <description>" right after the player's last beat text, "(PREVIOUS BEAT, resolution was determined and must now be narrated) ... Resolution: MIXED." in the beat progression, "Thread Resolution: MIXED" and "(Result: Mixed)" at a switch; the text rules ask the turn to set its tone by whether the previous beat "was favorable / mixed / unfavorable"; the fourth-wall rule named only 'NPC', 'player character', 'stat' and 'story beat'. Round 3's space pirates' turn 16 wrote "The mixed result remains plain in the room: your dates can be checked, but ..." from "RESOLUTION: MIXED. The schedule is visible, but the crew finds gaps ...", and "The unfavorable outcome hangs between you: they have examined ..." from "RESOLUTION: UNFAVORABLE. The contact sees the entries but cannot tell ..."; turn 13's "The favorable attention your procedure earned" translated Side B's wins for Oren, on side B.
  - Not the cause: the story's own summaries and facts that earlier turns wrote with result words ("After the mixed assessment ...") don't predict it (flagged turns 2 of 81 where they hold such words, 5 of 124 where they don't). Luna low writes group turns, Luna medium a single player's, the same lines in both.
- **The variant** (`storyTextRounds/resultWords.ts`, `resultWords`, eval only, test-first; production's request byte for byte elsewhere): one line under the fourth-wall rule, "Tell each result in the story's own words: the text, the options and the interludes never name a result's kind (favorable, mixed, unfavorable, Side A or Side B).", on a group turn that narrates a result (a later chapter step, a switch after the story's first beat). **My calls:** a turn line, not a change to what is shown (the labels say which result to narrate, and the thread configuration is also the switch planner's); the ending's own wording ("in the story's own words"); groups only (singles never leaked; a single player's turn stays as measured).
- **The cases** (`resultWordsCases.ts`, `--build-result-words-cases`, no calls): all seven flagged turns, each request the one its round sent (`casesByRound`, factored out of `contestSettledCases`, with `SENT_BY_ROUND`): five later chapter steps and two switches, six three-player and one two-player.
- **The check:** production's note (`resultWordsOfBeat`, deterministic) and a blind hand reading of every sentence holding a word that can name a kind (`--result-words-blind`, verdicts in `resultWordsHand.ts` by code before the key was opened; `--result-words` unblinds). No judge.
- **The run** (02:31-02:41 UTC, under `adopted27`, interleaved, production's checked retry in the loop): requests read end to end before paying (the space pirates' switch at 14 whole, 725 lines; every case's request against production's: the one line, nothing else); dry run 28 jobs, est $0.12; a 2-job smoke (the space pirates' 16, $0.0117), then 26; 28 as counted, every reply valid.
- **Readings (`result-words.md`), 14 turns an arm:**
  - **By production's note:** turns naming a kind 4 of 14 → 0 of 14, **moved lower (p 0.049; noise 0)**; player texts 4 of 40 → 0 of 40, moved lower (p 0.058).
  - **By hand, blind** (9 sentences; 8 told a label, "each side a place" matched by its words only): turns 6 of 14 → 1 of 14, **moved lower (p 0.038; noise 29 pts)**; player texts 7 of 40 → 1 of 40, within the noise (15 pts). Production's: "The mixed result is plain in the ship's silence", "The favorable hearing from the last exchange", "a mixed hearing", "the unfavorable timing remains", "the crew's mixed ruling", "the mixed response hangs in the chart-table light", "Your favorable live reference". The variant's one: "the mixed approach leaves the crew with a worse leak" (round 2's switch, sample 2).
  - Reasoning tokens 220 → 240 (not moved). **First replies one short paragraph 0 → 2 of 14** (beyond the noise, not moved, p about 0.24): both the space pirates' switch at 14, every player's text one paragraph, both rescued by production's retry; so that switch's wait with the retry, p95 40.9 → 70.7 s (the first reply alone 38.3 s), and cost $0.0055 → $0.0064 a turn with retries ($0.0056 the first reply). Chapter steps' waits level. Automatic checks: `note:resultWordsInText` 0.29 → 0 a turn (moved lower), `milestoneNotCopied` 0% → 75% (moved higher: the switch turns' milestones in their own words), `noMetaWords` 79% → 100% (beyond the noise, not moved).
- **No fix-and-retest:** the target moved on turns by both readings and nothing moved the wrong way under the stop rule. The short first replies are the one reading to watch: the short-replies stage saw a sticky state go short twice before; here the retry answered both times.
- **Verdict: adopted, `resultWords`.**
- **Production (test-first):** `game/services/resultLabels.ts` (`RESULT_LABELS_TEXT`, `takesResultLabels`), printed by `BeatPromptService` right after the fourth-wall rule's terms; production's turn is `resultWords` byte for byte, prompt and JSON schema, every turn kind and player count (no schema change). The variant builds on production with the line taken out (`resultWordsBase`, `withoutResultWordsLine`; one copy of the text and condition, production's). The beat repairs' note (`resultWordsInText`) stays, its comment updated. Test-first: the adoption test in `storyTextRounds/resultWords.test.ts` could not load before `resultLabels.ts` existed; after the change 34 tests in 13 suites failed and were changed on purpose (below).
- **Kept tests changed on purpose:** `adoptedDeltas.ts` gains `beforeResultWords` (in `productionThen` and `productionBeforeKidsAges`) and `withResultWords` (the line on forms measured before; a form built on production's live turn carries it already and stays as it is); `adoptedTurns.test.ts` (`asAdopted`) and `adoptedForms.test.ts` (the default turn expectation) read with it; `playthroughs3Sent` rebuilds round 3's turns without it; `choiceResultCases.test.ts`, `groupLeversCases.test.ts`, `groupOptionsCases.test.ts` and `playthroughReplay.test.ts` read production as it stood before with `beforeResultWords`; `resultWordsCases.test.ts` (production today is the variant, the stage's production arm its base). The other suites that failed (`choiceResult`, `kidsTurn`, `moneyAddsUp`, `parallelThreads`, `recordedResult`, `sharedScenesCases`) passed again through `productionThen` and `playthroughs3Sent` alone, unchanged.
- **Test-first, as it ran:** the variant's suite failed to load before its module; the case module was written before its suite (the suite then passed on the stored runs); the report's suite failed to load before `resultWordsPrep.ts`; `armsBudget.test.ts` was updated together with `arms.ts` and `budget.ts`, not run in between.
- **Unmeasured, logged:** a read-with-kids group's turn that narrates a result carries the line too (`kidsAges` is built on production's live turn); a learning group's turn likewise; the line in whole stories (no confirming round played).
- **A paid run of production's own group turn** on a later chapter step or a switch takes a new prompt-state tag (`adopted27`'s production records are before the adoption).
- **Spend:** $0.1666 of the stage's $0.26 (the smoke $0.0117; two retries), above the brief's $0.10, which priced two-player turns, and inside the cap. The ledger about $44.84 of $48 as the dry run prints it.
- **Checks:** `npm run check:all` clean after the adoption (core build; server 4,693 tests; client 276, untouched). No core edits. No browser check: nothing on screen changed.
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (the result-words bullet: the line, the cause, the measurement), `.context/text-model-eval.md` (production's kept forms, the variant, the stage's modes, cap, spend, arms and readings; the round-3 findings' entry points at it). `Privacy.tsx`: nothing to change (no new tool or practice). No setting and no log line changed.

## 2026-10-02: the review of the contest-settled adoption, a challenge the planner wrote on a contested outcome stores its camp (no calls, $0)

- **The finding (verified):** contest-settled's line has the chapter planner write a one-sided deciding thread as that side's challenge itself (`CONTEST_DECIDED.oneSided`), so PL-12 converts nothing there, and fix 2 stored the scoreboard side (`favorableSide`) only inside `oneSidedAsChallenge`, which converts a thread only where every result in it is a contest result. A planner-written challenge carried no side, `boardResultOf` read it as no contest, and the scoreboard repair read no winner on the chapter that decides the contest: a wrong-way move (round 3's space pirates' 50 -> 65 kind) would stand there. The stage's report showed it (`repair:contestOneSided` 0.38 -> 0), and my contest-settled entry's 'Unmeasured' bullet missed it ("as the plan check already did": the check also stored the side).
- **The fix (test-first, no calls):** `withChallengeCamp` in `planChecks.ts`, called by `checkThread` on a thread whose results are all a challenge's: in a group that plays contests, a challenge on a contested shared outcome stores its players' camp as `favorableSide`, where every player in it has a known camp (`campOf`) and all share one; a thread that already has a side (PL-12's conversion) keeps it. **My call:** stricter than `boardSideOf` (which the finding proposed): `boardSideOf` returns side A wherever player1 is among the players, which is right for a converted contest (the planner wrote them on one side) but would read a challenge holding player1 and player2 of a two-player game, both camps, as side A's; nothing in a challenge says its players are one side, so player1 beside a seat of unknown camp stores none.
- **Checked on the stored stories** (`playthroughReplay.test.ts`, every stored chapter plan checked again from the reply its run used, camps kept): two planner-written challenges store a side now, round 2's estate agents' turn 13 (Nia's sale chapter) and round 3's food trucks' turn 17 (Omar's contract crossing), both side B, each stored thread otherwise unchanged; replayed with the side stored, the repair reads a winner on five later turns where it read none (the estate agents' 14, Rory's side; the food trucks' 18-20, Omar's, and 21, Amara's), and no move changes (each already went the winner's way or left the board alone).
- **Checked on the stage's stored plans:** `contestPlanRows` now reads the stored side (`ContestPlanRow.favorableSide`, shown in the report's per-case Kind column); `--contest-settled` written again (no calls): all 16 one-sided plans of the stage, both arms (round 1's food trucks 6, round 2's estate agents 13 and space pirates 10, round 3's food trucks 17), read "challenge (sideB)", the lone player's camp; before the fix the variant's eight and production's two planner-written ones carried none.
- **Test-first:** two of three new `planChecks.test.ts` cases failed first (the third, where nothing is stored, passed as it should); the replay test was a reading of stored data, its expectations taken from it and read; the report's side was written before its test.
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (the scoreboard bullet, the contest-settled paragraph), the comments in `scoreboards.ts`, `playthroughs.ts` (`ContestResult.converted` now also marks a planner-written challenge read by its side) and core's `Thread.favorableSide` (core rebuilt). `Privacy.tsx`: nothing to change. ## 2026-10-02: the review of the money-2 adoption, the double-charge repair reads a counted stat's payment later in the chapter only where beats can't change it (no calls, $0)

- **The finding (verified):** on a learning story the adopted money turn line (`MONEY_TURN_TEXT.amountsNamed`) has every beat move a counted (number) stat by exactly what its text pays, sells or uses up, and the money setup line makes levers small round amounts in the stat's own units, the very amounts routine moves take (the stage's lemonade setups: "Use 1 crate for an extra batch", "Gain 2 coins", "Use 2 lemons"). The double-charge repair (`dropLeversChargedAgain`) dropped the first change of the lever's size on its stat on the turn after the payment and, since the review of the third playthroughs, on every later step of the chapter (`readLaterInChapter` true for every number stat), once a step: after "Use 1 crate for an extra batch", each later batch's "one crate used" (-1, as `moneyTurnB` writes it) would go as the sacrifice charged again, the count one too high a step while the money block forbids the text any total but the stat's. The repair's own docs accepted same-size drops because the stored playthroughs showed none, which predate counted stats that move every beat; my money-2 entry's 'Unmeasured' list missed the interaction. Its verifier: no false drop happened in the stage's runs (the one drop, s2 turn 4's +2 Stand Cash, was a real repeat: the reply's reasoning says "The previous reward adds 2 coins"), but the turn-5 cases' replies moved Lemons in Stock -2 a turn after the -2 sacrifice and escaped only because the lever reader missed the stat.
- **The fix (test-first, no calls):** `readLaterInChapter(story, stats, paid)`: on a story that takes the money rule (`takesMoneyRule`), a number stat's payment is read on a later step of the chapter only where the stat's rules keep a beat from changing it (`canBeChangedInBeatResolutions` false), as a list's and a ladder's have been since the review of decision A's fixes: its step is one every story move on such a stat shares. **My call:** the finding's first option (the second, a repeat dropped only where no other change on the stat could account for it, can't be read without the text); the turn right after the payment keeps its read, where the repair's stored repeats came and where the money-2 stage's one drop was a real repeat (a same-size sale that turn still goes, logged in `.context/story.md`); percentages on a money story read as before (the rule moves counted number stats).
- **Checked on the stored stories:** `playthroughReplay.test.ts` unchanged (no stored drop was a later-step number drop on a learning story); the money-2 stage's played runs hold one drop, on the turn after its payment (`money-2.json`), unchanged.
- **Test-first:** two new `beatRepairs.test.ts` cases (a routine crate on a later step of a learning story's chapter kept; dropped where the stat's rules keep beats from changing it, on the turn right after the payment, and on a later step of a story of another category); the first failed before the change (the second's assertions held already, once its fixture named another category).
- **Docs:** `.context/story.md` (the lever bullet), the comments in `leverPayments.ts`. `Privacy.tsx`: nothing to change. No setting and no log line changed.
